# metering — Business Rules

Format: BR-XXX-### · rule · source (spec/decision §) · status (CONFIRMED / PLACEHOLDER / OPEN) · test ref.
Rules CITE B-docs; they never copy values out of them (one source of truth).

| BR-MET-024 | Override appends a superseding read row (reason required); original immutable with version chain; compliance block display-only; **Dispute records a marker + reason on the read (feeds the S25 "disputes" count); v1 flow per the OQ-043 closure: record + flag, and the account is HELD from the bill run until a System Admin clears it.** ⚠ BUILT 2026-08-16 and the rule is unchanged — what CHANGED is that it is now reachable: `services.dispute_read` / `clear_read_dispute`, `POST /reads/{id}/dispute` and `/dispute/clear`, `selectors.dispute_holds_for_unit` which `apps/billing/runs.prepare_run` blocks on, and the S29 footer control that had rendered DISABLED since 2026-08-11 because no endpoint set a marker (DEF-046 — the columns, the counter and the chip all shipped and nothing wrote one). Clearing is System Admin ALONE, because the closure names that role and clearing is what releases the account. ⚠ **THE RULE IS ENTIRELY FORWARD-LOOKING AND OQ-245 ASKS THE REST:** it says what the NEXT run does and nothing about the invoice a disputed read ALREADY priced — which is the ordinary case. No late-fee pause, no dunning hold, no automatic J6 correction and no upheld/rejected verdict exists; each is refused by absence and named on every surface. | S29 ("v1 · immutable") · IS-6 · OQ-043 closure (PO R6) · OQ-245 (OPEN — the already-issued bill) | CONFIRMED (PO closure batch, 2026-08-08); BUILT 2026-08-16 | US-MET-013/TC-2,3,5,6 · tests/test_us_met_013_tc_dispute.py · tests/test_us_bil_001_prepare.py |

| BR-MET-026 | Estimation math: trailing-3-cycle daily average (actuals only) × days in period, NUMERIC(18,6); no history → no estimate | OQ-003 (B4 addendum proposal) · A2 ("trailing-3" EXPLICIT, math UNKNOWN) | CONFIRMED (PO R6, 2026-08-08 — OQ-003 closure) | US-MET-014/TC-1,5,6 · GT-9 |
| BR-MET-026a | **AMENDED 2026-09-07 (`CR-079`) — the METHOD is the tenant's choice of SEVEN; the ARITHMETIC above is unchanged and its window is the only variable.** PO verbatim: *"this should be drop down options - give 4 to 5 options here"*, then *"we are good with these + add last 6 month avg + Use avg from plan"*. BR-MET-026 named exactly one method and `est_method` accepted exactly one value; it now offers **trailing 3 (DEFAULT, UNMOVED) · trailing 6 · trailing 12 · the same period last year · repeat the last actual · route peer average · plan average**. ⚠ **THE FORMULA DID NOT MOVE:** every runnable method is Σ consumption ÷ Σ days × days in period over a different SELECTION of the same actual reads, so GT-9 ❄️ reproduces byte-for-byte (612 + 618 + 624 over 90 days → 20.600000/day → 618.000000). ⚠ **NOTHING MIGRATES A STORED ROW** — changing how a missing reading is estimated changes what a customer is billed, so it is a choice somebody makes. ⚠ **TWO OF THE SEVEN HAVE NO INPUT AND DO NOT RUN:** `route_peer_average` (`OQ-MET-1761`) and `plan_average` (`OQ-MET-1762`) are offered on screen and refused in three places — a disabled `<option>` with its reason, the form, and the server — and `estimate()` answers `estimation_method_not_implemented` rather than falling through to a different rule. ⚠ **`same_period_last_year` READS *last year* AS TWELVE PERIODS BACK** and is right on a monthly cycle only; `OQ-MET-1763` puts the bi-monthly case to the PO. | `CR-079` (PO 2026-09-07) | **CONFIRMED-AS-AMENDED (PO 2026-09-07)** | `tests/test_cr079_estimation_methods.py` · GT-9 (unmoved) |

| BR-MET-033 | **Routes are built by selecting Premises and their Units** (membership), with an OPTIONAL walk order — no numeric capacity ranges. Supersedes any sequence-range framing in EP-MET-02 (reconcile at review). ⚠ **WHERE THE WALK ORDER LIVES IS SETTLED — PO ruling 2026-09-22, verbatim *"a is okay"*, closing `OQ-MET-12572`: IT STAYS **PER PREMISES**, in `route_premises_orders`.** That table is built and live — two uniqueness rules (one stop one position, one position one stop), `walk_order.save_premises_order` as its audited writer behind a `SYSTEM_ADMIN` guard, `walk_order.premises_order_for_route` as its reader, drag-to-reorder on `RouteDetailPage.tsx`, and the printed route sheet’s stops read from it. It implements the PO ruling of 2026-08-23 (`CR-029` D11), verbatim *"Per premises"*, whose reasoning was *"a reader walks to an ADDRESS and reads everything there"*. ⚠ **THE PO's OWN SENTENCE OF 2026-09-22 MORNING IS SUPERSEDED BY NAME, NOT QUIETLY DROPPED** — *"i will update the walk order on units directly or asset … not keep them in routes"* — because it was reasoning about this rule’s own column, `route_memberships.walk_order`, which is SUPERSEDED, has **no writer** and holds **0 rows** (B17 §5 gap G5). ⚠ **AND BOTH RULINGS AGREE ONCE THE GRAIN IS RIGHT: a per-property order is 6 rows attached to the 6 premises `CR-131-BRC07` stores — declared, not derived — so it cannot go stale**, which is exactly what the September ruling required. **`CR-131-BRC07` steps 2 and 8 are CANCELLED: no column is added to `units`, nothing is migrated, the reorder screen is untouched.** Decision log 2026-09-22, *"THE THREE ANSWERS THAT RE-OPEN `CR-131-BRC07`"* | PO R7 (OQ-041) | CONFIRMED | — |

| BR-MET-040 | Unit may exist without a plan; plan REQUIRED at account activation (move-in blocks with message). ⚠ **APPLICATION POINT CORRECTED 2026-08-29 (`CR-047`) — THE RULE IS UNCHANGED AND THE CODE NOW AGREES WITH IT.** `_require_plan_on_unit` sat inside `_make_occupying`, so it fired when an imported row was WRITTEN rather than when an account was ACTIVATED — and because stage 4 was one transaction, one unit without a plan aborted a whole migration (`DEF-2519`). **This row's own words already said *activation*;** creation is not activation, so moving the check is a correction of where it is applied, **not an amendment of the rule, and no re-ratification is needed.** ⚠ **WHY THE DIRECTION IS RIGHT AS WELL AS LAWFUL:** a real cutover loads accounts on day one and assigns plans on day two, and the plan is held BY REFERENCE (`BR-CUS-029`) — so the day it appears on the unit, every account under it has it, with nothing to backfill. An account still cannot BILL without one: the bill run refuses a unit whose plan cannot be resolved, which is where the rule's teeth actually belong. ⚠⚠ **NARROWED 2026-09-21 AND NOT REVERSED — IT STOPS HOLDING FOR A COMMON AREA AND GOES ON HOLDING FOR A DWELLING.** **PO, verbatim:** *"common area dont need rate plan as of now; make this optional"*. **What it cost while it held:** `create_master_account` creates the owner's account ACTIVE and ran this check over every unit it covers, so a hallway with no plan refused the WHOLE build — measured as **eight New Connect builds raised in a browser and eight refused at the final press**, with *"This unit has no rate plan yet."* on every one that had a common area. ⚠ **THE PO'S WORDS *"AS OF NOW"* ARE THEIRS AND ARE KEPT, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A CONDITION AND NOT A DELETION.** `customers/services.py`'s `COMMON_AREA_REQUIRES_A_PLAN = False` is the whole narrowing: **one named constant, in one place, set back to `True` to restore the refusal.** A lane may not express this as a removed check. ⛔ **IT NARROWS NOTHING ELSE.** Every other path is untouched — `activate_account`, `update_account`'s status change into a billing class, S18 `create_account` and S19 `move_in` with an occupying status — and the only unit the exemption can reach is one carrying `units.is_common_area = True` (`BR-MET-101`). ⚠ **WHAT MAKES IT SAFE IS VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, AND IS NOW HELD BY A TEST** (the same guarantee `require_plan=False` has rested on since `CR-047`): `billing.gather.gather_account` raises `GATHER_NO_PLAN` for ANY unit with no `rate_plan_id`, `runs.STAGE_BY_REASON` files it on the `resolve-subscription` tab and `runs.NOT_RETRYABLE_HERE` marks it not-retryable. **An unplanned unit can EXIST and can never be BILLED.** If that ever stops being true this ruling needs re-putting, which is why `test_br_met_040_common_area.py` holds it rather than leaving it as a sentence. | PO PU-10 · application point `CR-047` 2026-08-29 · **narrowed PO 2026-09-21** · BR-CUS-672 · BR-CUS-029 · BR-MET-101 | **CONFIRMED-AS-NARROWED 2026-09-21** (application point corrected 2026-08-29) | `06-backend/tests/test_cr047_import_inheritance.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_040_common_area.py` |
| BR-MET-041 | Bulk plan assignment: multi-select units + plan + effective date; per-unit history rows + audit batch | PO PU-11 | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-MET-042 | Direct unit-level plan edit is ALLOWED even when occupied — as an error-correction override with warning + audit ("allow the override to handle human or user errors"); normal changes go through the account Change-plan flow | PO PU-12 | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-MET-043 | Asset tagging from unit side: pick from inventory or create new (BR-AST-028); untag = assets reassign flow with final read | PO PU-13 | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-MET-044 | After move-out the unit KEEPS its last plan as default for next move-in (prefill, changeable) — no silent revert | PO PU-15 | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-MET-045 | Premises detail (Units/Assets/Accounts/Audit tabs) and Unit detail (Overview/Assets/Account history/Plan history/Audit) per PU-16/17; list capability row per PU-18. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-22 (`DEF-578` · `DEF-580`) — THE TAB SETS ARE r10's, AND THE `Accounts` TAB ON A PREMISES IS REMOVED.** PO verbatim, reporting it with five screenshots of r10 PRM-02: _"premises details view does not match with this - look at button positions - look at tabs and details within tabs. lok at header and informationd etails in it ; does not match - all should be done using tailwind - why it doe snot match"_. **PRM-02 is now `Overview · Units · Common areas · Assets · Audit log` (FIVE)** and **PRM-03 is `Overview · Assets · Plan · Account · Occupancy · Audit log` (SIX)** — r10's own strips, read out of `05-prototype/r10-2026-08-16/SMARTBillHub.dc.html`. This rule's tab sets came from **PU-16/17, a PO walkthrough of the PREVIOUS design**, and CLAUDE.md's standing ruling (2026-08-16) is that r10 is THE reference and a contradicting screen description is stale. ⚠ **NOTHING IS HIDDEN BY THE REMOVAL, and that is why it is a conformance fix and not a scope cut:** the `Accounts` tab never listed a premise's accounts — it rendered a stated absence saying `GET /accounts` declares `unit_id` and no `premises_id` (`DEF-473`), so a premise's accounts are reached **through its units**, and that route is untouched (Units tab → unit detail → **Account** tab, which reads `GET /accounts?f.unit_id=`). The word `Audit` becomes `Audit log` on both screens, r10's label. ⚠ **AND `BR-AUD-010` WAS NEVER AMENDED AND WAS NEVER OBEYED (`DEF-579`)**: it already required a per-entity Audit tab to RENDER the entity's rows *and* link to S61, and the shipped tab only linked. | PO PU-16/17/18 · **PO ruling 2026-08-22 (DEF-578/579/580)** · r10 PRM-02/PRM-03 · CLAUDE.md 2026-08-16 | **CONFIRMED · AMENDED 2026-08-22** | `PremisesPage.test.tsx` "DEF-578: the tab strip is r10's FIVE" · `UnitsPage.test.tsx` |

Rejection of the same key twice inside one file stands.** | PO RD-5..8 · `BR-MET-1189` | CONFIRMED · **SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-10-04** | — |
| BR-MET-053 | **Read lifecycle**: staged → ok/flagged/estimated → committed → BILLED(locked) · disputed→cleared · corrections = SUPERSEDE (new row, original kept visible), never edit; billed-read error = supersede + automatic J6 correction path — read fix and money fix travel together | PO RD-9/10 | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-MET-054 | CRUD: single manual entry + bulk upload (any cadence, batches append per cycle); update=supersede only; delete=staged/rejected only; roles per OQ-042/043 closures | PO RD-11 | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-MET-055 | Scale: server pagination, hot-window default (2yr) + explicit archive search, monthly partitions, async imports, no unbounded read endpoints; **archival to cold storage runs as an AUTOMATIC scheduled job** (2yr hot → archive, 7yr total) — never manual | PO RD-13 + addition | CONFIRMED | — | <br>⚠ **NOT HONOURED AS BUILT, 2026-08-15 (S28, DEF-115):** the `async imports` clause is CONFIRMED law and the read-import commit runs **SYNCHRONOUSLY** — `services.commit_read_batch` executes inside the request because this deployment registers no BE-07 job store, and answers `async: false`. The atomic all-or-nothing semantics are identical either way; what is missing is the background execution and its progress. **S28 states the wait rather than drawing a progress bar over a blocking call**, and this line is the record so the divergence is not rediscovered as a surprise. Closing it is a job-store story, not a screen change. ⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-30 — THE READ POST IS ASYNC ABOVE A MEASURED BAR (`DEF-MET-8106`, PO ruling of that date, decision log *"READS UPLOAD 500 ON 480 READINGS"*).** The paragraph above was already false in its premise — the BE-07 job store has been registered since 2026-08-10 — and it cost exactly what it predicted: 480 readings took ~42-60 s inside the request, the web worker was killed at 60 s AFTER the readings were saved, and the clerk saw *Internal Server Error*. A batch of more than `services.READ_POST_ASYNC_ROW_BAR` staged rows now posts in a worker: `POST /read-batches/{id}/post` answers **202** with the job, `GET /jobs/{id}` serves `N of M` readings (written on a second connection so it moves while the post's one transaction is open), and `GET /read-batches/{id}/post-receipt` serves the receipt kept on the batch (`post_receipt`, metering 0064). At or below the bar nothing changes. The post is still ONE transaction and a second post is still refused (hard rule 6). The monthly-usage rebuild after every post is its own resumable job (`DEF-MET-8108`) — `BR-MET-141` clause 6 (*nothing bills off it*) is what makes a moment's lag lawful. <br>⚠ **THE ARCHIVAL HALF NOW RUNS ONCE PER UTILITY (PO ruling 2026-09-07, `OQ-AUD-1724`).** `meter_reads` and `audit_log` are archived by ONE mechanism (`core/archival.py`, NFR-28 *"matches reads pattern"*), so a ruling made on the audit side governs this rule's job too: both tables carry FORCE row-level security and no identity can see a whole partition, so the run enters each utility's context and counts inside it. The manifest's `row_count` is the sum over utilities and its `sha256` is a FOLD of the per-utility digests — **a digest recorded before 2026-09-07 is not comparable with one recorded after**. `BR-AUD-012` in `04-docs/05-modules/11-audit/02-business-rules.md` carries the ruling verbatim.

Generalizes the A-6 read_type decision; flat meter = single `total` channel; new register kinds = new channel values, never schema changes | PO RD-1 (Option B) | CONFIRMED | — |

This row existing is the fix; `CR-096-BRC02` is why.
| BR-MET-061 | TOU sum-sanity check (Σwindows vs total ±1%, BR-MET-052) severity = **WARNING** — postable only via explicit accept + reason (BR-MET-015 disposition), never a silent pass | PO OQ-066, 2026-08-08 | CONFIRMED | US-MET-008/017 TCs hold as written |
| BR-MET-062 | Missing `demand`-channel value is NEVER auto-estimated — always forces a field visit (a peak drives kW dollars; an estimated peak is a dispute) | PO OQ-067, 2026-08-08 | CONFIRMED | — |
| BR-MET-063 | **Billing periods are GENERATED from a repeat rule, and a gap or overlap is REFUSED (PO ruling 2026-08-10, OQ-094).** A cycle carries a recurrence (frequency + anchor); each generated window starts the day after the previous one ended, so gaps and overlaps are impossible by construction. Any period length is valid — 1 · 7 · 15 · 30 · 60 · 90 · 180 · 365 days are each just a window. **The guard is built REGARDLESS of how a period is created:** a hand-created or edited period that overlaps its predecessor, or leaves a day uncovered, is refused. An uncovered day is silent lost revenue (meters turned, nobody is ever billed); a double-covered day is a double charge, a refund and a complaint | Product Owner 2026-08-10 | **CONFIRMED** | to author |
| BR-MET-064 | **First-bill estimation when the customer has NO history (PO ruling 2026-08-11).** The confirmed estimation rule is a trailing-3-cycle daily average, which a brand-new account does not have. For a first bill the basis is **the average for that customer CATEGORY** (the platform categories: residential · commercial · industrial · government), and the plan's **minimum bill** is the floor — if the estimated usage plus base falls below it, the existing minimum-bill adjustment applies (B4 §minimum bill, OQ-007 closure). PO's words: *"in first bill customer charge avg for that category, you will have min base rate in plan."* **NEW DATA REQUIREMENT, named rather than assumed:** no category average exists anywhere in the corpus today. Where it comes from is NOT ruled — see the open question — and until it is, a first bill with no history falls back to the plan minimum, which IS already confirmed law and needs no new data. | Product Owner 2026-08-11 | **CONFIRMED** (basis) / the source of the average is OPEN | to author |
| BR-MET-065 | **Backdating a plan change behind an issued bill is ALLOWED with a reason recorded (PO ruling 2026-08-11).** A unit plan assignment whose effective date falls on or before the last day covered by an ISSUED bill for any account on that unit is PERMITTED, but REQUIRES a reason; the reason is stored on the assignment history row (BR-CUS-011) and an `audit_log` row naming the bill is written. Staff are warned, never blocked. Shown to the PO: *"a unit's plan is changed effective 1 June; June's bill went out on the 5th at the old rate; the records now disagree with the bill"*, with options block / warn-and-allow / allow-silently — ruled **warn-and-allow**. **Contrast BR-CUS-030 + US-CUS-005 TC-6, where a move-out behind an issued bill is REFUSED outright: the same question, a different verdict, both from the PO.** The difference is what the act contradicts — a move-out backdated behind a bill claims the customer did not hold the service on days already billed, while a plan change behind a bill leaves the days intact and only makes the current record disagree with the document sent. The first is a false statement about the past; the second is a true statement about the present that a reader must be told about | PO ruling 2026-08-11 (item 7) · BR-BIL-004 (the rule it protects) · CLAUDE.md rule 7 (the audit reading is stated in the code, not assumed) | CONFIRMED (2026-08-11) | US-MET-003 TC-8/9/10/11/13 · `tests/test_us_met_003_plan_backdating.py` |

## CR-004 — five r10 SET-04 reference lists metering OWNS and tenancy HOSTS (2026-08-16)

Added by the CR-004 write-back lane, documenting the vocabulary landed in commit
`4b02f48`. **Hosting is not owning (BR-TEN-031):** the five tables live in
`apps/tenancy` because `apps/metering` and `apps/assets` author no lookup CRUD,
but the CONTENT and its validation are metering's, the card names metering as the
owner, and when a metering lane lands it re-homes the table in one migration.

**None of the five mints a story.** All are ordinary lists on the one
reference-data surface, so CRUD is **US-TEN-009 / US-TEN-010 / US-TEN-011,
inherited unchanged** (CR-004: *"A new list inherits all three and does not need
its own CRUD story"*), and **BR-TEN-030** (code unique per tenant,
case-insensitive, immutable once used), **BR-TEN-034** (audit-lock: deactivate
allowed, delete blocked once used) and **BR-TEN-036** (seeded defaults, tenant may
extend) apply to every one without restatement.

**Seed source for every row below:** `06-backend/apps/tenancy/fixtures/lookup_defaults.json`
(the `_cr004_comment` block), delivered at TENANT CREATION through
`SEED_LIST_OWNERS` + each module's `seed_reference_list` — never by a later
script (CR-004 acceptance criterion 2). **Design source:** prototype
`05-prototype/r10-2026-08-16/SMARTBillHub.dc.html`, screen SET-04, which is THE
reference by PO ruling 2026-08-16.

⚠ **READ THE DIVERGENCE NOTE UNDER THE TABLE BEFORE CITING ANY ROW AS "VERBATIM
FROM r10".** The seeded vocabulary and r10's own `LISTS` object do not agree, and
this section records what the product actually seeds.

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-066 | **`areas` — the districts and routes premises are grouped by (S75).** Seeded vocabulary, four entries, exactly as a new tenant receives them: **`NORTH` "North district" · `EAST` "East district" · `SOUTH` "South district" · `CENTRAL` "Central district"**. **SAMPLE content, not structural** — the sample-data toggle may suppress it (BR-TEN-067), because no row in any table requires an area to exist. **NO parent/child column, and the shape is UNSPECIFIED rather than missing:** r10's card is titled *"Areas & sub-areas"* and its rows read *"North district · 3 sub-areas"*, so a hierarchy is clearly intended, but nothing in the corpus defines it — depth, whether a sub-area may hold premises directly, or what a route's relationship to it is. A `parent_id` added here would be an invented model. ⚠ **DIVERGES FROM r10 — see the note below** | prototype r10 `SET-04.areas` · seed `06-backend/apps/tenancy/fixtures/lookup_defaults.json` → `areas` · hosted `apps.tenancy.models.Area` (`areas`) · BR-TEN-031 (content owner = metering) | **CONFIRMED** for the list, its ownership and the four seeded codes as shipped; the sub-area hierarchy is **UNSPECIFIED — no rule, no column, not a gap to be filled by a lane** | inherits US-TEN-009/010/011 TCs · `06-backend/tests/test_reference_data_completeness.py` |
| BR-MET-067 | **`meter_size_classes` — the meter fleet's size/service classes (S76).** ⚠⚠ **RENAMED AND RE-RATIFIED 2026-08-27 — SUPERSEDED IN PART BY BR-TEN-170 + BR-TEN-171 (CR-039 wave E tasks 20–21).** The list is now `service_size_classes`, the **UTILITY SERVICE** size class — PO verbatim: *"it is utility service size class"* — because four of the five services the ratified bands add have NO METER (storm water is sized by the property, broadband by the tier bought, solar by inverter capacity, battery by its rating). **The seeded set is FORTY, not seven**: water 9 · electric 6 · gas 4 · trash 7 · storm water 4 · solar 4 · broadband 3 · thermal chilled water 3 · **sewer NONE by ruling** (a share of the water usage charge, BR-RAT-015, reading no register). ⚠ **Thermal STEAM and BATTERY seed nothing and are NOT ruled** — they were put to the PO as a dimension with no boundaries, so *"rest all are correct in this"* ratified the dimension (OQ-560, OQ-561). The codes THIS ROW lists were already superseded before today (CR-010 took the list 7 → 9 and dropped `W-4`/`E-1P`); read BR-TEN-171 and `apps/tenancy/service_size_classes.py` for the current set, never this sentence. **The hosted TABLE is still `meter_size_classes` — DEF-970.** Everything this row says about the list carrying NO PRICE is UNTOUCHED and still CONFIRMED. ⟨original text follows⟩ Seeded vocabulary, seven entries: **`W-58` "Water 5/8\"" · `W-34` "Water 3/4\"" · `W-1` "Water 1\"" · `W-2` "Water 2\"" · `W-4` "Water 4\"" · `E-1P` "Electric single-phase" · `E-3P` "Electric three-phase"**. **SAMPLE content** — a tenant's meter fleet is its own. **NO base-charge column, and this one is a HARD boundary, not a deferral:** r10 renders *"Water 5/8\" · base $12.00"* on this card. A base charge is **money** — `NUMERIC(18,6)` + `CHAR(3)`, B4-governed, priced by the rating engine and reachable from a plan — so a numeric price column on a settings lookup would create a second, unsigned rating input beside the signed one. If size-based base charges are wanted they are a Plans & Rates change with a finance re-sign, never a column added here. ⚠ **DIVERGES FROM r10 — see the note below** | prototype r10 `SET-04.sizes` · seed `.../lookup_defaults.json` → `meter_size_classes` · hosted `apps.tenancy.models.MeterSizeClass` (`meter_size_classes`) · CLAUDE.md rule 2 · B4 ❄️ | **CONFIRMED** for the list, its ownership and the seven seeded codes as shipped; a per-size base charge is **OUT OF SCOPE for this list by rule**, not unspecified | inherits US-TEN-009/010/011 TCs · `06-backend/tests/test_reference_data_completeness.py` |
| BR-MET-067b | ⚠ *(the list is `service_size_classes` since BR-TEN-170, 2026-08-27; this rule is otherwise untouched — a price column is still out of scope, and forty rows across eight services make that MORE true, not less: a base charge now varies by size for eight services and every one of those figures is priced by a plan.)* **`service_size_classes` CARRIES NO PRICE — sizes and parameters only (PO ruling 2026-08-16, CR-004).** Prototype r10 renders **"Water 5/8\" · base $12.00"** on this card, so the design shows a MONEY column that this table deliberately does not have. It was flagged rather than built, and the PO ruled: *"Dont build price here; we just need sizes here or parameters thats it"*. **The reason the flag existed, kept because it is the reusable part:** a base charge is money, money in this product is ❄️ B4/B5 finance-signed, and a lookup list that carries a price stops being a settings list — it becomes an input to the billing calculation, under a different review process. A numeric column added here "because the design shows one" would have moved a rating input into a screen a System Admin edits freely, with no worked figure and no finance signature. **TWO CONSEQUENCES, both now settled rather than provisional.** (1) The r10 card text is DESIGN GARNISH on this point and a lane must not treat it as a field map — the same caution the row above applies to certification types' "3-yr validity" and areas' "3 sub-areas". (2) This CLOSES the watch item recorded in the decision log 2026-08-16: the seed classification reasoned that meter size classes would have to be RECLASSIFIED from sample content to STRUCTURAL *"the moment size drives money"*. It never will, so the sample-content classification stands and the sample-data toggle continues to govern it. **What a size class MAY gain later** is a non-money parameter — a nominal diameter, a phase, a register multiplier — which is a metering shape and needs no finance path | PO ruling 2026-08-16 · CR-004 · B4/B5 ❄️ FROZEN · decision log 2026-08-16 (seed classification, watch item CLOSED) | **CONFIRMED** | none — the absence of a column is asserted by the model, not by a test |
| BR-MET-068 | **`occupancy_statuses` — how a unit is occupied AND therefore who is billed (S77).** Seeded vocabulary, five entries: **`OCC-T` "Occupied · tenant-billed" · `OCC-O` "Occupied · owner-billed" · `LNDLRD` "Landlord-billed · between tenants" · `VAC` "Vacant · base charges only" · `VAC-OFF` "Vacant · service off, no billing"**. **STRUCTURAL (in `STRUCTURAL_REFERENCE_LISTS`; the sample-data toggle CANNOT suppress it)** — and the reason is worth stating because it is not a naming convention: **these values are billing BEHAVIOUR, not description.** The distance between "bill the tenant", "bill the owner", "bill base only" and "bill nobody" is money, so a tenant who turned sample data off and then met a unit it could not classify would have undefined billing behaviour. It is also why the table is RLS-scoped in the same class as `charge_codes` — a cross-tenant read discloses how a competitor bills and a cross-tenant write would change who gets charged. ⚠ **NOT DRAWN IN r10's DATA — see the note below** | prototype r10 `SET-04.occupancy` (card only) · CR-004 (*"occupancy 5 from the PO's screenshot"*) · seed `.../lookup_defaults.json` → `occupancy_statuses` · hosted `apps.tenancy.models.OccupancyStatus` (`occupancy_statuses`) · `06-backend/db/rls_tenancy_cr004_reference_lists.sql` | **CONFIRMED** for the list, its ownership, its STRUCTURAL classification and the five seeded codes as shipped; **the CODES have no r10 `LISTS` source** and rest on the PO screenshot cited in CR-004 — flagged below, not reconciled. ⚠ **NOTHING CONSUMES THIS LIST YET.** No rule anywhere makes a unit's status drive who is billed; BR-MET-036 says occupancy is DERIVED from the active account. The vocabulary exists; the behaviour it names is unbuilt and unruled | inherits US-TEN-009/010/011 TCs · `06-backend/tests/test_reference_data_completeness.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_tenant_isolation.py::test_g5_every_rls_table_denies_a_query_with_no_tenant_context` |
| BR-MET-069b | **`occupancy_statuses` IS A MANAGED LIST AND NOTHING MORE, ON PURPOSE (PO ruling 2026-08-16, CR-004).** Reading the row above, a lane will notice the vocabulary names WHO IS BILLED and that nothing consumes it, and will be tempted to wire it to `units` and write the rule. **Do not.** PO verbatim: *"So dont connect it to Unit yet; in in CR-004 only we allow to user to set the value; later we will connect them to UNit or somewhere else and write rule"*. **CR-004's scope for this list is exactly: a tenant's System Admin can add, rename, deactivate and delete the five values on S77.** No FK from `units`, no field on any screen that assigns one, no billing behaviour. **The apparent gap is the ruling, not a defect** — do not file it as one, and do not close it by choosing a home for the FK. **Why the PO is right to split it this way, stated so the deferral is not mistaken for indecision:** the five values decide whether a customer is charged in full, charged base only, or charged nothing (`VAC-OFF` "Vacant · service off, no billing"). That is money, so it is a B4-governed rule needing a worked figure and a finance signature — not a settings tweak that rides a vocabulary change. Shipping the list first and the rule later is the ORDER that keeps an unratified billing rule out of the product. **What must be decided before it is wired:** where the status lives (a column on `units`, on the service agreement, or on the account — the PO said "UNit or somewhere else" and did not choose), what happens to a unit that has none, and how it interacts with BR-MET-036, which today DERIVES occupancy from whether an account is active — two sources for one fact is the conflict to resolve, not to discover later | PO ruling 2026-08-16 · CR-004 · BR-MET-036 (the derivation this would supersede or coexist with) | **CONFIRMED (scope); the billing rule is UNRULED and deliberately unbuilt** | none — nothing to test until it is wired; the list's own CRUD is covered by US-TEN-009/010/011 |

Seeding the type names does not answer that and must not be read as answering it — no asset table, tab or screen follows from this row. ⚠ **DIVERGES FROM r10 — see the note below** | prototype r10 `SET-04.devicetypes` (card: *"Device types (BTM)"*) · seed `.../lookup_defaults.json` → `device_types` · hosted `apps.tenancy.models.DeviceType` (`device_types`) · **OQ-249 OPEN** (whether BTM assets are in scope) | **CONFIRMED** for the list, its ownership, its STRUCTURAL classification and the six seeded codes as shipped; **BTM asset scope stays OQ-249 OPEN** | inherits US-TEN-009/010/011 TCs · `06-backend/tests/test_reference_data_completeness.py` |
| BR-MET-070 | **`certification_types` — the crew certifications a job or an asset test may require (S79).** Seeded vocabulary, six entries: **`CONF-SP` "Confined space" · `BACKFLOW` "Backflow tester" · `CDL` "Commercial driver" · `TRAFFIC` "Traffic control" · `EXCAV` "Excavation competent person" · `FIRSTAID` "First aid / CPR"**. **SAMPLE content.** **NO validity-period column, and the shape is UNSPECIFIED rather than missing:** r10's rows read *"Confined space · 3-yr validity"* and *"Backflow tester · 5-yr"*, so a per-type cadence is intended, but nothing defines whether it is a duration, a fixed renewal date, or what expiry DOES — warn, block a dispatch, or nothing. A compliance cadence that silently means nothing is worse than an absent column. ⚠ **Certification types is the one card CR-004 flags as possibly Work-owned** (*"CLAUDE.md's module map … if certification types turns out to be Work-owned"*). It is filed under metering because that is where the CR-004 build put it and metering owns assets per B2 r4; if OQ-249 puts Work in scope this row moves, and moving it is a re-home, not a rewrite. ⚠ **DIVERGES FROM r10 — see the note below** | prototype r10 `SET-04.certtypes` · seed `.../lookup_defaults.json` → `certification_types` · hosted `apps.tenancy.models.CertificationType` (`certification_types`) · CR-004 impact row · **OQ-249 OPEN** (Work ownership) | **CONFIRMED** for the list and the six seeded codes as shipped; the validity cadence is **UNSPECIFIED**; **module ownership is PROVISIONAL pending OQ-249** | inherits US-TEN-009/010/011 TCs · `06-backend/tests/test_reference_data_completeness.py` |

### ⚠ The seeded vocabulary is NOT verbatim r10, and the commit message says it is

Commit `4b02f48` states *"SEED VOCABULARY TRANSCRIBED, NEVER INVENTED. 28 entries
taken verbatim from r10's own SET-04 sub-screens"*. Read against r10's `LISTS`
object (`SMARTBillHub.dc.html`, the block beginning at line 10207), **that is true
of some entries and not of others.** This is recorded here rather than quietly
smoothed over, because the rules above are the record of what a tenant is seeded
and hard-won law 1 says a claim of a guarantee is not the guarantee. Exactly:

| List | r10 `LISTS` entries | Seeded entries | What differs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `areas` | `NORTH` "North district · 3 sub-areas" · `DTWN` "Downtown · 2 sub-areas" · `EAST` "East district · 2 sub-areas" · `IND` "Industrial corridor · 1 sub-area" | `NORTH` · `EAST` · `SOUTH` · `CENTRAL` | 2 of 4 codes match. **`DTWN` and `IND` are dropped; `SOUTH` and `CENTRAL` appear in no r10 row.** |
| `service_size_classes` (r10 drew it as `meter_size_classes`) | `W-58` · `W-34` · `W-1` · `W-2` · `E-1P-100` "Electric 1φ 100A" · `E-1P-200` "Electric 1φ 200A" · `E-3P` | `W-58` · `W-34` · `W-1` · `W-2` · `W-4` · `E-1P` · `E-3P` | 5 of 7 codes match. **`W-4` appears in no r10 row; r10's two amperage classes `E-1P-100`/`E-1P-200` are collapsed into one `E-1P`.** The `· base $…` text is dropped by design (BR-MET-067), which is a deliberate omission and not a divergence. |
| `occupancy_statuses` | **none — r10 defines the CARD (*"Occupied, vacant, owner, tenant, landlord-billed"*, badge 5) and NO row data** | `OCC-T` · `OCC-O` · `LNDLRD` · `VAC` · `VAC-OFF` | **All five codes are unsourced from r10's data.** CR-004 attributes them to *"the PO's screenshot"*; the five match the card's description and its count of 5. |
| `device_types` | `BACKFLOW` · `INVERTER` · `EVCHG` · `BATT` "Battery storage" · `COMM` "Comms endpoint" · `OTHER` "Other" | `BACKFLOW` · `INVERTER` · `EVCHG` · `BATTERY` · `ENDPOINT` · `MODEM` | 3 of 6 codes match. **`BATT`→`BATTERY`, `COMM`→`ENDPOINT`, and r10's `OTHER` is replaced by a new `MODEM` "Cellular modem".** |
| `certification_types` | `CONF-SP` "Confined space · 3-yr" · `HV-AW` "HV awareness · 2-yr" · `BF-TST` "Backflow tester · 5-yr" · `TRAF` "Traffic control · 3-yr" · `FA-CPR` "First aid / CPR · 2-yr" · `TRENCH` "Trench safety · 3-yr" | `CONF-SP` · `BACKFLOW` · `CDL` · `TRAFFIC` · `EXCAV` · `FIRSTAID` | **1 of 6 codes matches.** Four NAMES correspond under different codes (`BF-TST`→`BACKFLOW`, `TRAF`→`TRAFFIC`, `FA-CPR`→`FIRSTAID`); r10's `HV-AW` "HV awareness" and `TRENCH` "Trench safety" are **absent**, and `CDL` "Commercial driver" and `EXCAV` "Excavation competent person" are **new**. |

**What this lane did and did not do about it.** The rules above quote **what the
product seeds**, because that is what a tenant gets and what a rule must describe.
**The divergence is not resolved here** — deciding whether the seed or r10 is right
is a vocabulary judgment, and CLAUDE.md's prime directive forbids a lane resolving
one by choosing a value. It is reported to the CR-004 orchestrator for the defect
register and the PO batch. **Neither reading is a blocker for anything already
shipped:** BR-TEN-036 makes every one of these tenant-extensible and renameable, so
a corrected seed is a fixture change plus a migration for tenants already
provisioned, not a schema change.

## CR-006 · Routes & Cycles (SET-05/06/07) — PO rulings 2026-08-16, written down BEFORE the build

**Why these rows exist and are not edits to the rows above.** BR-MET-008/009/010/
033/035 were CONFIRMED on 2026-08-08 against a MODAL config screen with no
membership picker, a cycle that stored calendar DATES, and a five-role
vocabulary. CR-006 replaces that screen with two four-step wizards and carries
five PO rulings that change what a route and a cycle ARE. Each superseded clause
is named below with the row it supersedes; nothing above was edited in place
(HUMAN-GUIDE §6 — a signed rule is superseded with a date, never rewritten).

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-066b | **Areas gain a nullable SELF-REFERENCE, and this SUPERSEDES BR-MET-066's *"NO parent/child column… a `parent_id` added here would be an invented model"*.** PO verbatim, 2026-08-16: *"user can define their own areas and we wil tag them to premises ; in CR06 you can use area and sub area for filtering - all option 1 to many options will be required"*. That is the specification BR-MET-066 said did not exist, so the column stops being an invention. **The shape, stated exactly:** ONE list, one nullable `parent_id` into itself. **A row with no parent IS an area; a row with a parent IS a sub-area of it.** Depth is exactly TWO — a sub-area may not itself be a parent — because r10's card is titled *"Areas & sub-areas"* (two words, two levels) and nothing in the corpus describes a third. A cycle is refused, and so is a grandparent. **A sub-area may hold premises directly** (r10 SET-06.2 draws premises in *"South · SE"*, which is a sub-area). ~~**The four seeded rows stay top-level and no sub-area is seeded** — r10's own list data has none, and inventing "SE"/"SW" to match one screenshot's fixture would be seeding a tenant's vocabulary for it~~ ⚠ **THIS CLAUSE IS SUPERSEDED 2026-08-26 BY BR-MET-066c** (PO: *"Why you dont have sub areas below"*). The restraint was right when it was written and the PO has now asked for the opposite; every other clause of this rule — one list, one nullable parent, depth exactly TWO, a cycle refused, a grandparent refused, a sub-area may hold premises directly — is UNCHANGED and still CONFIRMED | PO ruling 2026-08-16 (CR-006 ruling 5) · r10 `SET-04.areas` card title · r10 `SET-06.2` "South · SE" · supersedes BR-MET-066's no-hierarchy clause | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_sub_area_may_not_itself_be_a_parent` |
| BR-MET-071 | **A premises carries an AREA reference — nullable FK `premises.area_id → areas`.** This is the link the CR-004 areas list shipped without: the vocabulary existed and NOTHING pointed at it, so "filter premises by area" could not be written. PO verbatim: *"we wil tag them to premises"*. **Nullable, and deliberately so** — every premises already in every tenant has no area, and a required column would make an existing row unsaveable. A premises tagged to a SUB-area is by construction also in that sub-area's parent, so one column answers both levels and no second column is added | PO ruling 2026-08-16 (CR-006 ruling 5) · BR-MET-066b | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_premises_area_filter_matches_sub_areas_of_a_parent` |
| BR-MET-072 | **The area/sub-area filter is MULTI-SELECT with an All option — one to many, never single-select.** PO verbatim: *"all option 1 to many options will be required"*. Selecting a PARENT area matches every premises tagged to it **and to any of its sub-areas**; selecting a sub-area matches only that sub-area. Selections OR together (a premises matching any selected area is in), which is the only reading of "1 to many" that returns more rows as more are picked. **All** = no area filter at all, which also returns premises with NO area — a filter that silently hid untagged premises would hide exactly the rows a utility is trying to find and tag | PO ruling 2026-08-16 (CR-006 ruling 5) · B14 rule 12 (removable filter chips, All is the default) | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_area_filter_is_multi_select_and_ors` |
| BR-MET-073 | **A route carries a SERVICE, an AREA, a READER and NOTES, and its code comes from the `route` numbering series (editable).** SUPERSEDES BR-MET-008's field list. Read type stays walk / drive / ami (BR-MET-008's vocabulary, unchanged — r10's *"Network — AMI, no visit"*, *"AMR — drive-by"*, *"Walk — manual entry"* are its display labels, not new values). **The service is what makes the rest of this CR coherent:** without it "one cycle per route PER SERVICE" and "already on another route for the same service" have no field to be true of. **Nullable on an existing row** — every shipped route predates the column — and a route with no service is the legacy "all services" route, which is why the uniqueness rule below has two constraints and not one | CR-006 SET-06 step 1 · r10 `SET-06.1` · PO ruling 2026-08-16 (Settings = System Admin) · core.numbering `ROUTE` | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_route_code_allocates_from_the_route_series` |
| BR-MET-074 | **Membership is chosen by PREMISES and stored by UNIT; a premises already on another route FOR THE SAME SERVICE MOVES, it is not refused.** SUPERSEDES the refusal shipped under BR-MET-033 (`metering.route.unit_on_another_route`), which made the CR's *"selecting moves them, with confirm"* impossible — the server refused before any confirm could be offered. **The confirmation is the UI's job and the move is the server's**, exactly as BR-MET-010 already splits them for cycles. **Uniqueness is now PER SERVICE**, because a route has one: a premises may be on the water route AND the electric route, and may not be on two water routes. Stored as a denormalized `service_id` on `route_memberships`, stamped from the route, under TWO partial unique constraints — one over (tenant, unit, service) where service is set, one over (tenant, unit) where it is NULL — because SQL treats NULLs as distinct and a single constraint would let two legacy all-service routes hold the same unit | CR-006 SET-06 step 2 · r10 `SET-06.2` *"Premises already on a route can still be added — they'll move to this one at confirm."* · supersedes BR-MET-033's refusal clause | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_selecting_a_premises_on_another_route_moves_it` · `::test_same_unit_may_sit_on_two_routes_of_different_services` |
| BR-MET-075 | **A meter may be EXCLUDED from a route and it stays on its premises.** r10 SET-06.3 verbatim: *"All are included by default — untick any that should be read elsewhere"*, and the CR: *"Excluded meters remain on the premise but off the route."* Stored as an exclusion row per (route, asset), never as a change to the asset — the asset's premises, unit and service point are untouched, so excluding a fire line from the drive-by route does not delete it, un-meter the premises, or change what it bills. A route's meter count is therefore `meters on member units − exclusions`, and the wizard states both (*"151 of 153"*) | CR-006 SET-06 step 3 · r10 `SET-06.3` | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_excluded_meter_stays_on_the_premises` |
| BR-MET-076 | **DRAFT is a third lifecycle value on routes and cycles, and a draft affects NOTHING.** CR verbatim: *"Draft state visible in tables with 'Draft' pill; drafts don't affect Reads/Billing"*. So `status` is **draft \| active \| inactive**, the wizard's last step offers **Save as draft** or **Activate**, and a draft route stamps no service point and a draft cycle is invisible to a bill run. **A draft is not an inactive:** inactive means "this used to run and no longer does" and keeps its history; draft means "this has never run" | CR-006 Shared rules | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_draft_route_stamps_no_service_point` |
| BR-MET-077 | **A CYCLE IS A DEFINITION, NOT A SCHEDULE — identity, frequency and membership — and it CARRIES DAY-OF-MONTH DEFAULTS that a run may override without rewriting them.** SUPERSEDES BR-MET-009's calendar DATES (`window_start`, `window_end`, `bill_date` as `DATE`) for everything this screen writes. PO verbatim (second ruling): *"here we are defining the defiantion of cycles and how many Routes (Premises and Units -> accounts in it) ; this is group by defination, here you define it"*; PO verbatim (third ruling): *"this is correct ; you can capture the defaults , but allow suer to override it during actual run"*. **What a cycle stores:** code · name · frequency (monthly / bi-monthly / quarterly) · its routes · and four DAY-OF-MONTH integers 1–31 — read window from/to, bill day, due day. **What it does NOT store:** any date on which money moves, and **no late/penalty anything** (ruling one — see BR-MET-078). Order is validated as read-from ≤ read-to ≤ bill day, and the due day may wrap past the bill day into the following month (r10's *"read 1–3 · bill 5th · due 26th"* does not wrap; *"bill 26th · due 5th"* would). **The override rule, recorded as a reading and cheap to correct:** an override at run applies to THAT RUN and does not rewrite the stored defaults — otherwise one unusual month silently redefines the cycle and the next month inherits a shape nobody chose. Making an override stick must be an explicit "save as the new default" action, never a side effect. **The legacy DATE columns are left in place, unwritten by this screen** — `read_schedules` and the shipped billing seams read them, and dropping them is a separate change with its own blast radius. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-23 — CR-029 (the pre-CR PO ruling + D2 + D9), and FOUR DAY-OF-MONTH INTEGERS BECAME ONE.** PO verbatim, pre-CR: *"routes and cycles are grouping only … we can capture start day, frequency, monthly, weekly daily, biweekly, once in two months , quaterly etc and days to complete it ; give business names to frequence - 1 word."* **What a cycle stores NOW:** code · name · **frequency — one of EIGHT business words** (Daily · Weekly · Fortnightly · Monthly · Bimonthly · Quarterly · Semiannual · Annual, replacing the three lowercase CR-006 values, which are still ACCEPTED on write and mapped) · **start day** · **days to complete** · its routes · and the two defaults D9 kept: **bill day** (a day of the month) and **due days** (a COUNT added to the bill date, BR-BIL-024 — which is the fact billing actually reads). **What it no longer stores:** `read_day_from` / `read_day_to` / `due_day` and the two calendar dates, all dropped by metering 0021 at RC-W5-T2. **The read window is DERIVED, never stored** — *"start day + N days IS the read window"*, INCLUSIVE — and the upgraded prototype states it as product copy on SET-07D (*"Derived, not stored"*). The ORDER rule survives in the new terms: bill day may not precede the derived close day. The OVERRIDE reading is unchanged and is now D9's own words: *"when we configure Cycle we are provideing default values; mostly it wil remain as it is but user can over ride them during billing"* — `bill_runs.due_days` is the column that made the second half buildable, and `runs.effective_due_days` is the precedence expressed once | PO rulings 2026-08-16 (CR-006 rulings 2 and 3) · **PO pre-CR ruling + D2 + D9, 2026-08-23 (CR-029)** · r10 `SET-07.1`/`SET-07.3` · the upgraded prototype `SET-07.3`/`SET-07D` · supersedes BR-MET-009's date clauses for the config screen | **CONFIRMED · AMENDED 2026-08-23** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_cycle_stores_day_of_month_defaults_not_dates` · `::test_day_order_is_validated` |
| BR-MET-078 | **NOTHING ON THESE TWO SCREENS TOUCHES MONEY. The late/penalty control is NOT BUILT.** PO verbatim: *"you dont need late fees in this. so dont have anything related to money when you create route and cycles."* r10 SET-07.3 draws a *Late / penalty date* select and its preview ends *"…due 26th · late fee 31st"*. **Neither ships.** Grace keeps exactly ONE home — `late_grace_days` (BR-BIL-008) and `late_grace_mode` (BR-BIL-002) on Billing defaults — so no second screen appears to decide when a fee starts. **The preview strip keeps every clause except the last**, and the last is not computed here, not derived here, and not displayed here from elsewhere either: a fee date rendered on this screen reads as this screen's field however it is sourced. Consequence, stated so it is not "fixed" later: **CR-006 needs no finance re-sign at any point**, because it never states a ❄️ B4/B5 figure. ⚠ **HARDENED 2026-08-23 BY CR-029, NOT REVERSED** — the PO's own words open the CR: *"routes and cycles are grouping only - so we will not tag late fees , etc"*, and the upgraded prototype states it as product copy on four screens. Three consequences were BUILT rather than assumed: SET-06D's Accounts tab is **status-only, no Balance column** (D12 — *"not required"*, superseding the screenshot's $142.30 / $88.10 rows), `GET /billing/runs` serves the cycle detail's Runs tab with **no money of any kind**, and the SET-05 cycles landing's last numeric column is a COUNT of accounts. Each absence is asserted by test rather than trusted to the reviewer's eye. **The ONE boundary the PO drew explicitly (D9):** bill day and due days are DEFAULTS the cycle carries and the run may override — they are not "late fees, etc", and neither is a money figure. **CR-029 therefore needs no finance re-sign either**, and no ❄️ FROZEN B4/B5 figure moved: golden 33 PASS / 0 FAIL on both sides of every wave | PO ruling 2026-08-16 (CR-006 ruling 1) · **PO pre-CR ruling + D9 + D12, 2026-08-23 (CR-029)** · B4/B5 ❄️ FROZEN · BR-BIL-002/008 | **CONFIRMED · HARDENED 2026-08-23** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_no_late_or_fee_field_is_accepted_on_a_cycle` |
| BR-MET-079 | **A route with READS HISTORY may not be deleted — deactivate only.** REFINES BR-MET-008's *"delete audit-locked while referenced"*, which locks on MEMBERSHIP: a route whose members were all removed became deletable even though a year of reads pointed at it. CR verbatim: *"deleting is not allowed once a route has reads history — deactivate only"*. Both guards now apply, and the refusal names which one fired and offers Deactivate | CR-006 Data integrity · refines BR-MET-008 | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_route_with_read_history_refuses_delete` |
| BR-MET-080 | **Coverage: the cycle wizard flags every ACTIVE account covered by NO cycle.** CR verbatim: *"flag any active account NOT covered by any cycle ('47 accounts unscheduled')"* — a real query, not a label. An account is covered when a service point of one of its units is stamped with a cycle whose status is not draft. The figure is computed at the moment step 3 renders, INCLUDING the routes selected in step 2 as if already assigned, because the number the staff member needs is "what will still be unscheduled after I press create", not "what is unscheduled now" | CR-006 SET-07 step 3 | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_uncovered_accounts_counts_only_active_accounts` |
| BR-MET-081 | **An edit to an ACTIVE route or cycle takes effect at the NEXT READ WINDOW, and the wizard says so inline.** CR verbatim: *"changes to an active route/cycle take effect next read window, stated inline"*. Nothing already issued changes (hard rule 4), no committed read is re-pointed, and no in-flight batch is re-scoped. The sentence is on the review step of an EDIT and absent on a create, because on a create there is nothing to take effect later. ⚠ **THE RULE IS UNCHANGED AND IS NOT KEPT — MEASURED 2026-09-22, `DEF-MET-6143`. THIS IS A NOTE ON THE IMPLEMENTATION, NOT AN AMENDMENT TO THE RULE.** *"No committed read is re-pointed"* is TRUE of the stored fact and FALSE of every screen that reports it. `meter_reads.route_id` (the `BR-MET-079` collection stamp) is written once and nothing updates it — moving RT-009’s 8 premises onto RT-001 through `services.update_route` on a seeded PostgreSQL clone left all **100** stamps in place. But `selectors.read_list`, `services.collected_read_count_impl`, `services.last_read_completion_impl` and `route_sheet.route_sheet_data` all resolve the route through `asset__service_point__route_id`, the LIVE membership projection, so the same move took RT-009’s reads list **100 → 0**, its progress **100 → 0**, its sheet **18 meters → 0** and its last-read date **2026-08-12 → None**, while RT-001’s delete guard went **8 → 108**. Only the delete guard reads the stamp. ⚠ **AND THE STAMP’S ONLY SOURCE IS `service_points.route_id`**, so any design that stops maintaining that projection stops stamping new reads — `collection_route_id` returns `None`, the column is nullable, and NOTHING GOES RED. ⚠ **RULED 2026-09-22 — PO, verbatim: *"100"*. A ROUTE REPORTS THE READINGS COLLECTED ON IT.** The question put to them was whether RT-009 should answer with the **100** readings collected on it or the **0** on its meters after the move; they chose the 100. So `read_list`, `collected_read_count_impl` and `last_read_completion_impl` resolve a route through `meter_reads.route_id` — the collection stamp, which IS the already-computed answer to *which route covered this meter when it was read* — and no longer through the live membership projection. ⚠ **THE COST IS STATED ON SCREEN RATHER THAN DISCOVERED: a reading older than the stamp has no route and leaves the route filter.** That is **0 of 516** on the seeded fixture and is not zero on a tenant predating `metering/0012`. ⚠ **THE PRINTED SHEET IS THE ONE SURFACE THAT STAYS LIVE, DELIBERATELY** — it is the walk a reader is about to make, so its meters come from the route's CURRENT premises; printing a meter that moved away last month would send somebody to the wrong door. Decision log 2026-09-22, *"THE THREE ANSWERS THAT RE-OPEN `CR-131-BRC07`"* | CR-006 Shared rules · r10 `SET-07.4` footer *"nothing already issued changes"* · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 | **CONFIRMED** | `SettingsRoutesCyclesTab.test.tsx` — "an edit states that changes land next read window" |
| BR-MET-035b | **Routes & Cycles is SYSTEM ADMIN ONLY. SUPERSEDES BR-MET-035's *"create/edit = System Admin + Billing Clerk"*.** PO verbatim 2026-08-16: *"all settingsa re Admin only I believ; follow same rule that we folowed for settings related permission"*. Gated exactly as `/lookups/*` is — `SystemAdminOnly` on the endpoint AND `@require_role(SYSTEM_ADMIN)` in the service, both, because CR-005 proved a permission test stays green with one of the two removed. Everyone else sees the screen **read-only WITH the reason on the control** (BR-SHL-025), never hidden. The CR's own *"Permissions: Owner/Admin"* is dead vocabulary — neither role exists | PO ruling 2026-08-16 · BR-IDN-009 · BR-SHL-025 · supersedes BR-MET-035 for this surface | **CONFIRMED** | `test_cr006_routes_cycles.py::test_route_write_endpoints_are_system_admin_only` |

**What is NOT in this CR, recorded so a later lane does not read it in.**
*"Dispatch can filter work orders by route/area"* is a DOWNSTREAM LINK, not a
deliverable — Work is R5 (PO, 2026-08-16), and OQ-249 still asks whether it is in
scope at all. No work-order table, tab or filter follows from BR-MET-071.

## BR-MET-082 — A ROUTE IS A CASCADE, NOT A LIST. **CONFIRMED, PO 2026-08-17.**

**PO verbatim, and it is the clearest statement of the route model we have:**

> *"Routes - Bascially Utility group few premises - based on geography/area/sub
> area -> in the process group all units in it -> group all meters/devices in it
> and -> all accounts; when you add new meter or new account, and tag it to unit,
> it automatically understand which route and cycle it should be in ; and when
> you run reading or billing it will be automatically sync; also when you move
> premises from 1 route to another; all accounts and meter units etc will move
> to"*

### The rule, in the PO's own order

1. **The utility groups PREMISES**, chosen by geography — area and sub-area.
2. Grouping a premises groups **every unit in it**.
3. Grouping a unit groups **every meter/device in it**.
4. …and **every account** on it.
5. **INHERITANCE IS AUTOMATIC AND PERPETUAL.** Attach a new meter or a new
   account to a unit *at any later time* and it "automatically understand which
   route and cycle it should be in". Nobody re-opens the route wizard.
6. **Reads and billing follow from that** without a second act — *"it will be
   automatically sync"*.
7. **MOVING A PREMISES MOVES EVERYTHING UNDER IT** — units, meters and accounts
   travel with it.

**The load-bearing word is *automatically*.** The route is a **standing
definition** whose members are DERIVED from where things sit, not a snapshot of
which ids were ticked on the day somebody drew it.

### Measured against the code the day it was ruled — two of three hold

| The PO's clause | Today | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A new **meter** on a unit already on a route inherits route + cycle | ✅ **HOLDS** | `services.py:705` — creating a `ServicePoint` looks up the unit's `RouteMembership` and inherits `route` and `cycle` from it |
| A new **unit** in a premises already on a route joins the route | ❌ **DOES NOT HOLD** | `RouteMembership` is written **only** inside `_set_route_membership`, which runs only when a route is created or edited. The route stores a **fixed list of unit ids**. A unit added afterwards has no membership row, so its service points keep `route = NULL` and **reads and billing never see it** |
| A new **account** follows the unit | ❌ **NOT MODELLED** | `apps/customers` has no route reference at all. Accounts are not part of route membership in any form |

**So the premises→unit step is an expander, not a cascade.** `premises_ids` is
expanded to the units that exist **at that moment** and then discarded; nothing
records "this route contains this PREMISES" in a way that keeps paying out.

**Filed as DEF-225.** The consequence is the quiet kind: a utility adds Unit B to
a serviced building, nothing warns anybody, and that unit is simply **never read
and never billed** until someone notices the revenue is missing.

### What this rule settles for anyone building on it

* **Route membership is a QUESTION, not a stored answer.** "Which units are on
  route R" must be derivable from where premises sit, at the time it is asked.
* **A route drawn by AREA is the same rule one level up** — CR-006 already lets a
  route carry an `area_id`, and by this rule a premises later tagged into that
  area should join the route without anyone re-opening the wizard.
* **Accounts need a path to a route** before clause 4 can mean anything. Today
  there is none, and that is a gap rather than a decision.
* ⚠ **This does NOT change what a cycle is.** The PO ruled 2026-08-16 that a
  cycle is a **definition, not a schedule** — *"here we are defining the
  defiantion of cycles… this is group by defination"*. BR-MET-082 says the
  grouping is live; it does not make anything run.

**Supersedes nothing.** BR-MET-033 and BR-MET-076 (membership drives
`service_points.route_id`) stand — this rule says **when** membership is
recomputed, which those two never stated.

### BR-MET-082 — AMENDMENT 1, PO 2026-08-17. **The chain, and RUN-TIME resolution.**

The PO restated the model one level higher and one step further than the
original ruling. **Verbatim:**

> *"Please note Cycles -> Route -> Premises -> Units {Meters, Devices, Accounts}
> - that is the relationship; any change any movement make the changes to
> downstream; hence Routes and Cycle are dyanmic and at the time of Run -> it
> will figure out how many accounts or meter or devices it has to take into
> account; every run it is not necessry it will ahve same number of accounts or
> meters - since Utility will make changes"*

**Three things this adds to the rule as first written:**

1. **THE CHAIN STARTS AT THE CYCLE.** `Cycle → Route → Premises → Unit →
   {Meter, Device, Account}`. The first version of BR-MET-082 began at the
   route; the cycle sits above it and the same cascade applies at every link.
2. **"ANY CHANGE ANY MOVEMENT MAKES THE CHANGES TO DOWNSTREAM."** A change at
   any level propagates to everything under it, in both directions of travel —
   things joining and things leaving.
3. ⚠ **RESOLUTION HAPPENS AT RUN TIME, AND A VARYING COUNT IS CORRECT
   BEHAVIOUR.** *"at the time of Run it will figure out how many accounts or
   meter or devices it has to take into account; every run it is not necessry it
   will ahve same number"*. **Two runs of the same cycle covering different
   numbers of accounts is not a bug and must never be guarded against as one** —
   it is the utility having made changes in between.

### Measured again under the amendment — the run is dynamic, its INPUT is not

| Clause | Today | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The run recounts every time rather than replaying a frozen list | ✅ **HOLDS** | `selectors.units_in_cycle` runs a live query at run time; nothing is snapshotted into the run |
| A new **meter** appears in the next run without anyone touching the route | ✅ **HOLDS** | `services.py:705` — a new `ServicePoint` inherits `route` and `cycle` from the unit's membership, so the next `units_in_cycle` finds it |
| A new **unit** in a premises already on a route appears in the next run | ❌ **NEVER** | `units_in_cycle` filters `service_points.cycle_id`, a **stamp** written only when a route is created or edited. A unit added later has no `RouteMembership`, so its service points are stamped `NULL` and **no run will ever see them** |
| An **account** follows the chain | ❌ **NOT MODELLED** | `apps/customers` holds no route or cycle reference |

**So the product is dynamic in the right place and static one link up.** The
count genuinely varies run to run — but only ever by meters, never by units.
`units_in_cycle`'s own docstring states the design plainly: *"the cycle stamp
follows route membership (BR-MET-033)"*. A stamp is the opposite of a cascade,
and until now no rule said which one was intended.

**⚠ THE CONSEQUENCE IS BIGGER THAN READS AND IS NOW NAMED: THE UNIT IS NEVER
BILLED.** `units_in_cycle` is **billing's scope seam** — `apps/billing/runs.py`
asks it which units a run covers. A unit that never gets a cycle stamp is
therefore invisible to the bill run as well as to the reading run. A utility
adds Unit B to a serviced building; nobody is warned; **Unit B is never read and
never invoiced** until somebody notices the money is missing.

**DEF-225 carries this.** Its title understated the blast radius — it said
"never read and never billed" of the reading path; this amendment establishes
that the same single stamp is what billing scopes on.

### What a fix must satisfy, stated so it can be checked rather than guessed

* **Membership is a QUESTION asked at run time, not an answer stored at draw
  time.** "Which units are in cycle C" must be derived from where premises sit
  when the run asks.
* **A varying count between runs is CORRECT** and no guard may treat it as
  drift. ⚠ Note the tension a fix must resolve deliberately: `units_in_cycle`'s
  docstring currently promises *"a run scoped twice covers the same accounts in
  the same order — a run whose membership shifts between the preview and the
  issue is a run whose approval meant something different from what shipped."*
  **That stability is about one run's preview-to-issue window, and the PO's rule
  is about run-to-run.** Both can hold — resolve live, then freeze for the
  duration of a single run — but a fix that reads only one of these two
  sentences will break the other.
* **Accounts need a path to a cycle** before the PO's *"how many accounts"* can
  be answered at all.

---

## BR-MET-083 — TAGGING IS THE CASCADE'S ENTRY POINT. **CONFIRMED 2026-08-17** (CR-006, DEF-220/DEF-225).

**The PO ruling this implements, verbatim:**

> *"when we add premises you should allow to select areas and sub areas on
> premises"*

**and BR-MET-082, which is what makes it more than a form field:**

> *"any change any movement make the changes to downstream"*

`routes.area_id` has existed since CR-006 and nothing has ever paid it out. A
route drawn for NORTH and a premises tagged NORTH were two facts that never met.
This rule is where they meet.

### The rule, in two clauses

**(a) TAGGING A PREMISES INTO AN AREA JOINS IT TO THAT AREA'S ACTIVE ROUTES.**
When a premises is created with an area, or its area is changed, every **active**
route whose `area_id` is that area **or that area's parent** gains every unit of
that premises as a member, and their service points are stamped with the route
and its cycle. Nobody re-opens the route wizard.

**(b) A UNIT ADDED TO A PREMISES ALREADY ON A ROUTE JOINS THAT ROUTE.** When a
unit is created, it joins the active routes its **premises** is on — one per
service — before its meter grid runs, so the service points the grid creates
inherit route and cycle through the path `services.ensure_service_point` already
uses.

**"The routes its premises is on" is read from BOTH directions, and reading only
one leaves a hole a whole building falls through:** where its **sibling units
actually are** (what a utility sees on SET-05, and what DEF-225 is literally
about) **and the routes drawn for its AREA**. The second is not redundant — a
premises created straight into an area has no units yet when the tag lands, so
the area cascade correctly does nothing, and every unit added afterwards is then
a "first" unit with no sibling to inherit from. A sibling-only rule would
silently never route that building at all. **Siblings win ties per service:**
where a human has put unit A on a route, unit B joins unit A.

### Four boundaries, each stated so it can be checked rather than guessed

1. **ACTIVE ROUTES ONLY, in both clauses.** A draft route gains nothing from a
   tag and nothing from a new unit. DEF-219 ruled
   the same day that *"a draft is a thing that has not happened yet and must not
   have consequences a person can see"* — growing a draft's Members count on
   SET-05 from the premises screen is exactly that shape. When the draft is
   activated through the wizard, the membership the admin saw is what it saves.
2. **A CASCADE NEVER TAKES A UNIT OFF ANOTHER ROUTE.** A unit already on a route
   **for that service** is left alone. Moving it is BR-MET-074's move, which the
   CR requires be **confirmed on screen** — a silent move from a form field
   would be a move nobody agreed to. So a cascade only ever fills a gap.
3. **A PARENT'S ROUTE COVERS ITS SUB-AREAS.** A premises tagged `SOUTH-SE` joins
   a route drawn for `SOUTH`. This is the same direction `_area_ids_for` already
   reads for the filter (a parent matches its sub-areas), applied to one row
   instead of a chip list, so the two never disagree.
4. **UNTAGGING CASCADES NOTHING OFF.** Clearing or changing a premises' area does
   not remove it from routes it is already on. Removing a premises from a route
   is the wizard's job and it is audited there; a tag change that silently
   emptied a live route is DEF-219's defect with a different trigger.

### ⚠ WHAT THIS RULE DOES **NOT** DO — read this before citing it as DEF-225 closed

BR-MET-082 Amendment 1 asks for membership to be **derived at run time**.
**This rule does not do that.** Membership is still a STORED answer; what changes
is that two more write paths keep it correct. Measured consequence, stated
plainly:

* The **two named symptoms are gone** — a premises tagged into an area is read
  and billed by that area's routes, and a unit added to a serviced building is
  read and billed. Both were previously never.
* **What still holds a stale answer:** a premises MOVED between areas keeps its
  old routes (boundary 4, deliberate); a route whose `area_id` is CHANGED does
  not re-derive its members; a unit moved between premises does not follow (no
  such path exists — BR-MET-046 forbids cross-premises moves).
* **Accounts still have no path to a route or a cycle at all.** BR-MET-082
  clause 4 remains unmodelled, and no code in this repo can answer *"how many
  accounts does this run cover"* through the chain.
* **The preview-to-issue freeze is not built and is not needed yet**, precisely
  because membership is still stored: `units_in_cycle` keeps its stability
  promise unchanged, because nothing about this rule makes it re-derive. The
  tension Amendment 1 names is therefore **still unresolved and still waiting**
  for the run-time derivation — this rule neither resolves it nor breaks it.

**DEF-225 stays OPEN** and says so in its own row.

## BR-MET-084 — UNIT TYPES ARE SEEDED, TENANT-EXTENSIBLE ROWS. **CONFIRMED 2026-08-17** (CR-010 A-section item 4, PO verbatim *"this is correct"* on "Premise & unit types (one card, two levels)"; built 2026-08-18).

Until this rule the unit-type vocabulary was `UNIT_TYPES`, a hard-coded 5-tuple
in `apps/metering/services.py` (US-MET-002 §5's option set) — a closed enum no
tenant could extend and no screen could show. Now:

1. **The table.** `unit_types` (metering 0011, RLS ENABLE+FORCE in the same
   migration) — the exact `premises_types` shape: tenant-scoped, code unique
   per tenant case-insensitively, `is_system`/`active`.
2. **The seed.** The five US-MET-002 §5 codes seed `is_system` per tenant
   (SEED_UNIT_TYPES; names are the product's existing `metering.units.type.*`
   display copy — nothing renamed). DISCRETIONARY under BR-TEN-067: nothing
   schema-required needs a unit type (`units.unit_type` is nullable), so the
   S111 toggle governs the list. Fixture carries NO entries — the constant is
   the single ratified source (DEF-026 posture).
3. **Validation.** `units.unit_type` STAYS a TEXT code (no FK — the column
   predates the table and importers write legacy codes). `_resolve_unit_type`
   consults the tenant's rows FIRST (a deactivated row is NOT pickable, and
   the fallback may not resurrect it), the legacy tuple second (a tenant with
   no rows keeps working; every value ever stored stays valid).
4. **The card.** S8 → PREMISES group → "Unit types", full CRUD beside
   "Premises types" — r10 SET-04's one "Premise & unit types" card is built as
   the group's two sibling cards (one-list-per-card frame + the DEF-236 join
   guard; recorded in the decision log 2026-08-18). Because there is no FK
   PROTECT, the HANDLER enforces BR-TEN-030 (a USED code is frozen) and
   BR-TEN-034 (a USED row is not deletable) by live count over units;
   BR-TEN-036 (seeded rows never deletable) as everywhere.

**⚠ REPORTED, deliberately not built here:** the S16/S14 unit-form dropdown
still offers the five DEFAULT types from its own frontend constant
(`UnitsListPage.UNIT_TYPES` + the `metering.units.type.*` catalogue), so a
tenant-ADDED type is accepted by the API and creatable from imports but not
yet offered by that dropdown. Wiring the dropdown to `GET /lookups/unit_types`
is a frontend slice in the unit screens' territory, not invented in this one.

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-084 | Unit types = Reference data rows: seeded 5 (`is_system`), tenant-extensible, S8 card; `units.unit_type` stays TEXT with table-first/legacy-fallback validation; handler enforces the used-row code-freeze and delete-lock | CR-010 A-4 (PO 2026-08-17) · US-MET-002 §5 (the seeded codes) | CONFIRMED | tests/test_cr010_subtypes_unit_types.py |

(PO ruling 2026-08-21 — CR-023; REVERSES r10.)** PO verbatim: _"We we load premises - it will be only premises."_ ⚠ **r10 draws the opposite** and prints it twice — its stage-4 dry run and its stage-5 receipt both read _"426 premises · **181 single-unit premises auto-create their unit**"_, and its NEXT callout offers _"or let single-unit premises create their own"_. **All three clauses are withdrawn by this rule.** The reason it matters is that an auto-created unit is a row the operator never supplied and never reviewed, and a unit is what an ACCOUNT attaches to — so 181 unreviewed billing anchors would have entered the tenant in one commit, inside a run whose receipt reported only premises. The standing instruction that r10 outranks a stale spec does NOT reach this: a ruling made today outranks both. ⛔ **NARROWED 2026-09-01 BY `BR-MET-1030` — READ THIS BEFORE CITING THE HEADLINE.** The PO ruled that morning: *"If owner name is given and one identify given - DL, SSN, Phone or Tax ID - then you create master account and Customer ID - otherwise dont create anything"*. So a premises import now creates **an owner's master account and a Customer #** where the row carries identity, and the headline *"and NOTHING ELSE"* is no longer literally true. **WHAT IS NOT REVERSED IS THIS RULE'S OWN REASON, AND IT IS WHY THE NARROWING IS SAFE:** the stated hazard is *"an auto-created unit is a row the operator never supplied and never reviewed, and a unit is what an ACCOUNT attaches to"*. **No unit is auto-created, then or now** — that clause stands in full, `BR-CUS-1010` hangs the owner's account off the ADDRESS rather than a unit, and nothing is created for a row whose operator did not supply the identity to create it from. So the units import is **the SAME five-stage ETL runner**, not a second mechanism — one workflow, two targets — and its sheet carries **unit details · unit attributes · services · the premise reference**, alongside the plan reference this rule already required. **"Similar to import premises" is the load-bearing half:** it means the stage bar, the mapping table, the row-handling checkboxes, the dry run, the write modes, the receipt and the rollback are ONE implementation parameterised by target, and a lane that builds a second, unit-shaped wizard has misread it. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 + amendment same day (decision log) · CR-023 · design B15 §4A · OQ-312 | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** · UNBUILT | CR-023 P8 · B15 §4A |
