Defensible as a policy, indefensible as a default, so the pair is configured together and any row it touched is flagged on the receipt. ⚠ **AND A RUN'S RESULTS ARE STORED, NEVER RECOMPUTED:** re-deriving them from today's configuration would rewrite history — a receipt reading _"2 warnings"_ in July would read _"2 errors"_ in August with nothing to show the run itself never changed. Changing a rule applies to the NEXT run and never re-validates loaded data; a utility wanting old rows re-checked is asking for a REPORT, which is a different feature. A retired rule's `code` is never reused, because run results still name it. ⚠ **BUILT 2026-08-21 (CR-023 P4 lane) for `target=premises`, and two things the ruling leaves open were settled at the BOUNDARY rather than chosen — both are open questions, not decisions.** **(1) r10's `Default to Residential` is NOT shipped.** This rule already calls it *"indefensible as a default"*, and there is a second reason that ends the argument: **`BR-MET-034` seeds five premises types and none is called Residential** (Apartment complex · Commercial building · Single-family · Mixed-use · Mobile-home park), so r10's action would set a foreign key to a code no tenant has — DEF-388 again. `PRM.TYPE_IN_LOOKUP` therefore ships `warning` + `flag_only`: the row loads with **no type set** and is flagged on the receipt. Whether it should default, and to WHICH of the tenant's own codes, is `OQ-329`. **(2) An unknown AREA rejects the row** (`PRM.REFERENCES_ON_FILE`, tier 1), because tier 1 says every FK resolves and the two alternatives each cost something nobody has agreed to: *creating* the area authors permanent reference data from a spreadsheet (and is a WRITE, which stages 1–3 may not do), *leaving it null* silently discards a value the operator supplied. `OQ-328` holds it. **AND TIER 1 IS ENFORCED AS UNCONFIGURABLE IN CODE, not merely documented:** an `import_rules` row carrying a structural code is IGNORED by the resolver, because a screen reporting that a rule the database enforces has been switched off is worse than no screen. | CR-023 · design B15 §4.3A · BR-MET-003 · `uq_premises_tenant_premise_no` · `metering.ReadValidationSettings` (the precedent) · `OQ-328` · `OQ-329` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** · **BUILT for target=premises 2026-08-21** | CR-023 P4 · B15 §4.3A · `tests/test_cr023_import_stages.py::test_tc17__changing_a_rule_does_not_change_an_old_runs_receipt` |
| BR-MET-091 | **A VALIDATION RULE THE PRODUCT CANNOT EVALUATE IS DECLARED AND MARKED UNAVAILABLE, NEVER OMITTED. (CR-023.)** r10's only ERROR-severity rule on a premises import is _"Address must geocode inside the service territory"_, with the action `Reject row` — and **this product has no geocoder and no service-territory boundary**, verified by grep across `06-backend/apps` and `07-frontend/src`. The rule is therefore listed on the Validate stage with its severity and its reason for being unavailable, rather than quietly absent. **This is the stated-absence discipline (`DEF-421`, `DEF-445`, G38) applied to a RULE rather than to a sentence**, and the reason it matters here is arithmetic: with that rule gone there are **no error-severity rules left at all** on a premises import, so the stage's own promise — _"Errors block the load"_ — becomes something the product can never do, while the screen goes on saying it. An operator reading three rules where four were expected learns nothing; one reading four, with one marked unavailable and why, learns exactly what protection they have. ⚠ **AMENDMENT 1, 2026-08-21 (CR-023 P4 lane, from building it) — THE ARITHMETIC CLAUSE IS TRUE OF THE PLATFORM RULES AND FALSE OF THE LOAD, AND THE DIFFERENCE DECIDES WHETHER A MIGRATION CAN BE STOPPED AT ALL.** The clause above reads _"with that rule gone there are **no error-severity rules left at all** on a premises import"_. Measured against the built rule set that is **exactly right about r10's four PLATFORM rules and wrong about the import**. r10 draws four rules and only `PRM.ADDRESS_IN_TERRITORY` is an error, so with it unavailable **the count of tier-2 platform rules that can block a premises load is `0`** — that half stands, and `GET`ing a validated run now SERVES it (`blocking_rules.platform`). But `BR-MET-090`'s **tier 1** rules are error-severity by construction, are not configurable, and DO block: as built there are **five** — `PRM.ADDRESS_REQUIRED`, `PRM.PREMISE_NO_UNIQUE`, `PRM.SERVICES_ENABLED`, `PRM.REFERENCES_ON_FILE`, `PRM.VALUES_PARSE` — and on the 431-row §16 fixture they reject **3** rows (no address · a service the tenant does not sell · a repeated premise ID) while the two platform warnings flag **2** more. So *"Errors block the load"* is a promise this product CAN keep; what it cannot do is let a **utility** configure an error, because every error it has comes from the schema. Left unamended the rule reads as licence to tell an operator that nothing can stop a bad file, which is the opposite of what is built. Nothing in the ruling is reversed — an unevaluable rule is still declared, still listed, still carries its reason. ⛔ **AMENDMENT 2, 2026-09-01 (`DEF-2794`) — THE PLATFORM HALF IS NOW FALSE TOO, BY RULING, AND THE FIGURES MOVE 0/5/5 → 1/6/7.** Amendment 1 says *"the count of tier-2 platform rules that can block a premises load is `0`"*. That was measured and right on 2026-08-21. **The PO ruled on 2026-08-27 that a premise number is issued by the tenant's own numbering settings**, and `CR-046` C2 built `PRM.PREMISE_NO_NOT_ON_FILE` — an id that is NOT already on file is one the operator invented — as a **tier-2 PLATFORM rule at ERROR severity with `reject_row`**. C5 added `PRM.EXTERNAL_REF_UNIQUE` as a sixth **tier-1 STRUCTURAL** error. So the built set is now **platform 1 · structural 6 · seven blocking rules in all**, and a premises import CAN be stopped by a platform rule. **The distinction amendment 1 drew still holds and is still the point** — a UTILITY still cannot configure an error into existence; both new rules come from a ruling and from the schema, not from a tenant's settings. ⚠ **AND THE ARITHMETIC IS WHY THIS WAS FOUND AT ALL:** the CR-023 §16 fixture supplied a `PREMISE_ID` on all 431 rows, so from the day of that ruling a file built the old way forwarded 428 rows and rejected all 428, and the two CR-023 suites stood **35 tests red for four days** with nobody reading them (`DEF-2794`; `DEF-665`'s shape, one CR later). | CR-023 · design B15 §4.3A · DEF-421 / DEF-445 (the same shape in copy) · G38 · `apps/metering/import_rules.py` · `import_rules.error_severity_rules` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** · **AMENDED 2026-08-21 (amendment 1)** · **BUILT for target=premises** (stages 1–3; `target=units` refused by name) | CR-023 P4 · B15 §4.3A · `tests/test_cr023_import_stages.py::test_with_the_territory_rule_unavailable_no_platform_error_rule_remains` |
| BR-MET-092 | **ONE UNIT CARRIES ONE PLAN AT A TIME AND MANY SERVICES — SO THE UNITS SHEET IS ONE ROW PER UNIT WITH ONE PLAN COLUMN. (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — closes `OQ-312`.)** PO verbatim: _"One Unit one plan at a time it can have multiple services."_ **The PLAN carries the services, so the unit does not have to:** a plan's `plan_services` rows say which services it prices, so one plan on a unit covers all of them. This is why `Unit.rate_plan` is a single FK and always was, and why `Unit.plan_effective_from` sits beside it for the _"at a time"_ half — a plan on a unit is a dated fact, not a permanent one. **The rejected alternative was one row per unit-service**, which would have made a 1,247-unit tenant produce a 3,741-row file where the operator has 1,247 units, and forced the loader to group rows before it could create anything. ⚠ **AND THE RULING CREATES A VALIDATION RULE NOBODY ASKED FOR:** if a sheet lists a unit with services `E,W,S` and its plan prices only `E,W`, that unit **cannot bill sewer** — the services and the plan disagree, and neither is wrong on its own. That is a stage-3 rule and it can only exist because the plan and the services arrive in the same row. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `OQ-312` CLOSED · design B15 §4B · `Unit.rate_plan` · `plan_services` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** · UNBUILT | CR-023 P8 · B15 §4B |
| BR-MET-093 | **THE NEW-UNIT WIZARD'S `Bulk from CSV` MODE IS THE ETL RUNNER, NOT A SECOND CSV PATH — AND SO IT CREATES NO METER. (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — closes `OQ-313`.)** PO verbatim: _"It is same ETL runner"_. Choosing that mode opens the five-stage workflow with target=units, consistent with _"import units will be similar to import premises"_ (`BR-MET-086`). ⚠ **THE CONSEQUENCE IS THE PART WORTH WRITING DOWN, because it is not in the words:** since the mode IS the import, `BR-MET-087` reaches it — **no meter and no asset is created on that path**, even though the wizard it launches from has a step called _Meters & plan_ and the mode's own description names _"existing meter IDs"_. That step belongs to the INTERACTIVE modes (Single unit, Pattern generator) and is not reached on the bulk path. **A lane building from the screenshot alone would have built the other thing** — a second, wizard-local CSV path that may set meters — and that is precisely the cross-module write `BR-MET-087` removed. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `OQ-313` CLOSED · `BR-MET-086` · `BR-MET-087` · design B15 §4B | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** · UNBUILT | CR-023 P2, P8 · B15 §2 |
| BR-MET-094 | **A VACANT UNIT IS BILLED, AUTOMATICALLY, AND IT IS NOT A CHOICE. (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — answers `OQ-316` in part.)** PO verbatim: _"Yes vacant units will be charged we will not option base charges or full billing"_. Two things are settled: billing a vacancy is **AUTOMATIC**, driven by the unit rather than by a staff decision per vacancy, and there is **no toggle** between base-only and full billing. `Unit.vacant_standby_auto` is the column that was anticipating it. ⚠ **This SUPERSEDES the earlier reading that base-only billing is a staff choice** — that ruling was about a BILL RUN, not about a vacancy, and the two were being conflated. ⛔ **ONE WORD REMAINS OPEN and the reason it matters is arithmetic:** with zero usage both treatments produce the SAME NUMBER, so the difference only ever appears in the case that gets disputed. Silver Creek residential water — base **$8.00**/month, first block **$4.00**/kgal — a vacant unit that used nothing bills **$8.00** either way; **a vacant unit with a running toilet that passed 3 kgal bills $8.00 under base-only and $20.00 under full billing.** The owner receiving that bill is the person most likely to query it. WHO receives it is `OQ-314`'s master account. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (decision log) · `OQ-316` (part) · `OQ-314` (the payer) · `Unit.vacant_standby_auto` | **CONFIRMED in part (2026-08-21)** · UNBUILT · the base-only-vs-full word is OPEN | CR-023 · B15 §2A.3 |
| BR-MET-095 | **A UNIT MAY BE MOVED TO ANOTHER PREMISE, AND THE MOVE IS A DATED EVENT — NEVER A REWRITE OF THE PAST. (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — CR-024; SUPERSEDES-REFINES `BR-MET-046`.)** PO verbatim: _"please ensure move units works in Units; we have that functionalities - you can have a modal for that; bascially user has put a unit in wrong premises they want to move it to correct premises."_ **What the move does:** `units.premises_id` changes from the effective date forward, an `audit_log` row names BOTH premises, the effective date and the reason (hard rule 7), and **every already-posted figure keeps the premise it was posted under.** ⚠ **WHY THIS DOES NOT COST THE TRACEABILITY BR-MET-046 WAS PROTECTING, measured rather than argued:** `journal_lines.premises_id` is **STAMPED AT POSTING TIME** (`apps/ledger/posting.py`, US-LED-002, landed 2026-08-16) and `ledger.reporting.by_premises_summary` groups on that stamp with **no read-time account→unit→premises join** — its own docstring says so and **US-LED-010 TC-5 is a structural test on that function's source asserting it**, for exactly this reason: _"a read-time account → unit → premises join moves LAST YEAR'S money the day a unit is re-assigned"_. So on LE-6's month the by-premises summary reads 500 Main St **247.87** · Oak Grove **16.42** · Marina Bay **289.40**, footing to **553.69**, and it reads the same three figures against the same three premises after a unit is moved. **The chain guarantee is the stamp. BR-MET-046 refused the move to protect a property the ledger already protects by itself.** **Two cases, and the screen says which one it is in:** a unit that has **never had an account** has never billed — a pure data-entry correction, allowed with a reason. A unit that **has had an account** is the case BR-MET-046 was written for: it warns, states what it can count, and takes an **override with a recorded reason** — the shape the PO already ruled for a refused plan assignment (BR-MET-042, _"allow the override to handle human or user errors"_). ⛔ **THE WARNING CANNOT STATE A BILL COUNT AND DOES NOT PRETEND TO — `DEF-511`.** CR-024 asks it to _"state how many bills"_; no seam or selector answers issued-invoices-by-unit, so the screen states the number of ACCOUNTS ever attached, says plainly that the bill count is unavailable, and carries that row. A plausible number here would be the defect the rule is written against. ⚠ **THE SILENT VERSION IS FORBIDDEN:** a move with no effective date, no reason and no audit row is refused by construction — `services.move_unit` requires all three, and a move onto the premise the unit is already on is refused rather than written as a no-op event. | PO ruling 2026-08-21 (CR-024) · supersedes-refines `BR-MET-046` (PO PU-19) · `apps/ledger/posting.py` US-LED-002 · US-LED-010 TC-5 · `BR-LED-011` · `DEF-511` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-21)** · BUILT (`services.move_unit`, `POST /units/{id}/move`, the PRM-01U / PRM-03 move modal) | CR-024 · `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_095_unit_move.py` (6 cases) · `UnitsPage.test.tsx` (the move-modal cases) |
| BR-MET-096 | **PREMISES AND UNITS RUN THE SAME IMPORT WORKFLOW. ONE RUNNER, FIVE STAGES, AND THE ONLY STAGE THAT DIFFERS IS TRANSFORM. (PO ruling 2026-08-21.)** PO verbatim: _"same process for premises and Unit - only at tranform stage we differntate field for premises and Units"_. So there is **ONE** workflow — `Extract → Transform → Validate → Load → Result` (r10 `WFL-01`, drawn identically for `WFL-01.premises.*` and `WFL-01.units.*`) — and the target is chosen at **Extract**, in the *"Loads into"* field (`Premises` \| `Units`). Everything else is shared: the four source cards (File · SFTP · API · Manual), the drop zone with its row count and SHA-256, the mapping template and encoding pickers, the batch label, the stage rail, the validate tiles and rules table, the *"fix the rejects here — no need to re-upload"* editor, the dry-run panel and write modes, the receipt with its rollback point. **What the target changes is exactly one thing: which field map the Transform stage offers, and which rules Validate runs against it.** A units file maps `UNIT_REF → Unit label`, `PREMISE_ID → Parent premise`, `UNIT_TYPE`, `SQFT → Floor area`, `METER_E`/`METER_W`, `OCCUPANT`, `STATUS`, `ROUTE`; a premises file maps its own fourteen (B15 §4.2A). ⚠ **This forbids building two importers.** A second code path for units would be two implementations of one ruled process, and they would diverge at the first defect. The runner takes the target as a parameter; the field map and the rule set are looked up from it. (Consequence for the load stage: `Target` is a line ON the dry-run panel — r10 prints _"Target · Units"_ — not a different screen.) | CONFIRMED |  **⚠ AMENDED 2026-09-07 — THE SEVENTH TARGET, AND IT IS THE THIRD MODULE THIS RUNNER WRITES THROUGH (`CR-077`).** `parts` joins premises, units, the three assets targets and accounts as a dict entry — `TARGET_PARTS`, its column set, its aliases, its default mapping template, its dedupe key and its rule set — and not as a second runner. ⚠ **THE TABLE IS `work`'s, SO METERING DOES NOT WRITE IT.** `BR-WRK-002` forbids a foreign write and `G78` fails one from Work's first commit, so the LOAD calls `apps/work/import_writer.py` through `resolve("work", …, kind="import_writer")` — the same `import_writer` seam KIND `customers.create_master_account_from_import` already uses, and for the same reason (`DEF-1108`: a deferred load runs in a worker carrying `_JobActor`, an id and no role by design, so the door must be UNDECORATED and the role is checked once at `load()`). The boundary is therefore STRUCTURAL rather than a thing to be careful about: there is no ORM statement in `apps/metering/` to review. ⚠ **AND A TARGET'S COLUMN SET IS THE OWNING MODULE'S WRITER'S SIGNATURE, NOT ITS TABLE'S** — measured the day this landed. `parts` carries `active` and `is_system`; neither is reachable through `work.services._clean_part`, whose allow list refuses an unknown field rather than ignoring it, so a template offering either rehearses CLEAN and rejects every row at the write. **No stage-3 rule can catch that**, because nothing in the catalogue asks *will the writer take this field*. Decision log 2026-09-07.
| BR-MET-097 | **AN IMPORTED ADDRESS IS ACCEPTED AS GIVEN. WE DO NOT CHECK IT, AND NOTHING REJECTS A ROW FOR IT. (PO ruling 2026-08-21 — the first of the five CR-023 decisions, answered in the working chat.)** PO verbatim: _"We dont have to check; accept what utility user give right now; theya re giving it from their GIS system hence it is assumed it is correct; any upgrade we can think later"_. So r10's validate rule *"the address must geocode inside the service territory"* is **NOT BUILT** — not "unavailable", not stubbed, not drawn greyed: absent, because the utility's GIS is the system of record for its own addresses and re-deciding them here would put us in an argument with it. No geocoder, no territory list, no `standardise to USPS` transform, and **no network call inside a load**. ⚠ **THE CONSEQUENCE, RULED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED: a premises import has NO error-severity PLATFORM rule left.** The only rules that can reject a premises row are the five STRUCTURAL ones no utility can configure (a required column unmapped, a row that will not parse, and so on). Stage 3's sub-line — *"Errors block the load. Warnings don't"* — stays true and stays honest, because structural errors are real errors; what it no longer implies is that we validated the address. `blocking_rules.platform` reads `0` on a premises import BY DESIGN, and BR-MET-091 already serves that number rather than letting a screen compute it. **The upgrade path is open and deliberately unscheduled:** a territory list or a paid geocoding service can be added later without moving a row already loaded, because nothing was rejected on this basis and so nothing has to be revisited. | CONFIRMED |
| BR-MET-098 | **A LOAD NEEDS A SECOND APPROVAL WHEN REJECTIONS EXCEED 5% OF THE BATCH *OR* EXCEED 10 ROWS — WHICHEVER COMES FIRST. BOTH FIGURES ARE THE TENANT'S TO SET. THE APPROVER IS AN OWNER OR AN ADMIN. (PO ruling 2026-08-21, option B of three; the second of the five CR-023 decisions.)** A percentage ALONE punishes small files, and the people running small corrective imports run them most often: on a 20-row batch 5% is **1 row**, and on an 8-row batch it is **0.4 rows** — so every correction batch with a single bad row would stop for approval, which is the rule staff learn to route around. A count alone has the mirror flaw: a 5,000-row migration with **200 rejects (4%)** would sail through unseen. So a big file is governed by the percentage and a small one by the count, and the screen prints **both** figures beside the run's actual numbers — r10's worked run is *"1 rejected of 246"*, which is 0.41% and 1 row, under both bars. ⚠ **THE APPROVER IS THE SAME PAIR OF ROLES AS THE RATE-CHANGE SECOND APPROVAL** (BR-RAT-091's 10% bar, PO 2026-08-21): Owner or Admin. Deliberately not a third role — one answer to *"who signs off on something unusual"* is easier for a 1–2 staff utility to run than two. ⚠ **THE `5%` PRINTED ON THE SCREEN IS NOW A RULED FIGURE.** Until this ruling it was r10's drawing and the screen said so; that hedge comes off, and a test asserts both bars so drift goes RED (HUMAN-GUIDE §6). ⚠ **WHERE THE APPROVER IS CHECKED ON A LOAD THAT DEFERS — LANE CLARIFICATION 2026-08-27, `DEF-1108`. THE RULE IS UNCHANGED AND STILL CONFIRMED AS RULED; only its enforcement POINT is stated, because it was in a place that could not answer.** Over `ASYNC_ROW_BAR` (5,000 rows) the commit runs in a worker (CR-026), and a worker carries an actor id and **no role** by design (`_JobActor`). `DEF-545` put this rule's approver check in `_load_locked`, which BOTH a request and a worker run — so **every** deferred load over the approval bar refused with `BIZ-APPROVAL (metering.import.approver_role)`, a refusal naming a role the caller had and the worker structurally could not. **Worked: 12,400 meters with 63 rejects** (0.51%, under the percent bar, over the 10-row bar) — the admin approves, the call answers 202, and none of the 12,337 good rows are written. **The approver named by this rule is checked on the REQUEST THAT ENQUEUES THE COMMIT** — `api.py::import_load` refuses `approved` from a non-admin at the edge, and `import_runs.load` now re-checks the same role itself before it defers, refusing on the caller's own call. The verified approver's user id is then carried on the job payload (`approved_by_user_id`, stripped from any caller-supplied payload and re-stamped only after that check passes), and the engine asks *"was this approval given by a role-checked approver"* rather than *"does this actor hold a role"*. **This is not a separation-of-duties posture and does not become one:** this rule names a ROLE, there is no approve route to visit later, and `BR-PAY-037` declines enforced separation for v1 — ⚠ **and `OQ-226`, which was where that would have changed, is CLOSED: the PO RE-AFFIRMED v1 unchanged on 2026-09-23** (*"allow it - we are dealing with smaller utilites they dont have too many people"*), so a lane arriving here should not expect an open question to walk through. Moving enforced separation forward now needs a NEW ruling. | CONFIRMED |
| BR-MET-099 | **"REPLACE THE BATCH" DEACTIVATES WHAT THE FILE NO LONGER CARRIES, REFUSES ANYTHING OCCUPIED, AND SHOWS ALL THREE NUMBERS BEFORE IT COMMITS. IT NEVER DELETES. (PO ruling 2026-08-21, option B of three; the third of the five CR-023 decisions.)** The load stage's third write mode had no defined behaviour for records that are ON FILE and NOT IN THE FILE, and that set is the whole of the question: they may be premises the utility genuinely no longer serves, or they may be missing because whoever exported the file filtered by one district and did not notice. Worked: **500 on file · 431 in the file · 69 not in it · 41 of those occupied**. So the run must say, in words, before the commit: *"69 premises are on file and not in this file. 41 have active accounts and will be skipped. 28 will be deactivated."* ⚠ **DELETION IS NOT AVAILABLE AND COULD NOT BE EVEN IF IT WERE RULED:** hard rule 4 forbids losing the premises an issued invoice was written against, so a true mirror would be *"delete 28 and refuse 41"*, which is not a mirror either. ⚠ **THE LABEL MUST STOP PROMISING A REPLACEMENT** — it is *"replace, and retire what is missing"*, because a mode called *"replace the batch"* that leaves 41 rows standing is a control whose name is a lie. This is the SAME shape as rollback (which reverses what a run created and skips units with an active account), deliberately: one sentence for a utility to learn instead of two. ⛔ **SUPERSEDED IN PART — 2026-08-27, CR-042. THE MODE IS NO LONGER OFFERED ANYWHERE.** PO verbatim: *"in all imports - we need 2 option on step 1 insert, insert & update; replace option is not required"*. **What is reversed is the OPTION, not this rule's answer.** The behaviour below — deactivate what the file no longer carries, skip anything occupied, never delete, show all three numbers first — is still the right answer to the question it was asked, and its implementation is UNTOUCHED in `apps/metering/import_runs.py`. It is simply **unreachable**: `WRITE_MODES` is `("insert", "upsert")` and `replace` is refused at the API boundary by name, naming **OQ-580**. ⚠ **The cost of that is real and is stated rather than buried:** with the mode withdrawn, the **28 premises** of this rule's own worked example are retired one at a time by hand, or not at all. That is the question OQ-580 puts back to the PO, with option (c) — a separately-named *"retire premises not in this file"* action outside the import — as the shape that would give the behaviour back without giving back the third mode. **Do not delete this branch** on the strength of it being dead code; a lane that does is destroying a signed capability that one ruling would restore. | CONFIRMED |
| BR-MET-100 | **A UNITS ROW NAMING A PLAN THAT IS NOT ON FILE LOADS ANYWAY, WITHOUT A PLAN, FLAGGED FOR ATTENTION. (PO ruling 2026-08-21, option B of three — the PO OVERRULED the recommendation, which was to reject the row.)** Worked: **246 units · 12 rows naming a missing plan · 1 distinct plan name** — one misspelling, twelve rows. Option A rejected those twelve and named the plan in the rejects list; the operator fixed one spelling and re-ran. The PO took B, so all 246 units exist after one run and the twelve are marked. ⚠ **THE RISK WAS PUT TO THE PO IN PLAIN ENGLISH AND THE PO RULED ANYWAY, SO IT IS RECORDED HERE AS A DECIDED COST, NOT A MISSED ONE: a unit with no plan does not fail — it simply never appears on a bill.** It looks complete on every screen. A rejected row sits in a list somebody has to clear; an unplanned unit is invisible until a customer asks why they were never billed. ⚠ **SO THE FLAG IS NOT DECORATION AND THE RULE IS NOT SATISFIED BY SETTING A COLUMN.** What makes B safe is that the unplanned unit is COUNTED AND FINDABLE: the run receipt states *"12 units loaded with no plan"*, the Units tab carries a filter that lists them, and a unit with no plan renders its missing plan as a visible gap rather than an empty cell. A build that writes the flag and surfaces it nowhere has implemented option C — *"just load it"* — and left the customer un-billed exactly as warned. | CONFIRMED |
| BR-MET-101 | **A COMMON AREA IS A UNIT WITH A FLAG. A MASTER ACCOUNT IS AN ACCOUNT WHOSE PAYER IS THE OWNER. NEITHER NEEDS A NEW TABLE. (PO ruling 2026-08-21, option A of three; the fifth of the five CR-023 decisions.)** Worked: **1408 Elm — 3 units + 2 common areas**; hallway lighting **$41.20** + shared irrigation **$28.40** = **$69.60** on one owner bill. Everything a common area needs already works for units and ONLY for units — meters attach to units, services attach to units, and reads, plans, routes, cycles and bill assembly all key off a unit — so a second table would teach every read path, every route membership and every bill assembler a second shape for no capability we would not already have. r10 agrees with itself twice: its premises tile reads *"3 + common"*, counting them alongside units, and its asset table puts the two shared meters in a row whose Unit column reads *"Common"*. ⚠ **THE COST, ACCEPTED: "unit" now means two things to a reader** — a place someone lives, and a shared space nobody does — so the flag must be visible everywhere it matters, and the flag is what separates a common area from a vacant unit billed to the owner (BR-MET-094), which would otherwise be the same row. ⚠ **TWO THINGS THIS RULING DOES NOT BUY, STATED SO THEY ARE NOT ASSUMED.** (1) *"Unallocated usage 3.1%"* is the gap between a building's master meter and the sum of its unit meters — a computed figure over a PREMISES, not a property of a common area. It needs sub-meter reconciliation, which is its own piece of work, and nothing computes it today. (2) **The master account itself has no home in `customers` yet** — it appears in one backend file and nowhere in the module that owns accounts. Whichever shape was chosen, that is the part still to build. *(STAMPED 2026-08-22 — BUILT: the home is `accounts.kind='master'`, BR-CUS-068, per the PO ruling of 2026-08-22 closing OQ-314's second half. This sentence was true when written and is kept for the record.)* *(STAMPED AGAIN 2026-08-22 — AND THE BILL NOW ADDS UP: `apps/billing/gather.covered_units` prices EVERY common area one master account covers onto ONE document, so this row's `$41.20 + $28.40 = $69.60` comes out of the product end to end (`tests/test_br_cus_068_master_bill.py`). Until that commit the owner's bill carried the ANCHOR area alone — `DEF-571` — and the run reported the other covered area as having nobody to bill — `DEF-572`. Three coverage shapes this rule does not decide REFUSE by name rather than being guessed: `OQ-334` · `OQ-335` · `OQ-336`.)* | CONFIRMED |
| BR-MET-102 | **A RUNNING IMPORT CANNOT BE CANCELLED, AND THE SCREEN DRAWS NO CANCEL — not even a disabled one.** PO ruling 2026-08-22, verbatim: _"No"_, answering the question CR-026's background stages raised (a 100,000-row load now runs for minutes in a worker, so an operator who starts the wrong file cannot stop it). **This is a ruled NON-REQUIREMENT, not an unbuilt feature**, and the distinction decides the UI: `BR-SHL-025`'s *"disabled WITH its reason"* exists for a control that cannot act YET, so drawing a greyed-out Cancel here would invite an operator to wait for something that is never coming. Draw nothing. **The door a wrong file leaves by is the ruled 30-day ROLLBACK** (`BR-MET-090`, `ROLLBACK_DAYS`) — reachable from the stage-5 receipt and from the run table, deactivating what the run loaded and never deleting it. ⚠ **THE ACCEPTED COST, written down so it is not re-discovered as a surprise:** the wrong file's rows are WRITTEN before they can be rolled back, so a 100,000-row mistake is a 100,000-row rollback rather than a stop, and the worker is occupied until the run ends. Both were part of what was ruled on. | PO ruling 2026-08-22 · CR-026 · `BR-MET-090` · `BR-SHL-025` | **CONFIRMED (2026-08-22)** | CR-026's closure walkthrough — assert no cancel control renders |
| BR-MET-103 | **AN AREA IS GEOGRAPHY. A ROUTE IS CREATED AND EDITED IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE — THE ROUTES REGISTRY (SET-05). (PO ruling 2026-08-23, option A of two — OQ-338, the unanswered half of CR-029's D5.)** PO verbatim: _"338 · Areas. A: geography only - option a"_. **What it settles:** an area is a TAG ON A PREMISES (`premises.area_id`, BR-MET-071) naming WHERE that premises is — North · Downtown · East, two levels deep (BR-MET-066b). It is not a grouping of routes, and Settings' **Areas & sub-areas** list (SET-04) holds areas and **nothing route-shaped**: no route column, no route count, no route editing, no second registry. Routes are selected BY area in the route wizard — that direction is unchanged and is the whole of the relationship. **SUPERSEDES the phrase _"districts and routes premises are grouped by"_ wherever it describes this list** — in BR-MET-066's rule text, in `settings.reference.sample.areas` (all eleven catalogues) and in `AreaLookupHandler`'s docstring; each was corrected under CR-029 RC-W6b, dated, rather than left to read as a second owner. **What was shown to the PO:** the worked case _"today a premises tagged North joins North's routes automatically — should Settings' Areas list also let someone edit routes there, or is it geography only?"_ and the consequence of the alternative (r10's duplicate registry put one premises on Route 3 and Route 1 at once). ⚠ **Nothing changes in behaviour — the code already worked this way.** The ruling makes it law, and it unblocks CR-029 RC-W5-T1's naming sweep, which stubbed at exactly this boundary | PO ruling 2026-08-23 (OQ-338, CLOSED-RULED) · CR-029 D5's unanswered half · B17 §5 gap G8 · supersedes the route half of BR-MET-066's rule text | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr029_rcw5_naming.py::test_oq338__no_reference_list_is_a_second_routes_registry` · `…::test_oq338__an_area_row_publishes_nothing_route_shaped` · `…::test_oq338__the_areas_list_refuses_a_route_field_rather_than_storing_it` · `07-frontend/src/features/tenancy/RoutesCyclesRulings.test.tsx` (SET-04 renders nothing route-shaped) |
| BR-MET-104 | **A FLAT SERVICE'S PRICING CHARACTERISTIC LIVES ON THE UNIT (PO ruling 2026-08-24, closing OQ-348).** Trash, storm water and broadband do not bill on consumption — they bill on a **characteristic**: container count and size, impervious area, speed tier. Nothing stored any of them. The PO ruled *"(a) a characteristic on the service point - we ahve it on unit"*, and `metering.Unit.square_feet` is the precedent being pointed at: a physical fact about the unit that pricing reads. So the characteristic is a **unit-level** fact, not a service-point-level one. ⚠ **ONE CONSEQUENCE IS FLAGGED AND DELIBERATELY NOT ANSWERED HERE, because this ruling fixes WHERE the number lives and not HOW MANY there are:** a single unit may take trash AND storm water AND broadband at once, and those are three different characteristics with three different units of measure. Unit-level therefore means either three (or ten) columns on `units`, or a value table keyed by unit-and-service. `square_feet` is one column because there is one of it; the flat services are not. **W6 owns that shape and must settle it before it writes a migration** — a column-per-service on `units` is a schema that grows with the service catalogue, which is the thing B2 §2's reviewed-interface rule exists to make somebody think about. | PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-348) · B2 §2 · `metering.Unit.square_feet` as precedent | CONFIRMED | W6 |
| BR-MET-105 | **BASE CHARGES ON AN EMPTY UNIT ACCRUE TO THE OWNER / LANDLORD (PO ruling 2026-08-24, closing OQ-350).** Worked figure as put to the PO: **$30.50/month** accruing against no account when a unit sits empty between tenants. The PO ruled *"(a) the owner/landlord"*, closing a deferral BR-MET-069b made deliberately and in the PO's own earlier words — *"later we will connect them to Unit or somewhere else and write rule"*. **This is later.** Consequences to build against, none of them assumed beyond the ruling: the five seeded `occupancy_statuses`, consumed by nothing until now, become load-bearing; the owner must be reachable as a payer, which is what CR-030 W0's `premises.owner_customer_id` FK exists for; and the charge is a real receivable against a real party, so it posts like any other — balanced pair, ledger service only, `audit_log` on every money action. ⚠ **HALF-CLOSED 2026-08-31 AT THIS SENTENCE — THE CHARGE IS LEVIED PER UNIT.** PO ruling of 2026-08-31 (`CR-056`), worked: **six empty flats at `$30.50` bill `$183.00` to the owner, not `$30.50`** — a standing charge pays for a CONNECTION, six flats are six connections, and the owner's document carries **one line per empty unit naming the unit it is for**, never one line of `$183.00` he cannot check. Nothing about the price changes when a flat empties: same meter, same tariff, same plan, only the name on the bill. ⚠ **THE OTHER HALF IS STILL NOT RULED AND STILL MUST NOT BE ASSUMED:** ~~whether the owner is billed per empty unit or once per premises, and~~ what happens in the gap before an owner customer is linked. Raise that before writing the biller, not after. ⚠ And the SAME-SHAPED question about COMMON PARTS — two shared spaces taking one service, standing charge once or twice — is **not** decided by this and stays open as `OQ-334`. | PO 2026-08-24 (OQ-350) · **PO 2026-08-31 (`CR-056`, per unit — `$183.00` on six flats)** · BR-MET-069b (deferral closed) · BR-CUS-068 · CR-030 W0 | **CONFIRMED — and the per-unit/per-premises half is now RULED (2026-08-31)** | W7 |
| BR-MET-106 | **A MONTH'S USAGE PER ACCOUNT PER SERVICE IS COMPUTED FROM METER READS, STORED, AND DISPLAYED (PO ruling 2026-08-24, CR-030 W3 task 3.8).** PO verbatim: *"based on meter reading calculate monthly useage and save them and display them"*. The figure is `Σ MeterRead.consumption` over the reads whose `read_at` falls in the calendar month, grouped by account and service, **carrying its unit explicitly** (hard rule 3 — kWh, kgal and therms are never added together and a stored quantity without its unit is unreadable). ⚠ **THIS IS THE OPPOSITE CHOICE FROM BR-CUS-016's AGEING, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE WHOLE JUSTIFICATION — do not read this as licence to store other derived figures.** Ageing is a function of **today**: it changes with no new data, so a stored ageing is wrong the morning after it is written, which is exactly why BR-CUS-016 says *never stored*. A month's usage is a function of **that month's reads only**: once the month is closed and its reads are in, the figure cannot drift on its own, and storing it is what turns a 24-month chart into one indexed read instead of a scan across a monthly-partitioned read table (BR-MET-055). **Corrections RESTATE, they never edit:** a superseded or late read (BR-MET-051's lineage shape) writes a NEW monthly row for that month, carrying `computed_at` and the read-lineage heads it summed, and the chart reads the latest — the same supersede discipline the reads themselves use, for the same reason. **An unread month is ABSENT, never `0`:** zero usage and no read are different claims about a customer, and the account-detail Usage tab states the absence rather than drawing a zero bar (hard-won law 1). **Estimates count and are marked** — BR-MET-028 estimates carry consumption with no `current_value`, so the month includes them and records that it did, because a customer looking at a 628 kWh month is entitled to know part of it was estimated. | PO 2026-08-24 (CR-030 W3 T3.8) · BR-MET-028 · BR-MET-051 · BR-MET-055 · contrast BR-CUS-016 | CONFIRMED | W3 |
| BR-MET-105a | **THE OWNER IS A REAL PARTY — `BR-MET-105` IS NOW BUILDABLE (PO 2026-08-24, closing DEF-650).** PO verbatim: *"Make the owner a real party"*. Both were flagged when BR-MET-105 was written and neither is answered here; until they are, the accrual is measured and reported, never silently posted to a guessed party. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-650) · BR-MET-105 · BR-CUS-083 · `premises.owner_customer_id` (W0) | CONFIRMED (the owner is a party) / **OPEN (per-unit vs per-premises · the unlinked case)** | W7 task 7.15 |
| BR-MET-107 | **AN UNBANKED EXPORT CREDIT IS APPLIED TO THE FINAL BILL, NOT FORFEITED (PO 2026-08-24, closing DEF-654).** PO verbatim: *"apply it to the final bill"*. Where a customer closes an account holding export credit their own generation earned and the utility has not yet paid out, that credit **reduces what they owe at closure**; only what remains after it follows the ordinary settlement path (deposit, then BR-CUS-081/096). **Worked, the case put to the PO:** `M-4023 · Net meter · electric` holds **$4.10** of unbanked credit at close. ⚠ **THE PROTOTYPE'S OWN SENTENCE IS WITHDRAWN BY THIS RULING** — `05-prototype` r10's move-out step 5 reads *"export credit stops accruing, unbanked credit of $4.10 is forfeited on close"*, and `forfeited` is exactly what the PO rejected. It was a confiscation of customer-generated value stated in an asset-disposition table cell, for a whole account class: the platform seeds both Solar and Battery, so any such account closing mid-cycle has an unbanked balance. ⚠ **WHAT THIS RULING DOES NOT SETTLE, AND MUST NOT BE ASSUMED:** what happens when the credit **EXCEEDS** the final bill. The ruling says where it goes FIRST, not whether the remainder is refundable, carried to a next account, or held. Raised again when W5 builds the settlement rather than inferred from *"apply it to the final bill"*. | PO 2026-08-24 (DEF-654) · B19 §15.10 · BR-CUS-025 | CONFIRMED (application) / **OPEN (the remainder when it exceeds the bill)** | W5 |
| BR-MET-108 | **A FLAT SERVICE'S PRICING CHARACTERISTIC IS A ROW IN `service_characteristics`, KEYED BY UNIT AND SERVICE — NOT A COLUMN ON `units` (CR-030 W6 task 6.9a, decided 2026-08-25).** `BR-MET-104` fixed WHERE the number lives (the unit) and explicitly did not fix HOW MANY there are; it named this decision and assigned it to W6 *before a migration is written*. Shape: `unit_id · service · key · value · unit_of_measure`, tenant-scoped like every metering table, one row per characteristic. **⚠ THE `square_feet` PRECEDENT WAS EXAMINED AND IT DOES NOT CARRY — this is the whole argument.** `metering.Unit.square_feet` is a fact about the UNIT: true whether the unit takes trash, storm water, both or neither. *"Three 96-gallon containers"* is a fact about the unit's **relationship to the trash service**. Those are different kinds of fact, so a column that works for the first does not generalise to the second, however similar they look on a form. **THE DECIDING ARGUMENT IS ABOUT MONEY, NOT TIDINESS: A NULL COLUMN CANNOT SAY WHICH KIND OF NOTHING IT IS.** Worked, on a 25-unit building where 20 units take trash + storm water + broadband and 5 take trash only. **Column-per-service:** `units` gains at least four columns (trash needs TWO — container count and size), and the 5 trash-only units carry NULL in three of them. That NULL means either *"does not take this service"* or *"takes it and nobody recorded the characteristic"* — **and those two price differently.** The first should bill nothing; the second is an unpriceable bill that must refuse. A single nullable column cannot distinguish them, so the engine either prices an unrecorded impervious area at zero (silently under-billing every one of those units, every month) or refuses service a customer is entitled to. **Row-per-characteristic:** a missing row is unambiguously *"no characteristic recorded"*, and whether the service is taken is answered where it already lives — the tenant's service list — so the engine can refuse the second case by name and bill nothing for the first. Under this shape that building holds `20 × 3 + 5 × 1 = 65` rows. **AND IT KEEPS THE UNIT OF MEASURE IN A COLUMN RATHER THAN IN A COLUMN NAME.** Container count, impervious square feet and a speed tier are three different UoMs. Column-per-service encodes each UoM in its identifier (`storm_impervious_sqft`), which nothing can validate, compare or migrate; `unit_of_measure` as data is the same discipline hard rule 3 already imposes on consumption — *never assume the unit*. **THIRD, THE CROSS-MODULE ARGUMENT (B2 §2).** Rating must read these to price. Under column-per-service, a rating rule hardcodes metering's column NAME per service, so adding the eleventh service edits rating; under a keyed table, rating reads `(unit, service)` and the interface is stable across the catalogue. B2 §2's reviewed-interface rule exists to make somebody think about exactly this, and BR-MET-104 said so. ⚠ **THE SHAPE STANDS; THE REFUSAL IS REVERSED (PO 2026-08-25).** This rule fixed the SHAPE and left three things open — the characteristic KEYS, their UoMs, and what happens when a required one is absent. **Two of the three are now ruled.** ✅ **HOW MANY: any number, named, per service.** PO verbatim: *"Any number of named characteristics per service — trash stores count=3 and size=96 gal"*. Two rows for trash; the shape already allowed it and nothing changes. ⛔ **AND AN ABSENT CHARACTERISTIC IS NOW BILLED AS ZERO, NOT REFUSED.** PO verbatim: *"Bill it as zero"*. **This reverses this rule's own closing clause**, which read *"A service whose characteristic is required and absent must REFUSE with the absence named, never price it at zero"* — and it reverses the argument that chose rows over columns, that a blank *"means either 'does not take this service' or 'takes it and nobody recorded the number', and those two price differently"*. ⚠ **NO RE-SIGN IS NEEDED AND THAT IS NOT A LOOPHOLE: this rule is AI-AUTHORED, CONFIRMED and HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING**, written by the build on 2026-08-25 under the PO's 2026-08-09 ruling *"AI will do it; we will review it later as human."* **This IS that review, arriving and ruling differently** — the protocol working as designed. A ❄️ FROZEN B4/B5 figure would have needed HUMAN-GUIDE §6; an unratified rule the build wrote does not. **Worked, so the cost is on the record:** 25 units, 24 carrying a container count, unit 12 blank. The run **completes**, unit 12's trash line is **$0.00**, nothing errors, the customer pays it, and the shortfall recurs every cycle until somebody notices by other means. **The build raised this objection once, in writing, before the ruling; the PO chose it; it is built as ruled and not re-argued.** ⚠ **BILLING ZERO IS THE RULING — BILLING ZERO *SILENTLY* IS NOT.** The ruling asks for a figure, not for concealment, and hard rule 7 already obliges a money path to record what it did. **The absence is stamped on the line and surfaced**, so a utility can list every unit billed at zero for want of a number. That is implementing the ruling, not softening it. **Still NOT ruled, and still not for a lane to invent:** which characteristic KEYS each service has and their units of measure. ⚠ **AI-AUTHORED SCHEMA ON A 🔒 PATH (migration ordering).** Recorded under the PO's 2026-08-09 ruling (*"AI will do it; we will review it later as human"*) with a row in `12-governance/09-human-review-ledger.md`; it is CONFIRMED as the build's working shape and **not** ratified. | CR-030 W6 task 6.9a · BR-MET-104 (PO 2026-08-24, OQ-348) · B2 §2 · B18 §5.4.6 · hard rule 3 · `metering.Unit.square_feet` (precedent examined and rejected) | CONFIRMED (shape) · **HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** | W6 |
| BR-MET-109 | **WORK-ORDER TEMPLATES ARE THE UTILITY'S OWN, AND BATCHING IS THEIR CHOICE — NOT AN ENGINE RULE (PO 2026-08-25).** PO verbatim: *"We are making it flexible ; utility users can issue as many work order they want to execute this job; we will give them ready to use tempaltes - they will develope these templats in work order section; then they can add as many they want - they can do single or group"*. ⚠ **THIS CHANGES WHAT B18 TASK 6.4 IS.** B18 frames it as batching LOGIC — *"scope per-premise/per-unit/per-asset; 25 units → 7 orders; the split control"* — as though the system computes the grouping. **It does not.** The utility authors templates in the Work order section and issues however many orders it wants, single or grouped. **Worked:** `CON-26-0117`'s **5 of 7 orders across 25 units** is an OUTCOME of that tenant's templates, **not a figure the engine derives.** A different utility with different templates gets a different number for the identical build, and both are correct. **So `work_order_templates` is a TENANT-AUTHORED CATALOGUE** — the same shape as the document checklist (BR-CUS-095) and discount programmes — and we ship ready-to-use ones as a starting point, which the utility edits. ✅ The drawn `Split into individual orders` control and the footer *"5 orders for a single unit — nothing to batch"* stay true as **affordances**, not as a computed policy. | PO 2026-08-25 · B18 task 6.4 (reframed) · §5.4.9 · BR-CUS-095 (the same tenant-authored pattern) | CONFIRMED | W6 |
| BR-MET-110 | **THE FIELD CONFIRMS, A HUMAN ACCEPTS, AND ONLY THEN DOES SERVICE GO LIVE (PO 2026-08-25).** ⊕ **AMENDED 2026-10-02 BY `CR-162-BRC10` (PO, Q2 and the approval items): THE BUILD IS APPROVED ONE UNIT AND SERVICE AT A TIME, AND A GATE HOLDS ITS UNIT.** On the request's Units tab each unit × service row whose own work orders are complete gets *Activate*, with *Activate selected* and *Activate all ready*; the header's *Approve* opens the same dialog over everything not yet active. Before anything is written the dialog lists what is ready, what a gate holds and why, and every open order on the build (*"2 of 4 not complete: WO-0055, WO-0056"*) with *Activate what is ready* / *Cancel*; open to-dos are listed and never block. **A gate inspection (`is_gate`, stamped from its template) that has not passed — not complete, or complete with a failing outcome — BLOCKS its unit** (a whole-build gate blocks every unit of its service); every other open order only warns, and an order raised for the whole build never holds a unit back. On activation, in one transaction per press: the service row → Activated through `transition_service` (the one write path); its assets → Installed, dated (the assets module's own `install_build_assets`, `DEF-AST-5443`); the unit reads Active when all its services are; on the FIRST activation the premises (`pending` → `active`) and the owner's master account (Pending → Active) — its customer reads Active through it; the request → *Partly activated* / *Activated*; the messages go (`BR-MET-115`). Idempotent and audited. A build that is a draft, cancelled or on hold cannot activate. **A move-in before approval is ALLOWED (PO, ruled B: *"allow it. The account opens, but billing waits until the unit is activated"*):** the days between the move-in and the unit's activation are dark days in `BR-CUS-103`'s sense (`customers.activation_windows`), so B4.3's frozen factor charges nothing for them; the account header says *Billing starts once the unit activates* and links to the request; cancelling the build lists the accounts still open on its units and closes none. PO verbatim: *"Work order will send the data and confirm from field; utility staff will review these work order , accept them and use data from them to activate these services or confirm the installation is completed"*. **Three steps, and the middle one is the rule:** the field returns DATA on the order → **a member of staff REVIEWS AND ACCEPTS it** → the accepted data is what activates the service or confirms the installation. ⚠ **THIS REFINES RATHER THAN CONTRADICTS THE HISTORY TAB'S *"Electric energised — gate WO-4904 passed"*, attributed to `System`.** The system does energise and the gate does cause it — but **the gate passes because a person accepted the field's data**, not because the field reported directly. `is_gate` and `gate_order_id` stand exactly as built (BR-MET-108's siblings); what was missing is the acceptance step and the data it accepts. ⚠ **AND THE SCHEMA CANNOT HOLD IT YET.** `work_orders` carries `outcome`, `external_ref`, `completed_at` and `attachment_ref` — enough to say an order FINISHED, **not enough to hold what the field sent, who reviewed it, or when it was accepted.** Owed. **The acceptance is the part that must be auditable**, because it is the human act standing between a field report and a customer's supply going live — and hard rule 7's discipline applies to it for the same reason it applies to money. | PO 2026-08-25 · B18 §5.4.9 (the History tab's `System` line) · BR-MET-108 (`gate_order_id`) · task 6.8 | CONFIRMED · **schema owed** | W6 |
| BR-MET-111 | **THE OWNER OF A NEW BUILD BECOMES A CUSTOMER RECORD, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THEM BILLABLE — drawn on the New Connect wizard, captured 2026-08-25.** Step 1's panel carries the badge **`Becomes a customer`** and states the mechanism verbatim: *"These details create a customer record, which is what makes the owner **billable at step 7**. So the score is: mechanism ✅ · column ❌ · backfill ❌. **DEF-650 stays OPEN on the last two**, and a lane must not read this rule as closing it. ⚠ **BR-MET-105's charge therefore posts only where the owner is a customer**, and refuses by name where they are a string — never to a free-text owner, and never silently to nobody. | B18 §5.4.10 (the PO's wizard screens, 2026-08-25) · DEF-650 · BR-MET-105 · BR-MET-105a (the absent column) · B19 §4 (the shared owner-type set) | CONFIRMED (the mechanism) · **DEF-650 OPEN on the column and the backfill** | W6 |

| BR-MET-113 | **THE OWNER GETS AN ACCOUNT NUMBER AT CONFIRM, AND EVERY CONNECTION CHARGE IS TAGGED TO IT (PO 2026-08-25, closing OQ-367).** PO verbatim: *"You will have to assign account number to owner and tag everything to account number"*. ⚠ **THIS IS OPTION (B) OF THE THREE OQ-367 PUT, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT KEEPS EVERY EXISTING MONEY RULE INTACT.** The New Connect wizard's step 7 offers three debtors for the quote — *the connection itself* · *the customer created at step 1* · *each unit's first bill* — and only the middle one named a party that exists at confirm time.
