A charge floating on a `connections` row would have needed a second, parallel money path — a *"payable"* that is not an invoice, settled by a payment that references no account — and every rule that says *"the account"* would have had a silent exception. **There is now one money path.** ✅ **AND IT ANSWERS `DEF-678` TOO:** NCT-03.9 says the quote is *"settled before energisation"*, and `derive_connection_state()` had nothing to read a settlement FROM. It can now read the owner account's balance for those charges, which is what makes the precondition implementable rather than merely stated. ⚠ **WHAT THE RULING DOES NOT SAY, AND MUST NOT BE ASSUMED:** which **account type/class** the owner's account is (a builder's account is not a residential one, and BR-CUS-083 already bills a developer via a **master** account — whether this IS that master, or a sibling, is unstated); whether the wizard's other two "billed to" options survive as ways of **re-tagging** the same charge to a different account, or are simply gone; and what happens to that account when the build completes and the units are sold. **Raised when W6 task 6.10 builds the orchestrator, not inferred.** | PO 2026-08-25 (OQ-367) · B18 §5.4.10 (`NCT-03.7` Billed to, `NCT-03.9`) · BR-MET-111 · BR-CUS-083 · DEF-678 · B4 ❄️ | CONFIRMED (the account and the tagging) / **OPEN (account class · the other two options · the account's life after the build)** | W6 |
| BR-MET-114 | **THE NEW CONNECT "OUTSTANDING WORK" LIST IS MARKED BY A HUMAN, NOT DERIVED (PO 2026-08-25, closing OQ-369).** ⊕ **AMENDED 2026-10-02 BY `CR-162-BRC10` (PO, Q3, and *"in to do also keep notes - allow user to add notes"*): THE LIST LIVES ON THE REQUEST'S OWN *TO-DO* TAB, AND IT HOLDS NOTES TOO.** *Mark / Close outstanding* leave the More menu; the To-do tab lists to-dos and notes together, newest first, each marked *To-do* or *Note*, with who wrote it and when; *+ Add a to-do*, *+ Add a note*, per-row *Mark done* and *Reopen*; a note is never done and blocks nothing, and only its author may edit it (audited, never deleted — the account notes' convention). The Overview says *"To-dos open: 2"*. Stored as `connection_todos.kind = 'note'` (`metering/0065`) because a single-family build has no account to hang an account note on. *Release* reads *Resume*. Open to-dos are LISTED on the Approve warning and never block activation. PO verbatim: *"human will mark everything to start with"*. Step 9 of the wizard (`NCT-03.9`) draws three items under **`Outstanding work`** with the load-bearing line *"3 things to finish — **none of them block creating the connection**"*: **Meter numbers** (*"50 meters still read 'to assign'"*) · **Storm water figure** (*"Impervious area recorded, but no assessment date"*) · **Sewer on common area** (*"Left off, because common area has no water service"*). OQ-369 asked whether that list is recomputed from the created rows or stored; the ruling is **stored, and authored by a person**. ✅ **THE THIRD ITEM IS WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT ANSWER AND NOT MERELY THE EASY ONE.** Items 1 and 2 are ABSENCES a query could recompute. Item 3 — *"Left off, **because** common area has no water service"* — is a **DECISION SOMEBODY MADE AND A REASON THEY GAVE**, and no derivation can reconstruct a reason. A pure derivation would also re-raise item 3 forever, since the sewer service genuinely is absent and always will be. **A human marking the list can say "we chose this, here is why", and that is the thing worth keeping.** ⚠ **"TO START WITH" IS PART OF THE RULING AND IS RECORDED AS SUCH.** The PO scoped it: humans mark it **now**. Deriving candidate items later — the system SUGGESTING *"50 meters have no number"* and a person accepting, amending or dismissing each — is not excluded, and is the obvious next step once the list has been used. **What is NOT permitted is the system silently closing an item a human opened**, because that is the reason half of item 3 disappearing again. **Shape this implies:** a row per item carrying its own text, its author, when it was marked and whether it is done — tenant-scoped, and **explicitly NOT a blocker**: the connection is created with the list outstanding, which is the wizard's whole stated behaviour. ⚠ **WHERE IT IS READ AFTER CONFIRM IS STILL NOT DRAWN** — `NCT-02.overview` has no such panel — so the detail screen that shows it is a design gap, not an implementation choice. | PO 2026-08-25 (OQ-369) · B18 §5.4.10 (`NCT-03.9` Outstanding work) · `NCT-02.overview` (no panel drawn) | CONFIRMED (human-marked and stored) / **OPEN (the surface that shows it after confirm)** | W6 |
| BR-MET-115 | **CUSTOMER COMMUNICATION FIRES WHEN A HUMAN MARKS THE JOB COMPLETE — NOT WHEN THE CONNECTION IS CREATED (PO 2026-08-25, closing OQ-368).** ⊕ **AMENDED 2026-10-02 BY `CR-162-BRC10` (PO, Q8: *"messages fire on approval"*): APPROVING THE BUILD IS THAT HUMAN ACT TOO.** The first activation fires step 5's messages the clerk asked for that were not already queued at Create (never one twice), and every activation fires the *service live* letter for the units and services it switched on — inside the activation's one transaction, through the one queue door. Today the platform still has no `connection` source kind (`OQ-376`), so each reports *not sent* with the platform's own sentence: the trigger is built, the queue refuses by name. Moving the Create-time queueing of the three step-5 messages to approval on the wizard and Messages tab is the Messages lanes' (NC-B / NC-C). PO verbatim: *"dont keep it at the time of new conenct creation , remove it; and sent it whnr utility user mar that job complete traigger it tat that time"*. ⚠ **THE READING THIS RULE IS BUILT ON, STATED PLAINLY SO ONE WORD CAN CORRECT IT:** *"remove it"* is taken to mean **the `On creation` trigger goes away**, and every piece of New Connect communication now fires from **one** event — a utility user marking the job complete. If the PO meant only the third piece, this rule narrows to that piece and nothing else changes. ✅ **NONE OF OQ-368's THREE OPTIONS WAS CHOSEN, AND THE ANSWER IS BETTER THAN ALL OF THEM.** The question offered (A) the system sends itself the moment the last job is signed off · (B) an outbox the system drains · (C) a person presses send. **The PO gave the trigger instead of the mechanism: the human act that already exists — marking the job complete — IS the send.** So there is **no subscription table, no poller, no outbox and no state-change listener**: the send happens inside the same transaction as the completion, and a letter cannot go out for work nobody signed off. ✅ **AND IT LANDS EXACTLY ON `BR-MET-110`, RULED THE SAME DAY:** *"Work order will send the data and confirm from field; utility staff will review these work order, accept them and use data from them to activate these services."* That human acceptance was already the thing standing between a field report and a customer's supply going live. **It is now also the thing that tells the customer.** One human act, one auditable moment, three consequences — data accepted, service activated, customer told. ⚠ **WHY *"ON CREATION"* DESERVED TO GO, RECORDED BECAUSE THE SCREEN STILL SHOWS IT:** a connection record being created is an **internal** event. For a 26-unit build the customer is a developer who already knows they applied; *"we have created your connection"* tells them nothing they did not know, three weeks before anything happens at the address. *"Your service is on"* is the event that matters, and it is the one a person can vouch for. ⛔ **THE DRAWN SCREEN IS NOW STALE** — `NCT-03.8` draws three pieces of which **two** carry `On creation`. The ruling removes that trigger; the screen has not been redrawn. **The rule wins over the drawing** (CLAUDE.md: where a screen contradicts a ruling, file it — and this is the reverse direction, a ruling superseding a screen, which is the same discipline). → **DEF-679**. ⚠ **WHAT IS NOT RULED, AND MUST NOT BE GUESSED: *WHICH* JOB.** `CON-26-0117` carries **7 work orders across 25 units**; `CON-26-0110` carries 5. Marking order 3 of 7 complete must NOT send *"your service is live"*. Three readings and no evidence between them: **(a)** the **gate** order for that service (`is_gate`/`gate_order_id`, already built) · **(b)** the **last** order on the connection, whichever it is · **(c)** the utility picks, per template. **Until it is ruled, the trigger is wired to nothing**: the completion path records that a send is due and names no piece, and `NotImplementedError("OQ-372")` guards the dispatch. **A letter sent three weeks early is not recoverable by an amendment.** | PO 2026-08-25 (OQ-368) · BR-MET-110 (the same human act) · B18 §5.4.10 `NCT-03.8` (now stale) · DEF-679 · OQ-372 | CONFIRMED (the trigger is the human completion) / **OPEN (which job fires it — OQ-372)** | W6 |
| BR-MET-116 | **THE OWNER'S BUILD ACCOUNT IS A `kind='master'` ACCOUNT, ANCHORED ON THE PREMISES' COMMON AREA (PO 2026-08-25, closing OQ-373).** ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-10-02 BY `CR-162-BRC10` (PO, Q1): THE PREMISES TYPE DECIDES WHETHER A BUILD RAISES ONE, AND IT IS PENDING UNTIL APPROVAL.** PO verbatim: *"Q1–Q8 as recommended, go"*; *"premises is not yet active - it will be activated at the time of approval of new conenct request"*; *"we are creating master acocunt on Premsies and not on unit that is out rule"*. A build on a premises whose type's *Account type* is Master opens the owner's master account (and the owner customer) even with ONE unit entered; a Regular type (single-family) opens none, and the applicant gets a regular account through Move-in. The premises the build creates is born `pending`; the master account is held Pending after the build's charges are on the books, and both become Active on the build's first activation (`metering.connection_activation`). The wizard reads the type's answer (`newConnectDraft.buildOpensOwnerMaster`), and only a type nobody has answered falls back to the two-home count. ⛔ **NARROWED 2026-10-02 BY `BR-CUS-1114` (PO ruling, decision log top row): A SINGLE-UNIT BUILD RAISES NO OWNER'S MASTER ACCOUNT.** The 2026-09-21 sentence *"a house or a duplex does get one"* now holds for the duplex (two units) and NOT for the house: `create_owner_account` reaches `create_master_account`, which refuses a premises of fewer than two units (`customers.account.master_needs_two_units`), and New Connect sends no owner account for a one-unit build (`newConnectDraft.buildOpensOwnerMaster`). The applicant gets a REGULAR account on the one unit through Move-in. PO verbatim: *"it will be master acocunt ; unit will be all vacant right now, if there is common area you can add that in premises"*. ⚠ **THE MECHANISM WAS ALREADY BUILT AND THE QUESTION SHOULD NOT HAVE NEEDED ASKING — recorded because the miss is worth more than the answer.** `BR-CUS-068` (PO 2026-08-22) had already made a master account **an ordinary `accounts` row with `kind='master'`** that **anchors on the premises' first common-area unit** and covers the rest through `service_agreements`; `BR-CUS-083` (PO 2026-08-24, closing OQ-349) had already ruled that **connection charges on a multi-unit build are invoiced to the developer through exactly that master account**, worked at `CON-26-0117` · 25 units · **$14,390.00**. OQ-373 was raised because `accounts.unit_id` is NOT NULL and a build account seemed to have no unit — **but the master row satisfies that column with the common-area unit and still bills for the whole building** (`1408 Elm`, `$41.20 + $28.40 = $69.60`, end to end in the product). **All three options OQ-373 offered were wrong for the same reason: they solved a problem with a signed answer two modules away.** ⛔ **An r11 step 0 miss — the customers packet was not read before the question was written** — the same failure `DEF-652` recorded, and the one the PO named *"today's most expensive miss"*. → **DEF-682**. ✅ **WHAT THE RULING ADDS THAT THE EARLIER TWO DID NOT SAY.** **(1) *"unit will be all vacant right now"*** — at confirm **no unit is occupied**, so `BR-CUS-017`'s one-occupying-account guard cannot bite and the master path's per-unit refusals are all satisfiable. That is a real fact about New Connect that neither BR-CUS-068 nor BR-CUS-083 states: they describe a master account over a **running** building; this one is created over an **empty** one. **(2) *"if there is common area you can add that in premises"*** — the common area is recorded as part of the premises' unit set, which is `BR-MET-101` exactly (*a common area IS a unit with a flag*), and it is what gives the master its anchor. So the wizard's step 4 must offer a common area on a multi-unit build, and the drawn manifest's **`Units 26` = 25 apartments + 1 common area** is that offer already taken. ⚠ **ONE CASE REMAINS AND IT IS NOT A BLOCKER:** a build with **no** common area (a house, a duplex) has no anchor unit — but it has a real unit whose ordinary account can carry the charge, and `BR-CUS-083` is scoped to **multi-unit** builds. `tag_charges_to_account()` already covers it. ~~**A single-unit build therefore does NOT get a master account**, and that is a boundary, not an omission.~~ ⚠⚠ **THAT CLAUSE IS SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21 — PO, verbatim: *"common area is option"*.** **A build with NO common area commits, and the owner's master account is still created.** The refusal it described (`metering.connection_no_common_area_for_master`) was the second half of the pincer that refused all eight browser builds: with a common area the build was refused for the area having no plan, and without one it was refused here. **Where the account anchors is the PREMISES**, and nothing new was built to make that true — `BR-CUS-1010` moved the anchor to the address on 2026-09-01, so `accounts.unit_id` is NULL and `accounts.premises_id` is set on a build with common areas and on a build without, identically. **The common areas were never the anchor; they were the COVERAGE**, and with none the account covers nothing and bills nothing (`CR-056` W4's *no lines, no document*). ⚠ **ANCHORING ON A DWELLING WAS MEASURED AND REJECTED:** it needs an answer to *which* dwelling, it puts the developer's coverage on a unit a resident's own account must later occupy (`BR-CUS-017` would then refuse the first move-in), and `covered_unit_ids` would bill the developer for a dwelling, which `BR-CUS-083` never said. **The premises costs zero new lines in `customers`** — `_master_attachment_units` already answers `[]` for a premises with nothing shared (`CR-056` W1 task 9). The money is unmoved: `1408 Elm` still bills `$41.20 + $28.40 = $69.60`, and a build with no shared space bills `$0.00` on no document. ⚠ **STILL NOT RULED:** what becomes of the master account once the build finishes and the units are sold — it holds no service and no meter of its own beyond the common areas, and nothing says whether it closes, stays open, or becomes the landlord's. Raised when it is needed; not inferred from this ruling. ⚠ **AND BUILDING IT FOUND AN ORDERING CONSTRAINT ON STEP 9's COMMIT THAT NO SCREEN SHOWS.** `create_master_account` creates the account **ACTIVE** — BR-CUS-068 made that deliberate, because an unattached master is exactly the half-built state that ruling closed — and **activating an account on a unit with no rate plan is refused by `BR-MET-040`**, correctly, since an active account on an unpriced unit is an unbillable bill waiting to happen. **So the owner's master account CANNOT be created before the common area has a plan.** The drawn manifest lists `Plan assignments 26` and (once this rule lands) one account; **the plan assignments must be written FIRST**. That is a real constraint on task 6.10's orchestrator, found by building it rather than by reading the screen, and it is the kind of thing the five-module commit has to get right the first time. | PO 2026-08-25 (OQ-373) · **BR-CUS-068** · **BR-CUS-083** · BR-MET-101 · BR-MET-113 · BR-CUS-017 · BR-MET-040 · DEF-682 | CONFIRMED · **the mechanism pre-existed** | W6 |
| BR-MET-117 | **AN IMPORTED DATE IS MATCHED AGAINST THE FORMAT THE UTILITY SELECTED, AND A MISMATCH SAYS SO IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (PO 2026-08-26, CR-036 (a)).** PO verbatim: *"when we match the date  wehave to compare it date format that utility has selected. the format should match otherwise it show the error saying - you setting says this data format and you have given differnt and it does not match"*. **(1)** The importer reads the tenant's own S8 Regional setting (`tenant_settings.date_format`, BR-TEN-020, set at provisioning) and matches the cell against THAT. It never sniffs the string, never falls back to a locale and never tries formats until one parses — **a format that "works" is not the format that was meant.** **(2)** A mismatch is an ERROR naming the DECLARED format and the value AS GIVEN, in one sentence. ⚠ This SUPERSEDES the message the PO hit — *"1/1/2026 is not a date this import can read"*, fix *"Use YYYY-MM-DD"* — which was wrong twice over: it implied no format works, and it named a format the tenant never chose. **Why it is worth a rule: `3/4/2026` is 4 March in the US and 3 April almost everywhere else, and NEITHER READING ERRORS.** On the PO's own 9,881-row file (`1/1/2026 … 1/10/2026`) only the first row reads the same both ways; by the tenth the two readings are NINE MONTHS apart, and if the column feeds a service start that is a first bill prorated from the wrong month on 9,881 units. ⚠ **NOT SETTLED BY THE RULING:** what happens when a tenant has selected no format. Provisioning defaults it, so it should be unreachable; under (1) an unset format has no authority to match against, so the safe behaviour is to REFUSE and say so, never to pick one — that is the consequence, not the PO's words, and it is flagged rather than smuggled. **(3) THE FORMAT IS SHOWN ON *TRANSFORM*, AND A MISMATCH IS RAISED THERE — NOT DEFERRED TO VALIDATE (PO 2026-08-26, same session, verbatim: *"or when date matching happen at transform level - show the format - and in case any issue highlight it there itself"*).** The mapping stage is where an operator says what each column IS, so it is where they must be told what a date column will be READ AS — the declared format printed beside it, and a value that does not match highlighted on that stage. **This is the same principle as CR-036 (g)'s write-mode move and it is worth naming once: a decision or a contradiction belongs at the stage where the operator can still act on it cheaply.** The PO met the alternative — 9,881 rows mapped, `Matched` on every column, and the date problem revealed one whole stage later as 9,881 identical rejects. A screen that says `Matched` about a column it cannot actually read is the defect; the reject list is only where it surfaced. | PO 2026-08-26 (CR-036 (a)) · BR-TEN-020 | CONFIRMED | CR-036 |
| BR-MET-118 | ⛔ **WITHDRAWN 2026-08-26, THE SAME DAY IT WAS WRITTEN, BY THE PO — verbatim: *"I think - lets stay with rule premises services = Unit services = we will not change it yet"* and *"so we dont need CR37"*. THE RULE IN FORCE IS: A UNIT'S SERVICES ARE ITS PREMISES' SERVICES** — no unit-level column, no override, no divergence. ⚠ **RE-CONFIRMED AND COMPLETED BY THE PO ON 2026-09-10, VERBATIM: *"no they will do it on Premises - if it is change on premises all units will inherit it"*** — asked because the units blueprint's `Edit a unit` frame draws removable per-unit service chips, which presume a unit-level list a clerk can subtract from. **The 2026-08-26 ruling said where the column is NOT; this one says how a unit gets its answer: by reading through to the building, LIVE.** A change on the premises is inherited by every unit under it AT READ TIME — not copied at creation, not synced by a job, which is what `BR-MET-404`'s services clause already requires in the data layer and what nothing had yet said on a SCREEN. ⚠ **AND IT MAKES THE ABSENCE SPEAK.** The unit edit form gets no services control and no silent gap either: it shows the building's list READ-ONLY with one sentence saying where it comes from (`B14` rule 32 — a stated absence, not a blank). ⚠ **THAT SENTENCE IS NOW A PROMISE, SO IT HAS TO BE TRUE RATHER THAN ASSUMED** — a screen promising inheritance over a stored copy is hard-won law 1 exactly, and `BR-MET-118`'s own withdrawal note already flags that *nothing has yet verified that every such read resolves through the premises*. Verify the read-through before writing the sentence. **The drawing is amended, not followed.** ⚠ **The word is "YET": a deferral, not a rejection**, and the superseded text below is kept in full so that whoever revives it does not re-derive it. ⚠ **The trap to carry forward: the answer is still NOT `ServicePoint`** — it records a service already REALISED, so deriving a unit's services from it makes a unit silently lose a service the moment its meter is pulled, which is the divergence case backwards and will look like a shortcut to the next reader. ⚠ **What the withdrawal makes load-bearing:** every read of a unit's services must now resolve through its PREMISES, and nothing has yet verified that every such read does. SUPERSEDED TEXT FOLLOWS — **A UNIT CARRIES ITS OWN SERVICES AND MAY DIVERGE FROM ITS PREMISES (PO 2026-08-26, CR-037).** PO verbatim: *"we should have services details on unit - so later any changes that are differnt from premises we cna maintain it on units"*. **THE PURPOSE IS DIVERGENCE OVER TIME** — a unit starts out like its building and later stops being like it, and that difference has to live somewhere. **THREE LEVELS, each answering a different question, and conflating any two is the mistake this rule exists to prevent:** `Premises.services_available` = what this ADDRESS CAN have (the offer) · **`Unit.services_available` = what this UNIT DOES have (the declaration) — NEW, this rule** · `ServicePoint` = what is METERED AND CONNECTED (the realisation). ⚠ **`ServicePoint` IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE AND READING SERVICES OFF IT WOULD INVERT THE REQUIREMENT.** It records a service already realised — metered, connected, billable. The declaration must be sayable BEFORE a meter exists and must SURVIVE a disconnection; derive it from `ServicePoint` and a unit silently loses a service the moment its meter is pulled, which is precisely the divergence the PO asked to be able to record. **INHERITANCE:** a unit with no list of its own **inherits its premises' list**, so nothing is re-keyed for a building whose units are all alike — the ordinary case. A unit that declares its own list **OVERRIDES**, and that override is the *"changes that are different from premises"*. Same shape as the plan inheritance already ruled: the parent supplies, the child may refine, the refinement is visible. ⚠ **ONE CLAUSE IS AN ASSUMPTION, NOT A RULING, and is marked so it can be overruled cheaply rather than discovered:** a unit's list is assumed to be a **SUBSET** of its premises'. Worked — premises `500 Main St` offers water · sewer · trash; Unit 12 may declare *water · sewer* (no bin collection); Unit 12 may **not** declare *gas*, because the building has none, and the fix for that is to correct the premises rather than let a unit invent a service its address does not have. **The cost if that is wrong:** a utility keeping a deliberately narrow premises list is blocked from recording a real unit-level service until they widen the address first. One line turns the refusal into a warning; it is not worth blocking on. | PO 2026-08-26 (CR-037, answering CR-036 (c)) | **CONFIRMED** (the subset clause: ASSUMED, awaiting a word) | CR-037 |
| BR-MET-119 | ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-25 — A UNIT INHERITS **BY DEFAULT** AND A CLERK MAY UNTICK, SO A UNIT'S SET IS *WITHIN* THE PREMISES' SET RATHER THAN EQUAL TO IT.** PO ruling 2026-09-25, verbatim: *"the premises' list is what a unit takes, the tick-list stays, and a clerk may untick"*. **WHAT MOVED IS ONE WORD — `INHERITS` BECOMES `INHERITS BY DEFAULT`** — and nothing else in this rule moves: a premises still DECLARES, a unit still has no list of its own, a meter may still only attach against one of the unit's services, and `_refuse_service_not_at_premises` is untouched in both directions (a unit may never take a service the address does not offer). **THE DEFAULT IS THE WHOLE OFFER AND IT LIVES IN THE SERVICE LAYER** (`services._services_for_a_new_unit`), so every door has it — the units wizard, `POST /units`, the premises create's inline first unit and the importers — rather than only the door that has a form on it. ⚠ **ABSENT IS NOT EMPTY, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF THE AMENDMENT (`DEF-762`'s shape one layer up):** no `services` key means nobody was asked and the ADDRESS answers; `services: []` is a clerk unticking everything and is HONOURED; a partial list is honoured. `data.get("services") or []` cannot tell the first from the second and is the shape the defect had — in `_create_unit_inner`, and again in `importers._stage_pattern`, which set the key unconditionally so **every unit the add-units wizard has ever generated arrived carrying an explicit empty list**. ⚠ **A PREMISES HOLDING `NULL` INHERITS NOTHING** — an address nobody has been asked about has provisioned nothing, which is this rule's own words — and the create SUCCEEDS rather than dying on a service nobody typed. ⚠ **THE SHORTFALL IS LAWFUL AND IS THEREFORE COUNTED RATHER THAN REFUSED:** `GET /units/service-gaps` (`selectors.units_short_of_offer_page`) reports units holding fewer service points than their address offers, with the count and the missing services, and the Units tab draws it. `BR-BIL-300` is why it is worth counting — service points decide what a bill CAN charge, so every row there is a service the address advertises and nobody is billed for. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-30 BY TWO PO RULINGS, NOT BUILT (`BR-BIL-300`, `BR-RAT-1015`):** a service point bills only when an ASSET is on it — *"if unit has 6 utility services and user only add 1 or 3 assets to it - it means you will consider only 3 service point exist"* — and a `%`-of-service (sewer) has NO service point of its own: *"for % of service there will not be any service point"*. So a point with no asset is not in service and prints no bill line, and a sewer point on a unit is not what bills sewer (it follows water). Whether unit creation stops making a sewer point is part of that build. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-26: A SERVICE POINT IS NOW A CEILING AND NOT A GUARANTEE (`BR-BIL-1048`).** An account takes a SUBSET of its unit's points (`BR-CUS-1101`) and the bill charges only for what it holds, so a unit WITH a point can still have nobody billed for that service. This report counts the ESTATE gap, which is what it was built for; the ACCOUNT gap is a different question and is not answered here. **A unit with ZERO points is in that set**, which is the row a report built by grouping the service-point table cannot see. Built `DEF-MET-6796`, 2026-09-25 · tests `06-backend/tests/test_def_met_6796_unit_inherits_premises_services.py`. ⸻ **THE RULE AS ORIGINALLY CONFIRMED, UNCHANGED BELOW EXCEPT FOR THAT ONE WORD:** **THE CHAIN IS PREMISES → UNITS → METERS, AND EACH LINK CONSTRAINS THE NEXT (PO 2026-08-26, CR-036).** PO verbatim: *"look at Order - Premises -> Units -> Meters/Assets - Hence units will inherit it from premises and display it; when we uplaod the assest at that time we can upload meter against those services on units"*. **A PREMISES DECLARES what services are available at the address · A UNIT INHERITS that list and DISPLAYS it (it has no list of its own — CR-037 withdrawn) · A METER ATTACHES AGAINST ONE OF THE UNIT'S SERVICES.** So a unit's service list is not decoration: it is **the set of slots a meter can be fitted into**, which is why it must be visible before any meter exists (DEF-755) and why it must survive a meter being pulled. ⚠ **MEASURED 2026-08-26 — THE THIRD LINK IS NOT ENFORCED.** `services.ensure_service_point` and the assets import rule `_eval_asset_service_enabled` both validate a meter's service against the **TENANT-ENABLED** set only — *"this utility does not sell X"* — and never against the premises. So a gas meter can be attached to a unit at an address that offers only water, sewer and trash, provided the utility sells gas somewhere. Nothing refuses it and nothing flags it. ⚠ **AND ENFORCEMENT MUST NOT BE A BARE SUBSET CHECK, because `NULL` IS NOT AN EMPTY LIST (DEF-756/762).** A premises whose services were NEVER ASKED holds NULL, and every premises created through the product before 2026-08-26 is in that state. Refusing on NULL would block every asset upload for those addresses. So: **premises has a list and the service is not in it → REFUSE and name it; premises has no list at all → LOAD AND FLAG**, saying the address has not been asked which services it offers. That is the same posture BR-MET-100 takes for an unknown plan, and the same distinction the no-backfill rule exists to protect. | PO 2026-09-25 (`DEF-MET-6796`, the *by default* amendment) · PO 2026-08-26 (CR-036) · supersedes nothing · BR-MET-003 (⊆ tenant-enabled) stays and is the OUTER bound | **CONFIRMED — BOTH CLAUSES, AND AMENDED 2026-09-25 (see the head of the rule).** ⚖️ **THE THIRD LINK REFUSES (PO 2026-08-26):** verbatim *"Refuse it ; if they want that servcie then they ahve to provision it on that unit or premise first"*. A meter naming a service the address does not offer is REJECTED, and the remedy is in the ruling: go and provision the service first. ⚠ **AND THE REMEDY HAS EXACTLY ONE HOME — THE PREMISES.** The PO said *"on that unit or premise"*, and a unit has no list of its own (CR-037 withdrawn, a unit inherits), so the only place to provision is the ADDRESS. A refusal that sends a clerk to the unit sends them nowhere. ⚠ **REFUSAL APPLIES TO THE NEVER-ASKED CASE TOO, and the cost is stated rather than discovered:** every premises created through the product before 2026-08-26 holds NULL because no screen could set services (DEF-756), so an asset upload against one of those addresses is refused until somebody says what it offers. That is the ruling working as intended — an address nobody has been asked about has provisioned nothing — but it means the FIRST asset upload after this ships can reject wholesale. ⚠ **SO THE SENTENCE IS THE FEATURE:** a refusal must name the service asked for, name what the address DOES offer (or say it has never been asked), and name where to change it. A bare *"not offered here"* on 9,881 rows is the M/D/YYYY wall again — obstructive, correct, and unactionable. | CR-036 |
| BR-MET-120 | **THE HIGH-USAGE ABSOLUTE FLOORS, PER SERVICE, RATIFIED (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · closes the Blueprint's Open 5).** The high rule stays two-part everywhere (BR-MET-020 / OQ-002 closure): a reading flags only when it is **above 200% of normal AND above the service's absolute floor**, each floor in the service's OWN unit. The ratified seed table: **electric 50 kWh** (unchanged, OQ-002) · **water 1 kgal** (unchanged, OQ-002) · **gas 20 ccf** · **thermal/heat 10 therms** · **sewer, only where a sewer meter exists, 1 kgal** · **solar (the export register) 50 kWh** · **battery 50 kWh** · **trash and storm water: NO RULE ROW — never read, nothing to flag** · **broadband: DEFERRED with Open 7 (no register exists)**. Presented to the PO with the worked case (a shop at 8 ccf jumping to 18 ccf is 225% and trivial — the 20 ccf floor silences it; a real leak at 60 ccf flags) and options including "no figure — rule stays skipped-and-reported"; **PO verbatim: *"I agree with all your recomendation - please mark them as rule"***. These figures SEED `read_validation_service_settings` (the S64 wave) and remain tenant-editable on S64 thereafter; BR-MET-020's fail-safe (absent figure → rule skipped and reported, never guessed) is unchanged for any service without a row. A test asserts the seeded figures so drift goes RED (sign-off rule 2026-08-11, part 2). | PO 2026-08-26 · BR-MET-020 · CR-038 | **CONFIRMED** | S64 wave (test to assert seeds) |
| BR-MET-121 | **"READING IS READING — WE STORE IT AS IT COMES" (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · reshapes the Blueprint's Open 6).** PO verbatim: *"I will keep on colelcting reading and load it in application for given utility. when you run bill cycle you pull the reading and at that time we have to decide whether it is for 90 days or whrther it for 30 days or 15 days ; and if there is an issue with reading days we flag it ; reading is reading we store it as it comes"*. **(1)** An interval-days problem NEVER refuses a reading at import: the `days_threshold` rule's import-time verdict becomes **WARNING at most** (the 20–45 "hard" tier stops blocking commit; the bounds stay tenant/service config for FLAGGING). **(2)** The AUTHORITATIVE days judgment happens when the BILL RUN pulls the readings: the run decides whether the span it is pricing is 15, 30 or 90 days against the cycle it is billing, and flags issues on its reads-analysis step (the W8 Days tab is the surface). A quarterly cycle therefore needs no special bounds — nothing blocks its readings, and billing judges the span it actually prices. | PO 2026-08-26 · BR-MET-020 (bounds stay config) | **CONFIRMED** | W2 (import severity) + W8 (bill-time judgment) |
| BR-MET-122 | **A METER IN THE FILE THAT IS NOT IN THE TAGGED CYCLE IS A WARNING A CLERK ACCEPTS WITH A REASON (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · closes the Blueprint's Open 1).** PO verbatim: *"Open 1 - (a) flag as warning, clerk accepts with a reason"*. The cycle-membership rule (W2) imports the row, flags it by name ("M-4021 belongs to CY-002, file tagged CY-001"), and BR-MET-015's disposition discipline applies — accepted with a recorded reason or it does not post. Never a silent import (today's behavior), never a hard refusal. | PO 2026-08-26 | **CONFIRMED** | W2 |
| BR-MET-123 | **THE SOURCE IS A TAG; THE TRANSPORTS ARE FILE (CSV OR EXCEL) AND MANUAL — NO INTEGRATIONS YET, AND THEIR ABSENCE NEVER BLOCKS THE TAG (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · rules the transport half of Open 4 and OQ-325).** PO verbatim: *"we are only selectiing the soruce and accepting the CSV or excel file; thats is the scope right now; there is no integration yet; but it should not stop us from tagging the soruce and accepting the CSV or excel file"*. **(1)** The read SOURCE (the method vocabulary and the batch's source detail) is a TAG chosen at upload and stored — an AMI-collected file is tagged AMI even though it arrives as a file. ⚠ **THIS CLAUSE SAID "the five-word method vocabulary" UNTIL 2026-08-27 AND THE VOCABULARY IS NOW EIGHT — BR-MET-140 (PO ruling closing OQ-392) added `Drive-by`, `Self-read` and `Field visit`.** The count is deliberately no longer written here: the list is DATA (BR-AST-043 · BR-TEN-036, tenant-extensible), a rule that restates its length goes stale the day a tenant adds a ninth, and this clause has now been that stale rule once. **(2)** The only transports are file upload — **CSV or Excel (.xlsx)**, Excel being NEW scope for the Extract stage — and manual entry. **(3)** No head-end/SFTP/API integration is built now; a transport card with nothing behind it is not offered (the DEF-520 posture), but the source TAGS remain complete. | PO 2026-08-26 | **CONFIRMED** | W7 (xlsx at Extract) · W3 (tags on rows) |

A device's figure (the driving case: a solar inverter's monthly generation, e.g. 612 kWh) enters through the SAME read pipeline against the device asset, with a device-generation register added to the extensible channel list (a new value, never a schema change — the channel's exact name is presented for the PO's nod with W6's packet, not minted silently). Validation applies the rules that make sense for a generation figure; the sixteen meter rules that do not apply are skipped and reported, never faked. | PO 2026-08-26 | **CONFIRMED** (scope) / channel name to present | W6 — now unblocked |

| BR-MET-126 | **THE READING AFTER A CORRECTED ONE: RECOMPUTE UNBILLED AUTOMATICALLY, RECOMMEND THROUGH THE MONEY DOOR WHEN BILLED — AND NEVER TOUCH SOURCE DATA (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · closes the Blueprint's Open 10).** PO verbatim: *"(b) auto-recompute when the next reading is still unbilled; flag it through the billed-correction door when it's billed - but we cant cahnge the source data, so we will ahve to accept the trust and make the recomendation"*. **(1)** After a supersede, the next reading on the same meter and register is found in the same transaction. **(2)** UNBILLED next reading: the system recomputes it automatically — as a SUPERSEDING CORRECTION ROW (hard rule 4: the original row is never edited; "we cant cahnge the source data"), marked system-generated and naming the correction that triggered it. **(3)** BILLED next reading: the system does not act alone — it FLAGS the row through the billed-correction door as a RECOMMENDATION with the worked delta, and a human confirms (the read fix and the money fix travel together). The Dec/Jan/Feb 200-double-billed-units case is the acceptance test, proven red first. | PO 2026-08-26 | **CONFIRMED** | W1b — now unblocked |
| BR-MET-127 | **A SUPERSEDING WRITE MODE MEETS A BILLED READ: THE ROW IS REFUSED BY NAME, THE REST OF THE BATCH POSTS (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038).** PO verbatim, adopting the on-file recommendation as the rule: *"(a) refuse by name — a money correction should never be minted as a side effect of a bulk upload; the clerk opens each through the billed door, deliberately"*. An **Insert & update** row whose existing read is BILLED is refused with the read named and the reason ("billed — correct it through the billed-correction door"); the refusal is a row outcome, never a batch failure; nothing bulk ever opens a money correction. | PO 2026-08-26 · BR-MET-053 · hard rule 4 | **CONFIRMED** | ✅ **BUILT — W7, 2026-08-26.** `write_modes.plan()` answers `refuse_billed` when a superseding mode meets a `lifecycle="billed"` head; `commit_read_batch` skips that row and names it (`"M-7001 · 2026-08-03 · total"`, `br_ref: BR-MET-127`, `door: correct_billed_read`) while the rest of the batch posts. ⚠ **Severity is WARNING, not ERROR, and that is the rule's own words**: *"the refusal is a row outcome, never a batch failure"* — an ERROR would gate the whole post via `_unresolved_errors` and stop the other 1,179 rows. Proven RED first (`'supersede' == 'refuse_billed'`) and pinned by `tests/test_cr038_w7_write_modes.py` ⚠ **REWORDED 2026-08-27 (CR-042) AND THE RULE IS UNCHANGED.** This rule was written when three write modes existed and it named the destructive one. `Replace` is withdrawn from every import, so the title and the body stop naming a control nobody can reach — a rule describing an unreachable control is the stale-comment failure (hard-won law 1), and this one sits in the money rules. **The refusal itself was never Replace-specific and this was MEASURED, not assumed:** the code read `SUPERSEDING_MODES = ("upsert", "replace")` and this rule's own original text read *"A Replace (or Insert & update) row…"*. `upsert` remains a superseding mode, so the billed-read refusal attaches to exactly the rows it always did. Pinned by `test_cr042_write_modes.py`. |

Every reading collected by a lifecycle event — move-in, move-out, transfer, disconnect, new connect — is an ORDINARY committed read (same pipeline, same rules) whose record additionally states **(a) the event that generated it**, per event (one context value per wizard event), and **(b) the user** who collected/entered it. | BR-MET-132 | **THE MISSING CHIP KEEPS COUNTING UNTIL THE BILL RUN CONSUMES THE PERIOD (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · closes OQ-389, amending US-MET-019 §5's literal reading).** PO verbatim: *"Keep counting until the run consumes the period — those are the chase days"*. Worked case: CY-001's reading window closes Aug 20 and the run is Aug 25; on Aug 22 sixty meters are unread. Under the literal §5 ("open windows only") the chip read **0** on exactly the days a clerk chases stragglers. The chip therefore counts missing required records for windows that are OPEN **or CLOSED-BUT-UNBILLED**, and drops a period only when its bill run has consumed it. | PO 2026-08-26 · amends US-MET-019 §5 | **CONFIRMED** | **BUILT 2026-08-26** — `completeness.current_window` gained a third state, `closed_unbilled`, and the skip condition changed from *ended* to *consumed*: `completeness.period_is_consumed` → `metering.seams.period_consumed_by_run` → `billing.selectors.period_consumed_by_run` (a period is consumed by a run whose status is `completed` and whose period OVERLAPS the window — a clerk may widen a run past the reading window and that run really did bill it). `period_engine.latest_opened_window` is new: `upcoming_windows` answers *what comes next* and had already moved on to September on 22 Aug. The chip (`missing_records_count`) and the derived Pending rows (`pending_read_entries`) share one predicate, `CHASEABLE_WINDOW_STATES`, so the chip's number always has rows behind it. The S25 caption states the amended predicate and the chip renders the real count with its unresolved-meter footnote (DEF-815). ⚠ TWO LANE JUDGMENTS, both in `current_window`'s docstring and the decision log: (1) ONE window is examined — the most recent to have OPENED — so an older period a run skipped is not counted; the PO's case is a single period and the alternative has no floor. (2) A seam `None` (billing cannot answer) reads as NOT consumed, so the chip stays loud. |
| BR-MET-133 | **A DISPUTE RAISED AFTER THE BILL ISSUED: THE BILL STANDS, THE MONEY DOOR RESOLVES IT, AND THE DISPUTED AMOUNT DOES NOT AGE (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · closes OQ-245).** PO verbatim: *"Bill stands, resolution through the billed-correction door, and the disputed amount doesn't age into late fees while open"*. Worked case: Sarah's July bill issued on a 684 kWh reading; on 5 Aug she disputes it. **(1)** The issued invoice is NOT voided or held — it is issued money and stays (hard rule 4). **(2)** The marker holds the account out of the NEXT run (existing behaviour). **(3)** Resolution goes through the billed-correction door: she is right → supersede + the money correction it opens; she is wrong → clear the marker, the bill was always correct. **(4)** NEW: while the dispute is open, the **disputed amount is excluded from ageing and from late-fee assessment** — a customer is not penalised for the days we investigate. The undisputed remainder of the bill ages normally. | PO 2026-08-26 · OQ-245 closed | **CONFIRMED** | **BUILT 2026-08-26 — clause 4; clauses 1–3 CONFIRMED BY TEST, not rebuilt.** New: `billing.late_fee.DisputeExclusion` + `ageable_portion(overdue_amount, dispute)`, and a `dispute=` argument on `assess_late_fee` applied BEFORE the zero test, the $25.00 balance floor and the grace clock — the rule takes money out of AGEING, it does not waive a fee that applied. `billing/dispute_ageing.py` answers *is a dispute open* through metering's published `dispute_holds_for_unit` (B1 — no cross-module query, no write, no invoice touched). Worked, three ways: $184.20 with $47.00 disputed ages $137.20; $1,000.00 with $400.00 disputed pays **$9.00** where the un-excluded bill pays $15.00; $60.00 with $40.00 disputed leaves $20.00, under the $25.00 floor, so **no fee at all**. GT-8 unmoved (default `NO_DISPUTE`), goldens re-run. Every surface stopped citing OQ-245: `billed_bill_effect` is now `issued_bill_stands_br_met_133`, the S29 banner states clause 4 in the clerk's words, and clearing records `outcome: "reading_stands"` (clause 3's second branch). ⚠ **BLOCKED HALF — OQ-418 (MINTED, 🔒):** nothing in this product records a disputed AMOUNT (measured 2026-08-26; `meter_reads.disputed_at` is a QUANTITY in dispute, not a sum of money), so an open dispute with no figure **raises `NotImplementedError("OQ-418")`** rather than choosing between "age nothing" and "age everything" — $137.20 apart on Sarah's bill. ⚠ **AND THE AGEING HALF HAS NO IMPLEMENTATION TO EXCLUDE FROM:** billing publishes no `account_aging_state` and customers' ageing reads OPENING BUCKETS only, so only the late-fee half exists to build. ⚠ The $25.00 floor applying to the undisputed REMAINDER is a lane reading — decision log + a test. |

So: percent-of-service (sewer off the water reading), flat and other unmetered-by-design services **EXPECT NO READ** and must never appear as a missing read (scenario 26); a **consumption-priced** service whose service point carries no meter is a COMPLETENESS EXCEPTION a human resolves — a setup fault, named, never silently base-only and never a phantom missing meter. | BR-MET-136 | **EVERY ENABLED VEE RULE ALWAYS RUNS, AND EVERY RULE SHIPS ENABLED (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · rules the S64 kill-switch question — and CHANGES what was built).** PO verbatim: *"yes every rule that is enabled always run - you ahve to keep default - all enabled"*. **(1)** A judgment rule carries an ENABLED flag per (tenant, service) — so a utility CAN retire a check that does not fit its business. **(2)** The default is **ALL ENABLED**, for every service, at provisioning and on backfill. **(3)** An enabled rule ALWAYS runs — no silent skipping, no per-batch bypass; the only lawful non-run is the BR-MET-020 fail-safe (no figure → skipped AND REPORTED). **(4)** The INTEGRITY rules (unknown_meter, retired_meter, unit_missing, negative_usage, rollover, duplicate, export_not_net) are NOT switchable — they stay locked on S64. ⚠ **This amends what the S64 lane shipped today**, which has no enabled flag at all: the column, its default, its S64 control and its audit row are a follow-up task, and a disabled rule must state on the read that it was disabled rather than vanish. | PO 2026-08-26 · BR-MET-020 · BR-MET-129 | **CONFIRMED** | **BUILT 2026-08-26** — `read_validation_service_settings.rule_enabled`, a JSONB map of rule → bool where an ABSENT key means ENABLED, so the column default `{}` IS clause 2 and no backfill pass exists to get wrong (metering migration `0036_cr038_br_met_136_rule_enabled`, HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING). **Clause 1:** the switchable set is `validation.JUDGMENT_RULES` — `ALL_RULES` minus `INTEGRITY_RULES`, derived by subtraction exactly as the ruling defines it, thirteen rules today. **Clause 3:** every rule still RUNS; the ONE place a verdict can be replaced is `validation.switch_outcome` (rows) / `switch_group_outcome` (the three group rules), so there is no skip branch to drift. **Clause 4:** the API refuses BY NAME (422 `VAL-003`, `metering.read_validation.integrity_rule_locked`, the rule in `params`) and `validation.rule_switches` forces every integrity rule back on whatever a row says — a shell, a fixture or a future migration cannot switch one off either. **The trailing clause:** a disabled rule is REPLACED, never dropped — it lands on the read as a new severity **`disabled`** (rank −2, never a verdict, never a primary status) with reason `disabled_by_tenant_setting`, and the batch summary carries `disabled_rules` BESIDE `unevaluated_rules`. ⚠ **`disabled` is deliberately NOT `not_evaluated`:** a gap a clerk should chase and a check the utility retired are different facts, and mixing them makes the chase list unreadable. **Audit:** its own action `settings.read_validation.rule_switched`, before/after as on/off, only the rules that moved. **S64:** per-service checkbox list from the SERVED vocabulary, integrity rules rendered as LOCKED rows with no input and the reason stated. ⚠ **`generation_channel` is switchable because the ruling named seven rules and not eight — `OQ-417` raised, not chosen.** |
| BR-MET-137 | **SINGLE-LETTER SERVICE CODES ARE AN ACCEPTED INPUT VOCABULARY (PO 2026-08-26, CR-038 · closes DEF-792).** Asked with the measured case in hand — a row's stated service is validated nowhere and every existing fixture carries `"E"` — the PO ruled: *"yes accept them"*. **(1)** The importer accepts the utility's own shorthand for a service the tenant SELLS: `E` → electric, `W` → water, `G` → gas, and the same first-letter shorthand for the tenant's other enabled services, resolved case-insensitively against the ENABLED set (never a hardcoded ten-row table — BR-MET-003's posture: a tenant may only state a service it sells). **(2)** An ambiguous letter — two enabled services sharing a first letter, e.g. `S` for sewer and solar, or `T` for trash and thermal — is NOT guessed: the row reports the ambiguity with BOTH candidates named, because a silently-picked service prices the wrong charge. **(3)** An unrecognised token still reports `not_evaluated` with the value named (the W2 posture, unchanged). **(4)** The full service code remains valid input everywhere; this ADDS a shorthand, it does not replace the vocabulary. ⚠ Where a tenant's enabled set makes a letter ambiguous, the fix is the file stating the full code — the product never chooses for them. | PO 2026-08-26 (DEF-792) · BR-MET-003 · BR-MET-014 | **CONFIRMED** | **BUILT 2026-08-26** — `apps.metering.validation.resolve_stated_service` + the coherence rule's three branches. The four clauses each have a test: `E` on an electric meter is now an **ok** (it was `not_evaluated` on every legacy file), `W` on that meter still WARNS with both named, `S` where sewer AND solar are enabled refuses with both candidates and resolves cleanly for the tenant next door who sells only sewer, and `elec` resolves to nothing — the ruling covered single letters, and a signature covers only what was shown. ⚠ W2's own test asserting `"E"` was the gap is AMENDED in place with the supersession stamped in its docstring, not deleted. |
| BR-MET-066c | **THE SEEDED ESTATE CARRIES A REAL TWO-LEVEL HIERARCHY, AND THIS SUPERSEDES BR-MET-066b's closing clause *"The four seeded rows stay top-level and no sub-area is seeded"* (PO 2026-08-26, CR-039 wave E task 22).** PO verbatim, looking at a tenant they had just provisioned: *"Why you dont have sub areas below - Areas metering 4 North district · Downtown · East district · Industrial corridor"*. BR-MET-066b's restraint was correct on the day it was written — the shape was ruled and no ROW had been asked for, so seeding one would have been inventing a tenant's vocabulary. The PO has now asked for one, so the invention stops being an invention. **WHAT IS SEEDED, EXACTLY — THREE SUB-AREAS UNDER TWO OF THE FOUR DISTRICTS:** `NORTH-NE` "NE" and `NORTH-NW` "NW" under `NORTH`, `EAST-SE` "SE" under `EAST`. **`DTWN` and `IND` deliberately get NONE**, and that is a rule rather than an omission: a tenant needs at least one childless district or two shipped behaviours cannot be seen on seeded data at all — the Sub-area picker's *"this area has no sub-areas"* help text, and a premises whose `area_path` is a bare district with no `·` in it. **WHY THESE NAMES:** r10 SET-06.2 is the ONLY place in the corpus that NAMES a sub-area, and what it names is a compass quadrant under a compass district (*"South · SE"*, *"South · SW"*). That register is r10's own, so nothing here is minted. **r10's SET-04 card also declares COUNTS — North 3 · Downtown 2 · East 2 · Industrial 1 — and they are deliberately NOT matched:** a count is not a row, r10 supplies no NAME for six of those eight, and inventing six names to make a decorative number come out is precisely the failure BR-MET-066b's superseded clause warned about. The restraint survives; only its conclusion moved. **THE ESTATE IS TAGGED INTO THEM:** `AREA_BY_STREET` now names each street's FINEST area with its district as a fallback, so a tenant holding the sub-areas reads `North district · NW` and a tenant provisioned before fixture V2.48.0.1 still reads `North district` rather than losing its tag (which would be DEF-256 reintroduced by an improvement). **Depth is UNCHANGED at exactly two** — BR-MET-066b's cap is CONFIRMED and this rule adds no third level; the seeder goes through the same `_resolve_parent` a person does, so a fixture declaring a grandchild is REFUSED rather than flattened. | PO ruling 2026-08-26 (CR-039 wave E task 22) · r10 `SET-06.2` "South · SE" · r10 `SET-04.areas` counts (cited, not matched) · supersedes BR-MET-066b's no-sub-area-seeded clause only · platform fixture **V2.48.0.1** | **CONFIRMED that sub-areas are seeded** (the PO asked for them, and CR-039's wave list carries *"seed sub-areas"* as task 22). ⚠ **THE COUNT AND THE THREE NAMES ARE NOT SIGNED — they are BUILT AND ON THE AGENDA.** The sign-off rule of 2026-08-11 says *"a signature covers only what was shown"*, and what was shown is one question about sub-areas being absent; nobody has been shown the figure **7** or the codes `NORTH-NE` / `NORTH-NW` / `EAST-SE`. Every choice is sourced (r10's own quadrant register) and none is invented, but sourced is not ratified. **What to put to the PO in one line:** *"a brand-new utility's Areas list goes from 4 rows to 7 — your four districts, plus NE and NW under North district and SE under East district; Downtown and Industrial corridor get none, so the demo can show a district that has sub-areas and one that has not."* `tests/test_cr010_reference_list_inventory.py` carries the same sentence and goes RED if the figure moves again | `06-backend/tests/test_cr039_wave_e_sub_areas.py::test_the_platform_fixture_declares_three_sub_areas` · `…::test_two_districts_deliberately_have_no_sub_area` · `…::test_the_seeded_estate_is_actually_tagged_two_levels_deep` · `…::test_a_tenant_seeded_before_the_bump_still_gets_its_district` |
| BR-MET-138 | **THE PREMISES LIST FILTERS BY AREA, AND A DISTRICT MATCHES THE PREMISES IN ITS SUB-AREAS (CR-039 wave E task 22, closing DEF-836).** BR-MET-072 has said since 2026-08-16 that the area filter is multi-select and that *"selecting a PARENT area matches every premises tagged to it and to any of its sub-areas"*, and `selectors._area_predicate` implements it — for the ROUTE premises picker and for nothing else. **`selectors.premises_list` read `type` and `status` and NOTHING ELSE**, and `core.pagination.parse_list_params` documents that *"undeclared filter names are ignored"* — so S14's own list could not be filtered by area at all, and `premises_list(filters={"area_codes": […]})` returned the WHOLE tenant for every input including nonsense. This is the shape BR-MET-122 already established for an OFF-cycle meter, one case over: a meter in NO cycle has nothing to be off, so it needed its own rule. ⚠ **THE COST, STATED BECAUSE THE RULING DOES NOT REMOVE IT:** an untagged read is still invisible to W1's expected-vs-received completeness for every period, so the warning is the only thing that gets it chased. | PO 2026-08-27 (OQ-422) · BR-MET-129 (a collected reading is STORED) · BR-MET-122 (an off-cycle meter WARNS with a reason) · BR-MET-014 (batch tags inherit to every row) | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_140_no_cycle_counter_read.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/QuickReadModal.test.tsx` |
| BR-MET-141 | **THE TWELVE-MONTH USAGE IS STORED PER METER AND PER REGISTER AS WELL AS PER ACCOUNT (PO 2026-08-27 · closes OQ-421).** PO verbatim: *"(b) Store it per meter too - otherwise we will ahve to calculate it again and again"* — **and the reason they gave IS the requirement: recomputation cost.** **(1) THE GRAIN.** `meter_read_asset_monthly_usage` holds one head row per (tenant, asset, channel, period_month), beside BR-MET-106's (tenant, account, service, period_month). **The REGISTER is part of the key**, because one meter can hold several registers whose figures BR-MET-135 (`generation`), BR-AST-029 (`export`) and BR-RAT-014 (demand, in kW) forbid adding together. **(2) THE WORKED FIGURE.** Sarah's meter M-4412 reports a **684 kWh** energy total and a **14 kW** demand peak on the same July day: the table stores **two rows** — `total 684.000000 kWh` and `demand 14.000000 kWh` — and her account's BR-MET-106 row stores **684.000000 kWh**. **DEF-798's measured 698 is not merely absent, it is unrepresentable**, because no row here is a sum across registers. **(3) ONE DERIVATION, NEVER TWO.** The stored row is produced by the SAME functions the computed figure uses — `monthly_usage.group_by_month` / `summarise` / `fingerprint` — reached through the SAME query and the SAME `ReadFact` construction as the account grain, and both grains are written by ONE `recompute_account` call in ONE transaction, so they can never be stale relative to each other. **(4) IT IS A CACHE, NOT A FACT.** Every column is derivable from `meter_reads`; the S50 payload reads a stored row when one exists (`source: "stored"`) and computes the identical figure when none does (`source: "computed_from_reads"`). OQ-421 put the asymmetry to the PO before they ruled — adding the dimension is a migration, removing it later is dropping a column with data in it — and this is the property that keeps the reversal cheap in MEANING even though it is not cheap in SCHEMA. **(5) All of BR-MET-106's structural laws hold unchanged at the finer grain**: the quantity carries its unit, corrections RESTATE and never edit, an unread month is ABSENT and never `0`, and estimates count and are marked. **(6) Nothing bills off it** — no rate, no invoice line and no charge reads this table. ⚠ **THE COST THE PO ACCEPTED:** the row count is multiplied by (meters × registers) against the account grain. | PO 2026-08-27 (OQ-421) · BR-MET-106 (the account grain, unchanged) · BR-MET-059 (a register is a channel) · BR-MET-135 · BR-AST-029 · BR-RAT-014 · DEF-798 (the measured 684 + 14 = 698) | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_oq_421_per_meter_monthly_usage.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_def_798_register_blind_usage.py::test_cr038_t8_tc10__*` |
| BR-MET-142 | ⚠ **RENUMBERED FROM `BR-MET-140` ON MERGE, 2026-08-27 (DEF-892).** Two lanes minted a rule into this id within nineteen minutes of each other and neither could see the other's branch; the lane that wrote first keeps the number, which is the resolution rule DEF-892 records so it is not re-argued. **The RULE below is unchanged in every word** — only its identifier moved, and `BR-MET-140` now means *a counter reading on a meter with no cycle is accepted and warned*. Folding self-read into Manual would assert that a customer's own reading and a clerk's are the same kind of record; folding field-visit into Mobile would assert a technician's visit is a device choice. Neither is true. **(3)** And mapping would have **RE-LABELLED HISTORY NOBODY VERIFIED**: every reading already collected under those three words would silently have become something it was not recorded as. **(4) THE LEGACY TRANSLATION IS APPLIED ON READ AND NO COMMITTED READING IS REWRITTEN** — BR-MET-123's posture, unchanged, and CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 is why: several of those readings are BILLED. `read_import.LEGACY_READ_METHOD_BY_DETAIL` grows 3 → 6 so all six legacy `source_detail` words now map, **each to its own word**; `read_method_origin` still serves `derived` for every one. **(5) WHAT THIS BUYS, AND IT IS THE POINT:** a reading tagged `drive_by` served **no method at all** (`read_method_origin = not_stated`) because the W3 lane refused to guess. It now serves `drive_by`. *Not stated* becomes an accurate recorded value rather than an absence. **(6)** `import` and `estimated` still map to **None** and that is unchanged: a file is a TRANSPORT, not a collection method (BR-MET-123), and nobody collected an estimate (BR-MET-028). **(7) NO BACKFILL SHIPS WITH THIS.** If history is ever to be rewritten it is a separate, flagged migration with the PO's word on it — REPORTED, not done. ⚠ **A DEMO FIXTURE COMMENT CALLED `mobile` *"the seeded read-method code for a drive-by round"*, ASSERTING `drive_by ≈ Mobile` BY COMMENT.** It was never a decision, it is what made OQ-392 ambiguous, and it is corrected in the same commit as this rule — a comment claiming a rule is not the rule (hard-won law 1). | PO 2026-08-27 (OQ-392) · BR-AST-043 · BR-MET-123 · BR-TEN-036 · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 | **CONFIRMED** | **BUILT 2026-08-27** — `apps/assets/services.SEED_READ_METHODS` 5 → 8, mirrored in `apps/tenancy/fixtures/lookup_defaults.json` (serial bump + re-pinned digest, so the sweep revisits existing tenants) and pinned equal by `tests/test_reference_data_completeness.py`; `read_import.LEGACY_READ_METHOD_BY_DETAIL` 3 → 6; three keys in all eleven catalogues. | BR-MET-143 | ⚠ **RENUMBERED FROM `BR-MET-141` ON MERGE, 2026-08-27 (DEF-892).** The second half of the same collision the row above records: this lane read the register, found `BR-MET-140` taken by a sibling and moved to `141` — which a THIRD lane had already taken, uncommitted, nineteen minutes earlier. **The lane did everything right and could not have seen it.** By DEF-892's resolution rule the earlier-written rule keeps the number, so `BR-MET-141` means *the twelve-month usage is stored per meter and per register* and this rule is **143**. **Not one word of it changed.** Anything citing `BR-MET-141` for the demand unit means THIS rule. **A READING'S UNIT MUST MATCH THE UNIT OF THE THING THAT PRICES IT — AND A DEMAND READING CARRIES kW (PO 2026-08-27, closing OQ-391 and fixing DEF-797).** PO verbatim: *"A demand reading carries kW - we have to match utility service unit and meter reading unit - if it does not match then we have intriduce multiplier factor on meter. you will ahve to seed that data."* **(1) THE UNIT IS RECORDED PER REGISTER, NOT PER METER** (OQ-391 option (a)): one electric meter holds **kWh** on its energy register and **kW** on its demand register. `_post_row` stamped `unit=asset.unit` on every channel until this ruling, which is DEF-797's label half — a peak printed `kWh` on an issued document while `rate_schedules.demand_rate_per_kw` priced it per kW. **(2) THE GENERAL RULE:** the service's unit and the reading's unit must MATCH; where they do not, a **multiplier factor on the meter** reconciles them, and that data is **seeded**. ⚠ **(3) THE ONE CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS THE MERGE LANE'S READING RATHER THAN THE PO'S WORDS — recorded here so ONE SENTENCE FROM THE PO CAN CORRECT IT.** A multiplier is a **SCALE** factor. It reconciles `ccf → kgal`, a ×10 register, or raw gallons → kgal (B4's own OQ-004: *"register stores raw gallons; billed unit per-service config: kgal (default) / gal / ccf; convert at rating input"*). **It CANNOT reconcile kW → kWh, because those are different physical quantities** — power against energy — and no number turns one into the other. So the rule is applied as *a reading's unit must match the unit of the thing that PRICES it*: an energy register's kWh matches the energy charge, a demand register's kW matches the per-kW demand charge (BR-RAT-014). Units differing by SCALE are reconciled by the meter's multiplier; units differing by DIMENSION are different quantities priced by different charges and are **NEVER summed** (CLAUDE.md hard rule 3). **(4) DEF-797's QUANTITY HALF IS SEPARATE FROM ITS LABEL AND IS FIXED HERE TOO.** A demand register is a **MAXIMUM**, so BR-MET-051's *"manual demand-register value at cycle read"* is its billable quantity — NOT BR-MET-019's `(current − prior) × multiplier`, which on two peaks is the difference between two maxima. Measured: peaks **398 → 412** committed `consumption=14.000000` and billed **$154.00** at the Industrial `$11.00/kW`, where the correct charge is **412 kW × $11.00 = $4,532.00** — one commercial account under-billed by **$4,378.00 in a single month**. **(5) `kW` IS TRANSCRIBED, NEVER INVENTED**, from three statements the repo already held: `rate_schedules.demand_rate_per_kw` (B2's own column), `tenancy.services.SERVICE_DEFAULT_UOMS["electric"] == ("kWh", "kW")` with `kW` scoped to electric in the seeded UoM catalog, and r10's screen string *"Demand (peak kW)"*. **An energy unit with no DECLARED demand unit gets NONE**: `register_unit` answers `None`, the existing `unit_missing` integrity rule refuses the row by name, and a tenant metering demand in kVA is refused loudly rather than stamped `kW` (rule 3 has no assume branch; hard-won law 3 — the unsafe option is the one you type). **(6) THE SEEDED MULTIPLIER** (*"you will ahve to seed that data"*): `EstatePlan.multiplier` is DERIVED from the tenant's own `tenant_services.primary_uom` (which is also the meter's UoM) against its `billed_uom`, classified through metering's own rule. Same unit — or no `billed_uom` configured, which is every tenant alive because r6.4 made it an option and nothing seeds it — is **×1**, and ×1 IS the reconciliation, not a placeholder; so no existing estate moves. A SCALE mismatch seeds the tenant's OWN `conversion_factor`. **A scale mismatch with no factor on file, a DIMENSION mismatch, and an unknown pair each seed NOTHING and name the reason on the Sample data panel** — OQ-004's closure is *"no silent conversion"*, and a plausible `0.748052` typed into the fixture would ride into every demo bill looking official. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (`CR-062` group C, PO statement (8)): THE FACTOR IS PER METERED SERVICE, NOT ONE PER ESTATE.** This clause said `EstatePlan.multiplier` — singular — because the seeded estate held exactly ONE register: `core/sample_fixture.estate_plan` took the first enabled metered service as the estate's backbone and refused every later one by name. The PO overruled that on being shown, from the code, that a utility selling Electric + Water + Sewer receives water and sewer and **no electric line on any bill** — verbatim: *"this is not correct - you cannot drop electricty or gas - can you tell me what corrections you will ahve to do"*. **The RULE never changed and is not being widened here**: a multiplier reconciles ONE service's register unit with the unit THAT service is billed in, so a per-estate factor was always a category error — it simply could not be seen while the estate held one register. The plan now answers `EstatePlan.metered`, a row per enabled metered service carrying that service's own `uom` and its own `multiplier`, and every branch above applies unchanged **per row**: a water register billed in `kgal` seeds ×1 while an electric register billed in a unit no factor reconciles is refused BY NAME and plants no electric meter, on the same tenant, at the same time. **The three refusal sentences are unchanged and are now per service rather than per estate**, which is what makes a partial refusal expressible at all — before this, one unreconcilable service refused the whole estate. `_NOT_THE_BACKBONE` — *"this fixture declares ONE register per unit"* — is WITHDRAWN, because it is no longer true. **(7) THE UNIT IS ON THE SCREEN.** The S28 review step served a staged `value` as a bare number, so a clerk approving 1,180 rows could not have SEEN DEF-797's wrong unit at the one moment a person looks at a reading before it becomes an issued document. The staged row now carries the REGISTER's unit — the very string `_post_row` will stamp — and S25/S29/S50 render the served consumption with it. | PO 2026-08-27 (OQ-391 closure) · DEF-797 · BR-MET-051 · BR-MET-019 · BR-MET-059 · BR-RAT-014 · BR-RAT-038 · OQ-004's closure · CLAUDE.md hard rule 3 | **CONFIRMED** for clauses 1, 2, 4, 5, 6 and 7 (the PO's words and their direct implementation). ⚠ **Clause 3 is the MERGE LANE's reading, not the PO's** — flagged rather than presented as ruled, and it returns to the agenda. **HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** for clause 3. | `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_141_register_units.py` (14 cases: the pure rule, both halves of DEF-797 end to end through the real post path, the billing seam handing rating 412 kW, a correction staying in kW, and the four seeded-multiplier branches) · `06-backend/tests/test_cr038_w4_demand.py::test_w4_tc16…` (AMENDED — it pinned the mismatch *"so the day a rule states the demand UoM, it goes red here and nowhere else"*) · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/DemandReset.dom.test.tsx` · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/UploadReadsPage.test.tsx` |
| BR-MET-144 | **A QUANTITY DISPLAYS AT MOST TWO DECIMALS, TRAILING ZEROS TRIMMED — STORAGE STAYS AT SIX (PO 2026-08-27, closing DEF-894).** PO verbatim, after finding it on the live app: *"decimal places and any other issues you observe"* → *"2 decimals max, trailing zeros trimmed - this is correct"*. **(1) THE RULE.** A quantity written for a person shows **at most two decimals**; more than two **rounds HALF-UP**, the direction B4 rounds money; and **trailing zeros are trimmed**, so a whole number keeps no decimal point at all — `8.000000` renders **`8`**, never `8.00`. **(2) THIS IS WHERE A QUANTITY DIFFERS FROM MONEY, and the difference is the point.** CLAUDE.md hard rule 2 fixes money at 2dp *and pads it*: `$8.00` is right because a currency's precision is a property of the currency. A quantity's precision is **what the meter measured**, so `8.00 kgal` is noise. A fix that reached two decimals and stopped would satisfy a careless reading of this rule and still be wrong; the test asserts `"8"` and explicitly **not** `"8.00"`. **(3) STORAGE DOES NOT MOVE.** BR-MET-019's `NUMERIC(18,6)` is what is stored and what the engine computes with. This rule governs the LAST STEP before a person reads the figure, and nothing round-trips through it. **(4) THE UNIT IS NOT OPTIONAL** (CLAUDE.md hard rule 3): a quantity with no unit is **refused a rendering** rather than printed bare, because a bare number in a column headed *Usage* is read as the tenant's own unit by whoever is looking at it. **(5) AN ABSENT QUANTITY STAYS ABSENT** — `None` renders `None`, never `0`, which is BR-MET-106's *an unread month is ABSENT, never a zero* held at the display layer too. **(6) ONE FUNCTION, SIX SURFACES.** `apps/metering/quantity_display.py` is the single composer; the reads list, the reading detail headline, its working, the read history, the printable route sheet and the correction modal's prefill all reach it. Six surfaces formatting independently is how three of them drift. ⚠ **WHY THIS RULE DID NOT EXIST UNTIL A CUSTOMER SAW IT.** Hard rule 2 covers money and percentages and is **silent on consumption**, so every quantity reached the screen at its storage precision and no gate could object — there was no figure to check against. `readsShared.tsx` had even written the reason down: *"rounding for display is a decision no signed document makes for these figures"*. That was true, and `05-prototype/r10` had been drawing `684 kWh`, `3.72 kgal` and `26.24 kgal` the whole time. Hard-won law 6: nothing in this repo reads rendered output. | PO 2026-08-27 (DEF-894) · BR-MET-019 (storage) · BR-MET-106 (an absent month) · CLAUDE.md hard rule 2 (the same shape, for money) · hard rule 3 (the unit) · r10 RDS-01/RDS-04 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_def_894_quantity_display.py` (17 cases: the four figures from the PO's own screenshots, r10's three draws, half-up at the boundary, the no-trailing-zeros half a two-decimal fix would miss, the absent quantity, the refused unit, and the stored value proved unmutated) |
| BR-MET-160 | **A LIST CELL ANSWERS ITS COLUMN IN ONE WORD; THE PROVENANCE OF THE VALUE BELONGS IN THE ROW'S OWN DETAIL VIEW (PO 2026-08-27, closing DEF-895).** PO verbatim, on the live app at v2.61.1: *"you dont have to provide sub text in bracket"*. **(1) THE MEASURED CASE.** The S25 SOURCE cell read `Walk-by (from the old source tag) · Manual entry · Walk-by` on every row — where r10 RDS-01 draws **one word** (`AMI`, `Walk`). The bracket was `metering.reads.readMethod.derivedSuffix`, which explains OUR OWN data migration (BR-MET-142's legacy `source_detail` translation) to a clerk who has never had an *old source tag*. **(2) THE RULE.** A list column answers ONE question in as few words as the value allows. The reason a value was DERIVED rather than recorded — `read_method_origin` — is detail for a reader who has asked for detail, i.e. the reading detail page (S29). **(3) THE FACT IS NOT DELETED, IT MOVES.** S29's read-method line states the origin in the same words, so nothing a clerk could learn from the bracket is lost; it is simply not repeated at them fifty rows at a time. ⚠ **A THIRD PART LEFT THE CELL WITH IT AND IT WAS A LITERAL DUPLICATE:** `source_detail` printed the SAME WORD as the method on every legacy row (`Walk-by … · Walk-by`), because a DERIVED method is derived *from* `source_detail`. It is on S29 as its own tag and is unchanged there. **(4) THE TRANSPORT STAYS IN THE CELL** (`· Manual entry`) because it is a different fact about the reading and not a footnote about our own history; BR-MET-123 makes source-tag and transport two things a clerk genuinely asks about. ⚠ **WHY NO GATE COULD SEE THIS.** `derivedSuffix` exists, is translated in all eleven catalogues and passes G15/G18 perfectly. **A string can be flawlessly localised and still be the wrong thing to say** — which is B14's own "RATIFIED and UNENFORCED" column and hard-won law 6 in one sentence. | PO 2026-08-27 (DEF-895) · r10 RDS-01 (the drawn column) · BR-MET-142 (the legacy translation the bracket was about) · B14 rule 12 | **CONFIRMED** | `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ReadsPage.test.tsx` (the SOURCE cell asserted to carry NO `derivedSuffix`, in both directions) · `ReadDetailPage.test.tsx` (the origin asserted PRESENT on S29) |
| BR-MET-161 | **A STORED LABEL RENDERS AS STORED — NO FIELD PREFIXES A VALUE WITH ITS OWN NOUN (PO 2026-08-27, second half of DEF-899).** PO verbatim, reporting the reading detail page: *"on reading detail page - decimal places databse keys"*, and on the same screen the premise line read **`Unit Unit 1`**. **(1) THE CAUSE.** `metering.read.linkage.unit` was `Unit {{unit}}`, and the tenant's own unit label already begins with the word *Unit* — as the seeded estate's do, and as most utilities' do. A label is TENANT DATA (BR-MET-034's estate is the tenant's own), so no template can know whether the noun is already in it. **(2) THE RULE.** The meaning of a field comes from its LABEL, its column header or its section heading — never from a word concatenated in front of the value. `Unit 1` renders `Unit 1`; `A` renders `A`. **(3) WHERE THE CONTEXT LIVES INSTEAD.** On S29 the section is already headed *Premise / unit / account*; in the S25 premise cell the unit rides as the `·`-separated qualifier r10 draws (`1408 Elm · A`). Both were already right — the detail page was the one surface that added the word. **(4) THE GENERAL SHAPE.** This is the same failure as BR-MET-160 one field over: copy written about the FIELD LIST rather than about what a reader will see when real data lands in it. | PO 2026-08-27 (DEF-899) · BR-MET-034 (the estate is the tenant's own) · r10 RDS-01/RDS-04 | **CONFIRMED** | `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ReadDetailPage.test.tsx` (a unit labelled `Unit 1` asserted to render exactly once, and `"Unit Unit"` asserted absent) |
| BR-MET-162 | **A ROUTE SHEET IS PRINTED FOR A ROUTE *OR* FOR A CYCLE, AND A CYCLE SHEET IS THE UNION OF ITS ROUTES' STOPS IN WALK ORDER (PO 2026-08-27, closing DEF-898).** PO verbatim: *"allow route of Cycle - both"*. **(1) WHY THE FEATURE WAS HALF-BUILT AND NOTHING CAUGHT IT.** CR-038 W5 shipped the sheet keyed on the route it is NAMED after, and every test asks for a route sheet by route and gets one. **A missing ENTRY POINT is invisible to any test that only exercises the entry point that exists** — only somebody who works the other way round notices. **(2) THE CYCLE IS ALREADY A FACT OF THE SHEET**, printed in its header (`Cycle SCW-M · System (auto-pull) · 41 stop(s)`), which is exactly why it could not also be a way to ask for one: a first-class fact with no way in. **(3) THE SHAPE OF A CYCLE SHEET.** It is the union of the stops of the cycle's ACTIVE routes (`routes.cycle_id`, BR-MET-010 — one cycle per route at a time), **grouped by route and, within each route, in that route's own walk order** (D11 · `walk_order.premises_order_for_route`). It is NOT a re-sequenced merge: a walk order belongs to a route, there is no ruled ordering ACROSS routes, and inventing one would print a sequence nobody walks. Routes are sectioned in `code` order so two prints of the same cycle are identical. **(4) A PREMISES ON TWO ROUTES APPEARS ONCE PER ROUTE**, with that route's meters — because a reader really does visit it on both walks, and de-duplicating would drop a register from one of them. **(5) DRAFT AND INACTIVE ROUTES ARE EXCLUDED** (BR-MET-076): a draft route stamps no service point, so Reads cannot see it, and an inactive route is one nobody walks. **(6) NOTHING ELSE CHANGES** — same stops, same meters, same last-read-with-its-unit, same print-ready HTML, same `AnyTenantRole` read. **(7) THE GENERAL LESSON, WRITTEN DOWN BECAUSE IT RECURS:** where a feature is keyed on ONE dimension of a pair this product treats as equals — route and cycle, meter and account — the story states which entry points exist and which deliberately do not. | PO 2026-08-27 (DEF-898) · D11 (per-premises walk order) · BR-MET-010 (one cycle per route) · BR-MET-076 (draft/active/inactive) · BR-MET-033 (membership) | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_def_898_cycle_route_sheet.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ReadsPage.test.tsx` (the dialog offers both and refuses neither-chosen) |
| BR-MET-163 | **THE COMPLIANCE STAMP RENDERS PEOPLE AS PEOPLE — AN ACTOR ID IS NEVER THE VISIBLE VALUE OF A FIELD (PO 2026-08-27, first half of DEF-899).** PO verbatim: *"on reading detail page - decimal places databse keys"*. **(1) THE MEASURED CASE.** S29's COMPLIANCE METADATA printed `Uploaded by  01a01d99-d617-7aa9-9f07-e2c08cc4fdc9` — the uploader's primary key — while the row directly beneath it, *Uploaded at*, was formatted for a human. **The panel was half-translated: the timestamp was thought about and the actor was not.** **(2) THE RULE.** `uploaded_by` and `cosigned_by` are rendered as the person's NAME. Where the id cannot be resolved to a person — a user removed from the tenant, an actor from before the row existed — the field says so **in words**, and the id is available on the element's `title` as evidence, never as the visible value. **(3) THE NAME IS RESOLVED SERVER-SIDE, THROUGH THE SEAM THE AUDIT TRAIL ALREADY USES** (`apps.audit.selectors.actor_names`, one tenant-scoped query for the whole payload). It could not be resolved in the browser: `GET /users` is **SystemAdminOnly**, and the reader this defect is about is a Billing Clerk — so a client-side lookup would 403 for exactly the person who needs the answer. **(4) `uploaded_by` AND `cosigned_by` KEEP THEIR RAW-ID SHAPE ON THE WIRE** and gain `uploaded_by_name` / `cosigned_by_name` beside them, so no existing consumer of the contract breaks and the evidence is still served. **(5) A NULL NAME IS NOT AN ERROR.** BR-IDN-030 keeps user rows, but the stamp outlives any row, and BR-AUD-003's posture is already *the id still renders, the name stays null* — this rule only says which of the two a PERSON is shown. ⚠ **WHY NO GATE COULD SEE IT.** A UUID is a valid string: the field is present, populated, correctly typed and rendered without error, and no check in this repo can tell a primary key from a name because to the code they are the same type. ⚠ **AND THE CODE HAD ALREADY WRITTEN THE FIX DOWN AND NOT DONE IT** — `selectors.read_batch_dict`'s own comment said *"The screen resolves people through identity's own endpoint, as it already does for the uploader"*, and the screen did no such thing and could not have (see (3)). Hard-won law 1: a comment claiming a guarantee is not the guarantee. | PO 2026-08-27 (DEF-899) · BR-MET-017 (the permanent stamp) · BR-AUD-003 (a null actor is a system job) · BR-IDN-030 · hard-won law 1 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_def_899_uploader_name.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ReadDetailPage.test.tsx` (the name rendered, the raw id asserted ABSENT from the visible text, and the unresolvable case rendered in words) |
