A free-text note column on this table would re-introduce exactly what was rejected, one row lower down. Do not add one. **(3) THE CATEGORY IS REQUIRED AND THE CUSTOMER LINK IS NOT.** A record with no category is the free-text box again. A record with no customer is a real state — the ruling's own caveat: *"a builder holding a site before handover may not be [a customer], so the link is optional and its absence is a stated fact rather than a gap."* **(4) CONTACT DETAILS ARE OPTIONAL AND THAT IS NOT SETTLED** — `OQ-503`. The ruling names them; it does not say a bill-bounce path may be empty. **(5) THE CATEGORY VOCABULARY IS TENANT-EXTENSIBLE**, per BR-TEN-150 and the DB-14 house pattern — `OQ-502` asks the PO to close or confirm it. **(6) THIS RULE ROUTES NO MONEY BY ITSELF.** `customers.services.open_account` still reads `premises.owner_manager` for the default payer; whether the typed owner supersedes it is `OQ-500` and is NOT decided here. | PO 2026-08-27 (decision log *"an owner is a TYPED record with a category"*) · CR-039 finding 4 · CR-039 wave C tasks 12–14 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr039_c12_premises_owner.py` |

A category is chosen by a person, on the review surface, one name at a time. **(4) MATCHING LEAVES `owner_manager` WHERE IT IS.** It is the provenance of the match (stamped on the owner row as `source_owner_manager`) and it is what `customers.services.open_account` reads today — retiring it is `OQ-501` and dropping it would change who a new account bills. **(5) THE FREE-TEXT BOX IS STILL WRITABLE.** Removing it before every tenant is matched would strand the names this rule exists to preserve. | PO 2026-08-27 (the ruling's second unsettled caveat, quoted in full above) · CR-039 wave C task 13 · CLAUDE.md hard rule 4 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr039_c13_owner_migration.py` |
| BR-MET-152 | **A SEEDED OWNER'S CATEGORY IS STATED BY THE FIXTURE, AND WHERE NOTHING STATES ONE THE PREMISES IS SEEDED UNMATCHED.** CR-039 wave C task 14 is *"seed several owner kinds"*, and the fixture already invents the owner NAMES — so stating their category is the same act by the same author, in one place, with its basis written beside it. **(1) THREE KINDS ARE SEEDED, EACH FROM A FACT THE CORPUS ALREADY HOLDS.** `builder` — *Ridge Property LLC* at `500 Main St`, which BR-CUS-083 already calls *"the developer, via a master account"* on a 25-unit build. `owner_occupier` — *Creekside Laundromat* and *Quarry Hill Diner*, where the fixture makes the BUSINESS TRADING AT the address its own owner and each premises holds exactly one commercial suite, so owner and occupant are the same party by construction. `landlord` — *Mill Pond Apartments LP* (six let flats) and *Silver Creek Duplex Holdings* (four let duplex halves), each an owner who occupies none of the units it lets. **(2) TWO KINDS ARE REFUSED BY NAME.** `property_manager` asserts a managing AGENT acting for an owner, and nothing in the corpus names one at any address. `hoa` asserts an association, and nothing names one either. Seeding either would state a second party that has never been mentioned — wave E's own posture (*"a seeded value nobody chose is worse than a blank, because a demo makes it look decided"*), applied to a category instead of a column. **(3) THE SEEDER NEVER DERIVES A CATEGORY FROM A STRING** (BR-MET-151 clause 3); it reads what the fixture states and nothing else. **(4) THE UNMATCHED PREMISES ARE DELIBERATE.** `seed_demo`'s six named owners are seeded as free text with no owner row, so the review surface BR-MET-151 requires has real rows to show on a fresh demo — the same reason the sample COMMERCIAL plan deliberately states no outside-city factor. | CR-039 wave C task 14 · BR-CUS-083 (*"the developer, via a master account"*) · CR-039 wave E's per-field refusal posture | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr039_c14_seeded_owners.py` |

| BR-MET-400 | **THE PREMISES OWNER *IS* THE MASTER ACCOUNT — A DECLARED LINK, NOT AN INFERRED ONE (PO ruling 2026-08-27, closing `OQ-500`; `DEF-650` critical).** PO verbatim, in full: *"so premises owner is master accountin system. you ahve to showall details form account - Bascially Premises -> owner = Master account - it can be existing master account also so at the time of premises creation we should check - how you will handle this"*. **THE CHAIN, AND ONLY THE LAST LINK IS NEW:** `Premises.owner` → `PremisesOwner` (BR-MET-150) → **`premises_owners.master_account_id`** → `customers.accounts`. The decision log measured it and said so: *"THE ONE MISSING LINK is `owner → master ACCOUNT`. Today nothing joins a `premises_owners` row to an `accounts` row of kind `master`."* **WHY IT IS CRITICAL AND NOT COSMETIC:** `BR-MET-105` (PO 2026-08-24) sends the empty-unit base charge — worked at **$30.50/month** — to *"the owner / landlord"*, and a charge cannot be posted to a string. The move-out wizard printed the limitation in its own shipped copy: *"Owner is free text on the premise record, not an account. That limits where a shortfall can be sent."* **WHAT IT REPLACES:** `BR-CUS-171`'s GEOMETRIC derivation, which found the master by walking the premises' COMMON AREAS and taking whoever pays for the hallway. **RETIRED, not kept as a fallback** (PO decision 4) — *"a fallback that disagrees with the declared owner would silently bill a different party"* — and the two failures it had are gone by construction rather than by a better tie-break: a premises with **no common area** (a single-family home) had no master at all, and **two masters over one premises** made `master_account_for` answer `None`. **SHOW THE DETAILS FROM THE ACCOUNT, NEVER A COPY.** *"you ahve to showall details form account"* — the owner read carries the account's number, kind, payer and balance, fetched through `apps.metering.seams` at the moment of asking. Nothing is copied onto a metering row, so there is no second writable copy to drift, which is the complaint `OQ-501` files against the free-text name. ⚠⚠ **SUPERSEDED AT THE NEXT SENTENCE 2026-09-01 (`CR-056` R1+R2, PO ruling 2026-08-31).** The clause below reads *master-ness is derived from occupancy and never chosen at creation*, and that is exactly the 2026-08-27 reading the PO reversed: **every address gets its owner a Master Account, whether or not anything is shared, and whether or not the owner lives there.** `OQ-790` had already closed on 2026-08-27 (option (c), master immediately) leaving one obstacle — a master needed a common area to anchor on — and `CR-056` W1 removes it: `accounts.premises_id` exists and `accounts.unit_id` is nullable, so `ensure_owner_master_account` no longer raises. **The link this row is about is unchanged**: `premises_owners.master_account_id` still points at the account, and the account is still found by declaration and never by walking hallways. ⚠ **THE COLUMN IS NULLABLE AND STAYS NULLABLE.** Every owner row predates it, and **when the account comes into existence and whether it is `master` or `regular` was `OQ-790`** — the same PO ruled master-ness *"derived from whether anyone else occupies the premises"* and *"never chosen at creation"*, and at premises creation nobody occupies anything. ⚠⚠ **NARROWED IN ITS CHAIN — NOT IN ITS PRINCIPLE — ON 2026-09-22 BY `BR-MET-1181` (PO ruling), AND THE NARROWING IS STATED HERE RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE INFERRED FROM A COLUMN APPEARING.** PO verbatim: *"why you are point is at owner id - why not directly at master accoutn Id - it will solev the problem … owner IDs are not required hence no screens or fucntionality for it"*. **WHAT MOVES:** the chain this row draws — `Premises.owner → PremisesOwner → premises_owners.master_account_id → accounts` — loses its middle hop for every link written from 2026-09-22 onward. A premises now names the account itself (`premises.master_account_id`) and **that declaration wins**; `premises_owners.master_account_id` is read SECOND, for the rows written before the ruling, and is still WRITTEN beside the new column wherever a typed owner exists — so nothing that reads it answers less. `accounts.premises_id` is THIRD and is **never promoted**: it says which premises an account COVERS, and a tenant's ordinary account covers a premises it does not own. **WHAT DOES NOT MOVE, AND IT IS THIS ROW'S WHOLE POINT:** *declared, never inferred*. Both columns the new precedence reads are DECLARATIONS; the hallway derivation stays retired, for this row's own stated reason — *"a fallback that disagrees with the declared owner would silently bill a different party"* — and that sentence is exactly why the ORDER is RULED in the decision log instead of being left to the reading order of a function. ⚠ **AND THE CLAUSE ABOUT `OQ-790` DIRECTLY ABOVE IS STALE, WHICH IS CORRECTED HERE RATHER THAN QUIETLY DELETED (hard-won law 1):** `OQ-790` CLOSED on 2026-08-27 on option (c), MASTER IMMEDIATELY, and the PO reversed *"derived from whether anyone else occupies the premises"* on 2026-08-31 (`CR-056`). It is left standing above because it is what this row told every lane for twenty-six days. | PO 2026-08-27 (OQ-500, and decisions 1-4 of its second half) · PO 2026-08-31 (`CR-056`) · PO 2026-09-22 (`BR-MET-1181`, `DEF-MET-6139`) · BR-MET-105 · BR-CUS-171 (superseded) · BR-MET-150 · DEF-650 | **CONFIRMED (the principle) / NARROWED IN ITS CHAIN 2026-09-22 (`BR-MET-1181`)** | `06-backend/tests/test_def650_owner_is_the_master_account.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_1181_premises_master_account.py` |
| BR-MET-401 | **AT PREMISES CREATION THE OWNER IS REQUIRED, AND AN EXISTING PARTY IS REUSED RATHER THAN DUPLICATED (PO ruling 2026-08-27, decisions 1 and 3).** Decision 1, verbatim: *"when presmies are created you have owner for it, without owner how you can create premsies = owner = master account"*. **THREE ANSWERS AND NO FOURTH** (`resolve_owner_name`): **EXACT** — the party exists and is REUSED whole, with its master account and every other address that points at it. The key is the DATABASE's own `uq_premises_owners_tenant_name_ci`, so this is a lookup and not a heuristic, and the decision log says so: *"the match key the PO asks for already exists"*. **NEAR** — OFFERED, and the clerk decides (decision 3, verbatim: *"Near match on the name - (b) offer the match, clerk decides"*); nothing resolves, nothing pre-selects, nothing auto-merges, because *"two landlords with similar names are two parties, and merging them bills the wrong one"*. **NONE** — the caller creates the party WITH A CATEGORY IT SUPPLIES; no category is ever read off a name (BR-MET-151 clause 3). ⚠ **THE REFUSAL IS ON CREATE ONLY, AND THAT IS NOT A LOOPHOLE.** Every premises on every tenant predates the typed owner and `metering 0041` backfills none of them, so a required field on PATCH would make a ten-year-old address unsaveable for a reason about OUR schema. Those rows stay listed by `unmatched_premises_qs()` until a person matches them. ⚠ **NEAR-MATCH DETECTION STRIPS LEGAL FORMS ONLY** — `llc` · `inc` · `corp` · `ltd` · `lp` and the rest — and NOT trade words. Stripping `property`/`holdings` collapsed *Ridge Property LLC* and *Ridge Holdings LLC* onto one key; offering two unrelated landlords never merges anything, but it teaches a clerk to dismiss the offer, and an offer nobody reads is decision 3 defeated by noise. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-08-27 — NARROWED TO THE CREATE SCREEN. THE BULK IMPORT ACCEPTS A BLANK OWNER AND FLAGS IT.** PO verbatim, the same day: *"in premises - 2.4 Does the owner name match a party already on file? what if owner column is empty - in such case we create the premises and keep owner name empty - we cna edit and updaste it later"*. **THIS CONTRADICTS DECISION 1 ABOVE AND THE SUPERSESSION IS STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED** — decision 1 is asked of a CLERK filling in a form, who knows the answer or can find it; this is asked of a COLUMN IN A SPREADSHEET, of which the PO's own words are *"we cna edit and updaste it later"*. **The narrowing is minimal on purpose and the rule already distinguished contexts for the identical reason** (*"THE REFUSAL IS ON CREATE ONLY, AND THAT IS NOT A LOOPHOLE"* — a required field on PATCH would make a ten-year-old address unsaveable); import-create is a third context with the same problem at a different scale. **The measured consequence of the wider reading:** a migration is thousands of rows and half the owner answers are in a filing cabinet, so refusing them means a utility cannot load its own address book until it finishes a research project — and the addresses it cannot load are the ones its meters and accounts then cannot name either. **SO `resolve_owner_name` HAS FOUR ANSWERS AND NO FIFTH:** `empty` (NEW — the column names nobody: the premises LOADS with no owner, flagged) · ~~`near` (rejected, unchanged)~~ → **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-31 (CR-055 → `near` LOADS the premises with no owner, flagged, exactly as `empty` does — the amendment at the end of this cell is the current reading). ⛔ `DEF-2877`, corrected 2026-09-01: this sentence was left unstamped when that amendment was APPENDED, so one rule cell carried two opposite answers and a reader stopping at the first got the reversed one.** · `exact` (the party is reused whole, with the account it already has — **no second account**) · `none` (nobody by that name). ⚠ **`empty` USED TO BE SPELLED `none`, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT FACT** — *"the column is empty"* and *"nobody is called that"* lead to opposite next actions, and one word for both is how the empty case went unnoticed until the PO read the picture. ⚠ **WHAT A BLANK OWNER COSTS, STATED SO IT IS A CHOICE AND NOT A SURPRISE:** no owner → **no master account** → **that address has nobody to charge**. It is a real premises — units, meters, readings — and it cannot be BILLED until somebody fills the owner in. The rows are **COUNTED ON THE IMPORT RECEIPT** (`premises_with_no_owner`) on the day of the run. ⛔ **THIS SENTENCE ALSO SAID THEY ARE *“listed by `unmatched_premises_qs()`”* AND THAT HALF WAS NEVER TRUE — `DEF-2793`, corrected 2026-09-01.** That queryset EXCLUDES a null or empty `owner_manager` and a blank Owner column writes none, so the two conditions are mutually exclusive and a blank-owner premises is on that list on no day, for no tenant. The NEAR case does land there because it keeps the operator's string, which is why every existing test passed and why the claim read as covered (hard-won law 1). **So the honest state is: counted on the receipt, then on NO standing worklist** — `unmatched_premises_qs()` does not hold it and S14 offers no *no owner* filter to find it with. Widening what *unmatched* means moves the S14 badge, the PRM-04 list and its count — three surfaces CR-055 does not name — so it is a CHANGE and not a fix; `test_cr055__DEF_2795_a_BLANK_owner_premises_is_NOT_on_the_unmatched_list` pins the product as it is and inverts the day it is widened. `PRM.OWNER_COLUMN_EMPTY` is the rule row that says so on the screen. ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-08-31 — CR-055. THE IMPORT NO LONGER REJECTS A NEAR MATCH EITHER, SO THE PREMISES UPLOAD CANNOT REJECT A ROW ON OWNER GROUNDS AT ALL.** PO ruling, in the CR's own words: *"an uploaded owner name that matches no owner exactly but resembles two or more existing owners now LOADS THE PREMISES WITH NO OWNER, flagged for someone to fill in later — exactly as a blank owner column already does. The row is no longer rejected."* **THE SCOPE, STATED SO IT IS NOT READ WIDER THAN IT IS — AND IT IS THE SAME NARROWING THIS RULE ALREADY CARRIES TWICE.** ⚠ **THE CREATE SCREEN IS UNTOUCHED:** `create_premises` still refuses a premises with no owner, because a clerk filling in a form knows the answer or can find it. That is now the THIRD context this rule distinguishes (create refuses · update never did · import accepts), for the identical reason each time. ⚠ **DECISION 3 IS NOT REVERSED.** *"Offer the match, clerk decides"* says the import may not PICK, and the import still does not pick — `_resolve_owner_ref` resolves EXACT names only, so a near name writes no `owner_id`, ever. What moved is the CONSEQUENCE of refusing to choose: the row loads ownerless instead of being thrown away. **Picking the closest candidate was put to the PO as an option and DECLINED**, because the owner carries the master account and a wrong pick eventually sends somebody else's bill to the wrong party. ⚠ **`resolve_owner_name` STILL HAS FOUR ANSWERS AND NO FIFTH, UNCHANGED** — four callers share it and CR-055 changed none of them. The change is what the IMPORT does with `near`, not what the resolver says. **SO THE FOUR ANSWERS ON THE IMPORT NOW READ:** `empty` → loads with no owner, flagged · `near` → **loads with no owner, flagged** (was: row rejected) · `exact` → the party is reused whole · `none` → loads, keeps the name, waits for matching. ⚠ **THE COST IS THE SAME COST AS THE BLANK CASE AND IT IS NOW PAID BY MORE ROWS:** no owner → no master account → **nobody to charge**. The mitigation is the receipt, and CR-055 splits it in two — `premises_owner_blank` (needs RESEARCH) and `premises_owner_ambiguous` (needs a DECISION between two named parties, whose names the run carries in `premises_owner_ambiguous_names`) — because merging them into one figure throws away the half an operator can settle from the screen today. `PRM.OWNER_NEAR_MATCH` is `WARNING` + `FLAG_ONLY` from this date. | PO 2026-08-27 (decisions 1 and 3; the blank-column narrowing the same day) · **PO 2026-08-31 (CR-055 — the near case joins the blank case)** · BR-MET-150/151 · DEF-650 · CR-046 C2 | **CONFIRMED · AMENDED 2026-08-27 (narrowed to the create screen) · AMENDED 2026-08-31 (CR-055 — the import rejects no row on owner grounds)** | `06-backend/tests/test_def650_owner_is_the_master_account.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr055_owner_never_rejects.py` |
| BR-MET-402 | **THE BULK IMPORT RESOLVES THE OWNER TO A PARTY, AND REFUSES AN AMBIGUITY RATHER THAN CHOOSING (`DEF-1088` high, `DEF-1247` high).** The PO raised this case themselves: *"we will need to careful what we do when we upload presmies in the system first time"*. **(1) THE `Owner` HEADER NOW REACHES THE TYPED OWNER**, and `Owner name (legacy)` / `owner/manager` / `manager` reach the free-text column. It used to be the other way round, which — once the model-derived offered set started shipping BOTH headers (BR-MET-088) — left the `owner_manager` header unclaimed and fed the free-text field from the typed column's own header. **(2) RE-POINTING A HEADER UTILITIES ALREADY SHIP IS SAFE HERE *BECAUSE NOTHING IS LOST*:** the load writes **both** — the typed link when the party exists, and the operator's own string into `owner_manager` as BR-MET-151 clause 4 provenance. A file that used to fill one column now fills that same column **and** resolves a party; it is a strict addition, not a redirection. **(3) A NEAR MATCH REJECTS THE ROW** and names the candidate. An import has no clerk in front of it, and both ways of resolving an ambiguity on the operator's behalf are forbidden — merging bills the wrong landlord (decision 3), and creating a second party beside the first is the *"twelve unrelated strings"* the typed record exists to stop. **(4) AN UNKNOWN NAME LOADS, FLAGGED AND COUNTED** — an ERROR here would refuse every row of every FIRST upload, since a new tenant holds no typed owners at all. The string is kept as provenance and the address appears on PRM-04, where a PERSON supplies the category the import may not invent. **(5) THE IMPORT NEVER CREATES A PARTY FROM A NAME ALONE.** ⛔ **NARROWED 2026-09-19 BY `BR-MET-1175` — THIS CLAUSE READ *"THE IMPORT NEVER CREATES A PARTY"*, UNCONDITIONALLY, AND ONE THIRD OF ITS PREMISE IS NOW FALSE.** It gave three reasons: `premises_owners.category_id` is NOT NULL, BR-MET-151 clause 3 forbids reading a category off a name, **and `premises` has no category column to map one from**. The first two STAND. The third was removed by PO ruling on 2026-09-19 — *"Yes add owner category column in csv template"* — so a row that **STATES** a category now creates the party and links it. **Being TOLD a category was never the same act as reading one off a name**, which is why this is a narrowing and not a reversal: *Whitfield Rentals LLC* is still not proof of a landlord, and a row that states nothing still creates no party. ⚠ **AND THE ORDER MATTERS:** this ships WITH BR-MET-401's mandatory field and never behind it — DEF-1088 states it in as many words. | PO 2026-08-27 (decision 3; the first-upload concern is the PO's own sentence) · BR-MET-088 · BR-MET-151 · DEF-1088 · DEF-1247 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_def650_owner_is_the_master_account.py` |
| BR-MET-403 | **A PREMISE NUMBER IS ISSUED FROM THE TENANT'S SERIES, NEVER TAKEN FROM AN UPLOADED FILE — AND THE UTILITY'S OWN REFERENCE GETS A COLUMN OF ITS OWN (PO ruling 2026-08-27, widened to all four uploads by `CR-046`).** PO verbatim, of the meters upload and then of all four: *"here we should not allow usse to fill in Asset ID - we have number system set in the settings, we shoudl call that function and get unique number and assign it"* · *"you ahve understood the happy path for Meters - can you use similar logic and update the upload logic for all 4 entities we discussed"*. **FOUR CLAUSES.** **(1) THE NUMBER IS OURS.** A new premises takes `premise_no` from the `premises` series in Settings › Numbers, **inside the write transaction** — so a rejected row burns no number and a rollback returns the counter. The PO's own words about WHEN: *"these decisiona should be taken just before updating teh record in the database"*. **(2) THE COLUMN STAYS, WITH A NEW MEANING:** *"an EXISTING number, if this row updates a premises already in SMARTBillHub — blank for a new one"*. **Blank is the normal case and the only case in Insert mode**, and the sample cell is an explicitly declared `""` (`sample=None` falls through to a kind-generic worked value and would ship an invented number — `DEF-962`). It is NOT deleted, because it is the **match key**. **(3) THE WRITE MODE DECIDES A KNOWN RECORD'S FATE** — *Insert new only* → **REJECTED** (`PRM.PREMISE_NO_ON_FILE` becomes an ERROR for the run, `_for_write_mode`); *Insert & update* → **the details are updated**, and an update never clears the number a blank cell did not mention. A number that is **not** on file is an INVENTED one and is refused in both modes (`PRM.PREMISE_NO_NOT_ON_FILE`). **(4) `external_ref` IS THE UTILITY'S OWN REFERENCE AND IS UNIQUE PER TENANT**, case-insensitively and partially (`uq_premises_tenant_external_ref_ci`, metering 0042) — which is what makes it a lawful MATCH KEY, and it is the only identifier a first migration's corrected re-run carries. **Worked:** 428 premises loaded as `P-00101 … P-00528`; the operator finds nine wrong postal codes and re-cuts the SAME file, which holds no SMARTBillHub number because it never did. Without a unique legacy key: **428 second premises**, two records per address, no way to tell the sets apart. With it: **428 matches, 9 updates, 419 no-ops.** ⚠ **A CROSS-FILE REFERENCE IS NOT AN IDENTIFIER** — a unit NAMES a premises, and that reference points at a record we numbered, so it is quoted rather than invented and is untouched by this rule. | PO 2026-08-27 · `CR-046` C2/C5 · the signed premises walkthrough · BR-AST-049 (the meters precedent) · BR-CUS-054 (the original `external_ref` posture) · BR-TEN-035 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr046_identifiers.py` |
| BR-MET-404 | **A UNIT'S IDENTITY IS ITS LABEL WITHIN ITS PREMISES — THE PAIR, NEVER THE LABEL ALONE — AND ITS NUMBER IS ISSUED FROM THE TENANT'S SERIES (PO ruling 2026-08-27; `CR-046` C3 asked the question and the PO settled it).** PO verbatim: *"premsies and unit name - not just unit name that combination is unique"*. **THE DATABASE SAYS THE SAME THING AND SAID IT FIRST**, which is what `CR-046` C3 instructed the lane to build to: `uq_units_tenant_premises_label` is over two **NOT NULL** columns, so every unit that exists carries that key; `uq_units_tenant_unit_no` is over a **NULLABLE** column, so N units may carry no number at all and still be lawful rows. **A column that can be absent cannot be an identity.** **CONSEQUENCES, each of which is a code change:** **(1) `UNT.LABEL_UNIQUE_IN_PREMISE` IS RAISED FROM WARNING TO ERROR** — two rows naming one `(premises, label)` are two rows claiming to be the same unit. **(2) IT IS SPLIT IN TWO, AND ONLY THE IN-FILE HALF CAN BE THE ERROR** (`DEF-543`'s lesson from the other direction): the on-file half is `UNT.PAIR_ON_FILE`, whose severity is the RUN'S — ERROR under *Insert new only*, a flag under *Insert & update*, which is the row that mode exists for. **(3) THE MATCH KEY INCLUDES THE PREMISES, AND LEAVING IT OUT IS A SILENT OVERWRITE.** Two buildings each holding an `Apt 3B` are two units; matching on the label alone would find the OTHER building's flat and, under *Insert & update*, quietly rewrite it — a row that loads clean, reports `updated`, and is wrong. **It is the failure that never looks like a failure**, and it has a test of its own. **(4) THE NUMBER IS ISSUED, NOT READ OFF THE FILE** — `unit_no` from the `unit` series, inside the write, with the same four clauses as BR-MET-403; the row's PREMISES reference is a quote of a number we issued and is unchanged. **(5) A SECOND UNIQUENESS CHECK RUNS IMMEDIATELY BEFORE THE WRITE**, and it is NOT the series' guarantee restated: the series is unique AT ISSUE, and this asserts *this premises does not already hold a unit with the issued number* — catching a series renumbered or reset, a number typed by hand on the screen, and two runs racing on one address. A number used twice is a unit two things can point at, and a meter or an account pointing at the wrong flat is not noticed until a bill arrives. **(6) `units.external_ref` IS NEW** (metering 0042), unique per tenant, case-insensitive, partial. ⚠ **(7) THE THREE RULE CODES CLAUSES 4 AND 6 IMPLY ARE NAMED HERE, BECAUSE `CR-046` C3 RAISED THAT THEY WERE NOT** (`CR-046` wave H3, 2026-08-27). The signed unit walkthrough's eight-row rule table lists neither, and clause 4's *"the same four clauses as BR-MET-403"* is a cross-reference rather than a name — so a lane greping the rule file for what the units target enforces found six of the nine built codes. **They are `UNT.NUMBER_ON_FILE`** (a number the file supplies that IS on file: ERROR in *Insert new only*, the match key in *Insert & update* — `_for_write_mode`), **`UNT.NUMBER_NOT_ON_FILE`** (a number that is NOT on file is an INVENTED one and is refused in BOTH modes) and **`UNT.EXTERNAL_REF_UNIQUE`** (the in-file half of `uq_units_tenant_external_ref_ci`, the same posture the other five targets carry). All three are built and covered — `import_rules.PLATFORM_CATALOG["units"]` and `test_cr046_identifiers.py`; this clause is the RULE catching up with the CODE, and nothing about the behaviour moved. ⚠ **SERVICES ARE INHERITED FROM THE PREMISES BY REFERENCE AND NOTHING IS COPIED AT IMPORT OR EVER** — `Unit` has no services column, the payloads serve `premises.services_available` read live (`DEF-755`), and a copy would let a unit and its own address disagree about what can be sold there. ⚠ **A PLAN IS RESOLVED BY ID/CODE *OR* BY LABEL/NAME AND NEITHER IS REQUIRED** — an unknown or absent plan loads the unit UNPLANNED and flagged (`BR-MET-100`): the address is structure, the plan is pricing you can add later. | PO 2026-08-27 · `CR-046` C3/C5 · the signed unit walkthrough · `Unit.Meta.constraints` · BR-MET-100 · DEF-755 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr046_identifiers.py` |
| BR-MET-500 | **METERING PUBLISHES THE TWO PER-CONNECTION FACTS BILLING HAS BEEN RESOLVING BY NAME AND NEVER RECEIVING: the CONNECTION SIZE (`connection_size_for(unit_id, service_code)`) and the METER MULTIPLIER (`billing_reads_for_unit`'s `multiplier` key, plus `billing_multiplier_for` for a caller holding no reading row).** ⚠ **NEITHER CALLER CHANGED, AND THAT IS THE EVIDENCE THE HOP WAS ALWAYS BUILT.** `apps/billing/gather.py` has resolved `connection_size_for` through `_resolve("metering", ...)` since 2026-08-21 and got `None` every time; its docstring staked the claim in terms — *"the day metering publishes `connection_size_for` the size reaches the bill and nothing here changes"* — and nothing there changed. **BOTH TAKE `(unit_id, service_code)` AND NOT AN ACCOUNT**, because a house on water and electric has TWO connections that need not be the same size; a per-account answer could only ever be one of them, and the per-size ladder is per SCHEDULE, which is per service. **THE SIZE IS READ THROUGH THE ASSETS SEAM, NEVER BY JOINING** — assets owns the meter, metering owns the service point, and B1 draws the boundary at exactly that join; billing reaches assets through this module and never directly. **TWO METERS ON ONE SERVICE POINT RECORDING DIFFERENT SIZES ANSWER `None`**: one connection has one size, two different recorded sizes is a data error, and the only alternatives are *pick one* — which prices a bill on a coin toss — or *say it cannot tell*. Absence is NOT a competing claim, so a meter with no size beside a meter with one leaves the recorded size winning. **THE MULTIPLIER IS NEVER DEFAULTED TO 1.** BR-MET-056 stores the factor USED and the seam publishes that figure or nothing: `resolution.MeterMultiplierUnstated` exists for this and says *"an absent factor is not 1"*. Measured before the key landed: on a ×10 meter this seam published `consumption=500.000000` and `current − prior = 50` and **nothing told the caller which factor produced the 500** — the live path prices the 500, the R1–R7 chain scales the 50 itself (BR-RAT-001), and until both were on one row a caller could not choose. ⚠ **A DEFAULTED 1 IS THE WORST OPTION AVAILABLE, NOT THE CONVENIENT ONE:** ×1 of the register difference equals metering's stored figure exactly when the multiplier really is 1, so the R1–R7 shadow would report PARITY ON EVERY ACCOUNT by construction and its report would read as the best possible news while measuring nothing. | DEF-1811 · DEF-1812 · DEF-514 · DEF-484 · step 1 of `12-governance/19-execution-sequence.md` | **CONFIRMED** (lane judgment; the seam NAMES and signatures were specified by `gather.py` and `resolution_shadow.py` before this lane existed and were not chosen here — the ambiguity ruling and the never-default posture are this lane's, recorded in `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` 2026-08-27) | `06-backend/tests/test_def_1900_connection_size.py` TC-3..6, TC-8, TC-9 · `06-backend/tests/test_def_1810_disagreements.py::test_DEF_1812_no_meter_multiplier_crosses_metering_s_BILLING_seam` |
| BR-MET-710 | ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (`CR-062` D6, PO ruling) — THE SAME ROW NOW ALSO CARRIES A CAPACITY THAT NO ASSET HOLDS.** `_stamp_installed_capacity` branches once, by name, on `connections.unit_capacity_key`: for **storm water** the `installed_capacity` is read from a `service_characteristics` row on the UNIT (key `assessment`, `ERU` in `unit_of_measure` — `BR-MET-108`'s shape) instead of from the assets on the service point. a surcharge multiplies the price, never the measurement). ⚠ **The estate figure read `$243.00` / `$247.50` until 2026-09-02, when `CR-062`'s addendum appended two premises with units under it (`DEF-2368`); the per-unit figures and this rule are unchanged.** **THE COUNT AND THE OTHER FOUR CAPACITY SERVICES ARE UNTOUCHED** — `unit_capacity_key` answers `None` for solar, battery, broadband, trash and fire, and a pure case asserts it. **THE THREE OUTCOMES ARE THE SAME THREE, reached differently:** a recorded value is stamped with its unit; **no row** is `BR-MET-108`'s ruled zero (`0`, so the line prices `$0.00` and goes on the document); a value that is not a number **or is recorded in another unit of measure** leaves `installed_capacity` `None` and the bill REFUSES by name, because reading either as zero would discard an answer the customer gave us. **METERING PUBLISHES THE BILLABLE ASSET COUNT — BR-RAT-090's UNMETERED QUANTITY — ON THE SAME ROW THE SERVICE IS PRICED FROM (`billing_reads_for_unit`'s `asset_count` key, plus `billable_asset_count(unit_id, service_code, as_of)` for a caller holding no reading row).** BR-RAT-090 (PO ruling 2026-08-20, option A, closing OQ-283): an unmetered service's quantity is a COUNT of its assets on the unit, **OBSERVED, never typed**. **Worked, ratified: 3 carts × `$15.00` = `$45.00`; remove one and the next bill is `$30.00`.** ⚠ **THE COUNT IS PUBLISHED, A `consumption` IS NOT.** A module answering `consumption=3` would be claiming a meter was read; whether a count BECOMES the billable quantity is BR-RAT-090's rule and BILLING's decision, taken in `apps/billing/stages/measure_consumption.py`. ⚠ **AND IT CROSSES THE SEAM WITHOUT A UNIT, WHICH IS SIGNED LAW AND NOT AN OVERSIGHT** — BR-RAT-036's own row: *"`per_meter` prices a COUNT, and a meter is not a measure of anything… It is the same distinction BR-RAT-090 draws for an unmetered service's cart count."* Three carts is three things. The unit the PRICE is quoted per belongs to the tariff and is attached where the tariff is in hand, so CLAUDE.md rule 3 is met with the tariff's own `billed_unit` and BR-RAT-038 is never asked to reconcile two units nobody stated. A tariff stating no billed unit REFUSES. ⚠ **`None` IS NEVER `0`, AND THIS IS THE WHOLE RULE.** `0` is *the utility looked and this unit has no carts* — a real answer that bills nothing for carts while the base still applies. `None` is *nobody looked*, and the row's `absent_reason` becomes `asset_count_unavailable`, which is **not** exempt from BR-BIL-015's refusal: the account STOPS. Reading the absence as a zero bills a three-cart household `$0.00` for trash on a document that foots and prints, and — for the R1–R7 cutover this seam serves — would make the shadow agree with the live path by construction on every unmetered service, exactly as a defaulted multiplier would. **A DECLARED-UNMETERED SERVICE POINT HOLDING CARTS NOW ANSWERS `service_not_metered`, NOT `no_read`.** A cart is not a meter that was missed: BR-RAT-081 says no read is ever owed for the service, and that is true whether or not something stands on its point. Before this, the carts themselves made `asset_ids_for_service_point` non-empty, so the one shape BR-RAT-090 is about was the one shape DEF-161's fix could not reach — the account refused outright. **The two branches DEF-161's ruling calls the deliverable are untouched:** a declared-unmetered service with a real GOOD read still bills the read, and an UNBILLABLE read is still `read_flagged` for a human, on any service. **READ AT THE SERVICE-PERIOD END DATE** (BR-RAT-090), through the assets seam and never by joining (B1/B2 r4), on assets' own three predicates — this unit on that date from the dated append-only `AssetAssignment` (BR-AST-017), IN SERVICE by `behavior_class` and never by label (BR-AST-031), and FOR THIS SERVICE through the service point. **ONE extra read per unit and only on a tenant that has actually declared a service unmetered**, so every other tenant issues not one extra query and prices byte-identically. ⚠ **`tenant_services.metered` IS NOW `tenant_services.billing_basis` AND CARRIES THREE VALUES — `BR-TEN-1180`, PO ruling 2026-09-02, closing `DEF-2409`** (`metered` · `share` · `capacity`). This rule is unchanged in substance: `capacity` is precisely what the old `metered=False` meant, and `TenantService.metered` survives as a read-only property, so every reader and every signed figure stands. The value it must NOT be read as is `share` — sewer is a percentage of the water usage charge (`BR-RAT-015`) and has no assets to count. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-02 (`CR-062` D1/H2) — THE ROW NOW CARRIES *HOW MUCH* AS WELL AS *HOW MANY*, AND BOTH ARE FACTS RATHER THAN DECISIONS.** `billing_reads_for_unit` publishes two further keys on the same designed-absence row: **`installed_capacity`**, the SUM of the capacity standing on the unit, and **`capacity_uom`**, the unit that sum is in. Both are `None` for every BANDED service — trash, fire, water — and that `None` is the statement *"these do not sum in any unit"*, read from `BR-TEN-171` through `service_size_classes.capacity_uom_for_service`. **Worked:** three solar panels of 2 kW, 4 kW and 8 kW publish `asset_count=3` AND `installed_capacity=14 kW`, and WHICH of the two bills is BILLING's decision, taken where the tariff is in hand — the same split this rule already makes for the unit, and for the same reason. ⚠ **`installed_capacity=None` ON A SUMMING SERVICE IS NEVER A ZERO**, exactly as an absent count is not: an empty unit sums to `0` and BILLS (`BR-MET-108`'s ruled zero), while `None` means an asset records no size class — or one this service does not have — and the account STOPS. Totalling only the equipment the product recognises would bill 2 kW for a 14 kW array, on a document that foots. The population is the COUNT's population BY CONSTRUCTION: `assets.billable_asset_size_classes` runs the same three predicates at the same `as_of` as `billable_asset_count`, because a bill that counted one set of assets and summed another would foot perfectly and be wrong with nothing able to see it. | BR-RAT-090 · BR-RAT-081 · BR-RAT-036 (the count-is-not-a-measure asymmetry) · BR-AST-017 · BR-AST-031 · DEF-1813 · DEF-161 · DEF-1812 (the convention this follows) · OQ-303 / OQ-317 (both CLOSED) | **CONFIRMED** (lane judgment; the RULE and its figure are the PO's, signed 2026-08-20 and re-confirmed 2026-08-27.
