| BR-MET-530 | **THE METERS IMPORT TARGET CARRIES A CONNECTION-SIZE COLUMN, THROUGH ALL FIVE STAGES — AND AN UNKNOWN CODE IS REFUSED AT VALIDATE, NOT DROPPED AT LOAD.** PO ruling 2026-08-28, verbatim ***"Please do both"*** — a column in the meter upload (this row) AND a field on the meter screen (BR-AST-530). ⚠ **WHY IT MATTERS MORE THAN THE SCREEN DOES.** A utility does not tag 1,847 meters one screen at a time; it uploads a file. `BR-AST-500`'s column landed 2026-08-27 and the meters template offered fifteen columns and no size among them, so every migrated meter arrived with the field NULL and `rating.selectors.pricing_inputs` priced all of them off the NULL *every size* tier group. **THE COLUMN:** `size_class`, label **Connection size**, `KIND_LOOKUP`, resolved at stage 2 against a FOURTH per-field index the meters target alone builds (`seams.service_size_class_index()` → `tenancy.selectors.service_size_class_index()`, ACTIVE rows only, read ONCE per run per DEF-324). Headers auto-detected: `size_class`, `size class`, `size`, `connection size`, `connection_size`, `meter_size`, `meter size` — `meter_size` because that is what a legacy export almost certainly calls it, BR-TEN-170 having renamed the LIST without renaming ten years of other people's files. **THE RULE:** `AST.SIZE_CLASS_IN_LOOKUP`, **TIER 1 · ERROR · REJECT_ROW** — the SECOND lookup miss in the whole importer that is an error rather than a flag (`AST.ACCOUNT_TYPE_IN_LOOKUP` is the first, `DEF-618`). ⚠ **AND THE TIER IS THE POINT.** `_lookup_misses` tells an operator the row *"will load with the field unset"*, which is true and harmless for a meter type; for a connection size it means the meter bills, quietly, at the wrong rung. A switchable rule would let a tenant turn ON exactly the silent mispricing `DEF-514` and `DEF-1900` were filed for. **BLANK IS NOT A MISS AND NEVER REACHES THE RULE** (ruling judgment 2): a required column would refuse the very 1,847-meter load the ruling exists to serve, and BR-AST-500 makes *"no size recorded"* a real answer. **THE SAMPLE CELL IS DERIVED, NEVER LITERAL** — `DEF-963`'s lesson applied before the defect rather than after it. It is the intersection of the sizes BR-TEN-171 ratified for the service THAT sample row names and the rows THIS tenant has active; first match wins (the ratified tuples are smallest-first). A tenant with no match ships the cell blank, which loads — so the shipped sample still survives its own import (`DEF-616`) on every tenant rather than on the ones we happened to test. **SUB-METERS AND DEVICES DO NOT GET THE COLUMN** (judgment 4) and the boundary is held by the OFFERED SET, in one place: `_assets_meter_columns(with_size=…)`. `_auto_map` skips any alias whose field is not offered, so the sub-meters alias table can inherit the entry and still never map a `size` header. Widening it is `DEF-1901`'s call. **WORKED FIGURE:** on r10's own water ladder, a meter that arrives with no size prices at `3.20 / 4.80 / 7.20` where a `W-2` connection should read `3.45 / 5.10 / 7.70`. | `DEF-1900` (closed by this row + BR-AST-530) · `BR-AST-500`/`501` · `BR-TEN-170`/`171`/`078` · `DEF-616` · `DEF-963` · `DEF-618` (the ERROR-tier precedent) | **CONFIRMED** (PO ruling 2026-08-28 *"Please do both"*; the four judgments beneath it are in `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` and each follows from a rule already signed) | `06-backend/tests/test_def_1900_size_doors.py` TC-1..9 |
| BR-MET-610 | **THE DOWNLOADED IMPORT TEMPLATE IS HEADED IN THE OPERATOR'S WORDS, AND IT STILL MAPS ITSELF.** PO verbatim 2026-08-27: *"when we add assets we have to tie them to units; and in CSV file that we download there is not unit ID or unit name that we can provide - what about this"*. **(1) THE HEADER ROW IS EACH COLUMN'S `label`, NEVER ITS FIELD NAME**, on every target — `Unit`, not `unit_ref`; `Address line 1`, not `address_line`. The file a first-time operator is told to start from is the one place the product speaks to somebody who has never seen our schema. **(2) AND STAGE 1 MUST STILL AUTO-DETECT EVERY ONE OF THEM, TO THE COLUMN THAT WROTE IT.** `_auto_map` derives a column's own label as an alias, DERIVED from the offered set and never hand-copied into `ALIASES_BY_TARGET` — a hand-kept copy rots on the first label edit, which is `DEF-616`'s exact mechanism. The property is *label → the SAME field*, not merely *label → something*: a label that is another column's alias points a utility's data at the wrong destination and passes a detection test (measured 2026-08-27, `Owner` → the free-text `owner_manager`; closed by `DEF-1247`). **(3) SO A NEW OR RENAMED COLUMN'S LABEL MUST BE UNIQUE WITHIN ITS TARGET AND MUST NOT BE ANOTHER COLUMN'S ALIAS.** Measured at the time of writing: zero duplicates and zero collisions across all six targets. **(4) THE FIELD ORDER IS PUBLISHED AS `sample_columns(target)`** — the one list `sample_csv` writes — so nothing derives it a second time beside the function. | `DEF-1077` (closed by this row) · `DEF-616` · `DEF-1247` · `BR-MET-088` (the offered set is derived from the model) | **CONFIRMED** (measured, and the two guards below fail per target) | `06-backend/tests/test_def1077_label_headers.py`; `test_def616__every_shipped_sample_maps_itself` |
| BR-MET-611 | **A REFERENCE COLUMN THIS TENANT CANNOT YET FILL IN SAYS SO, IN THE BUILD ORDER — IT DOES NOT SHIP A SILENT BLANK.** `DEF-962` is right that a template must never invent `U-00001`, and `BR-AST-045` makes a blank unit LAWFUL (the meter loads to inventory). Together those two correct rules produce the state the PO actually met: a blank column that is simultaneously a lawful value and a fact about their own tenant, stated in the one way nobody can read. **(1) WHEN THE TENANT HAS NO UNITS AT ALL**, every offered `unit_ref` column carries a `blank_reason` naming what to do first — Premises, then Units, then this import — because that is the only next step that works (`BR-CUS-047`: a row naming a missing unit is an ERROR, never an auto-create; J20 step 1). **(2) WHEN THE TENANT HAS UNITS, NO KEY IS WRITTEN AT ALL**, so an operator who sees the sentence knows it is about them. It is a reading of the tenant, not copy about a column. **(3) IT MUST NOT SAY "LEAVE IT BLANK"** — that is true, and it is how twenty meters land seated nowhere behind a green screen (`DEF-1079`'s pairing). **(4) AND WHAT THE COLUMN ACCEPTS IS SAID AT STAGE 1**, where the file is still being created, not only at stage 2 where it has already been written. | `DEF-1077` · `DEF-962` · `DEF-1079` · `BR-AST-045` · `BR-CUS-047` | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_def1077_label_headers.py`; `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ImportTemplateNotes.dom.test.tsx` |
| BR-MET-612 | **AN IMPORT REFUSAL CARRIES A REFERENCE A PERSON CAN READ ALOUD, AND THE SERVER HAS ALREADY WRITTEN THAT REFERENCE DOWN BEFORE THE WORK BEGINS.** `DEF-965` and `DEF-1080` are one production failure reported twice and neither could be diagnosed, because the mechanism (`DEF-1075`) was a **gunicorn worker killed at sixty seconds** — no exception, no envelope, no `trace_id`, and a strip reading *"Something went wrong. Nothing was saved — try again."* **(1) THE CLIENT MINTS A REFERENCE BEFORE THE REQUEST LEAVES** and sends it as `X-Support-Ref` — same eight hex characters, grouped and uppercased, as the short form `supportRef` renders from a server trace id, because the human act is identical and one shape is what stops the product having two. **(2) THE IMPORT STAGES LOG IT ON ARRIVAL**, before any row is touched: a line written after the work is a line a killed worker never writes. **(3) THE SCREEN SHOWS ONE IDENTIFIER, NEVER TWO** — the server's short trace reference whenever there is one, the client's only when there is not (`DEF-332`: our internal ids do not belong on a utility's screen). **(4) THE REFERENCE IS A LABEL AND NEVER AN AUTHORITY**: nothing reads it back, nothing is keyed on it, and the server validates its SHAPE before logging it because an unvalidated header written into a log line forges log lines. | `DEF-965` · `DEF-1080` · `DEF-1075` · `DEF-964` · `DEF-332` · `DEF-328` (the short form) · `DEF-2161` (the `core` logger that made the line reachable) | **CONFIRMED** | `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ImportTemplateNotes.dom.test.tsx::def965__…` |
| BR-MET-830 | **A SAMPLE-DATA REPLAY RECONCILES AN ALREADY-SEEDED ADDRESS TO WHAT THE TENANT NOW SELLS — THE ESTATE HALF OF `BR-RAT-830` (`DEF-1250`).** This block was idempotent per PREMISES: an address the fixture had already planted was appended to the id list and never revisited, so a service switched on afterwards reached **no address's OFFER and no unit's SERVICE POINT**. Under `BR-BIL-300` the service points are what decide which services a bill charges, so the utility switched trash on, reloaded the sample data, and **nothing at all happened on the estate**. **MEASURED before the fix**, provisioning `water + sewer`, loading, switching trash on and loading again: `service points: {water: 75, sewer: 49}` — identical to the first run, on a tenant that now sells trash. ⚠ **THE ADDRESS'S OFFER IS THE LOCK, NOT THE POINT.** `assets.sample_data` plants its four `container_cart` assets through `create_asset` → `ensure_service_point`, which refuses with `metering.service_not_at_premises` unless the ADDRESS offers the service (`BR-MET-119`); the asset block is idempotent per asset NUMBER and would happily plant them on a replay, and could not, because the address still offered water and sewer. **THREE GUARDS:** sample rows only (`is_sample`, for `BR-TEN-068`'s reason); the offer is a **UNION** with what is stored, never a replacement, so an operator who narrowed an address keeps every service they removed and `NARROW_OFFER_PREMISE_NO` keeps the one this fixture holds back to demonstrate `BR-MET-119`'s refusal; and it writes through `update_premises` and `ensure_service_point` (a get-or-create, so the idempotency is the call rather than a check in front of it). ⚠ **`DEF-1250`'S OWN REGISTER ROW IS HALF STALE AND THIS RULE CORRECTS IT:** it says *"metering.sample_data seeds trash service points regardless of the flag"*, which has not been true since CR-039 wave A (`DEF-795`) replaced the literal `water/sewer/trash` intersection with `fixture.estate_plan(enabled…)`. The real metering hole is the MIRROR image, and neither half of the defect is the one that was written down. | `DEF-1250` · `BR-TEN-063` at its own grain (`BR-RAT-830`) · `BR-MET-119` / `BR-MET-003` · `BR-BIL-300` · `BR-TEN-068` · `DEF-795` (the stale half) | **CONFIRMED · BUILT 2026-08-28** — a lane's judgment, recorded with its measurement; no signed figure moves | `apps/metering/sample_data.py::_reconcile_estate_services` · `tests/test_def_1250_enable_after_seed.py` tc5 |
| BR-MET-170 | **THE READING SHEET IS GENERATED WITH ONE ROW PER EXPECTED METER PER REQUIRED REGISTER, AND THE CLERK IS NEVER ASKED TO DERIVE ONE OF THEM.** PO verbatim 2026-08-30 (CR-054): *"on step 1 we have given cycle and details - on step 2 we have ready csv file - with all meters and existing data that is availale - only details clark has to fill is readings and correct any data they want and upload the file - so instead of they figuring out what to do we have hande dthem all details"*. **(1) THE ROW SET IS `BR-MET-049`'s EXPECTED SET, PER METER**, over the cycle's own membership (`BR-MET-033`) inside the reading month's window (`BR-MET-057`): a flat meter is ONE row, a demand meter is TWO, a time-of-use meter on a three-window schedule is FOUR, and a net meter gains its export row (`BR-AST-029`). **(2) THREE COLUMN GROUPS, THREE CONTRACTS.** IDENTITY (`row_key` · `meter_id` · `channel`) says which reading the row is; CONTEXT (`premise` · `unit_of_measure` · `previous_reading` · `previous_read_date` · `issue`) is IGNORED on upload and exists so the clerk can confirm the meter; THEIRS (`reading` · `read_date` · `read_method` · `demand_reset` · `notes`) is pre-filled and correctable. **(3) THE REGISTER IS NAMED IN THE WORDS THE SCREENS USE** — *Total register · On-peak · Mid-peak · Off-peak · Demand · Export · Generation* — because `tou_on` appears on no screen in the product and asking a clerk to spell it was one of the six derivations this rule removes. Those words PARSE BACK to their channel codes (`read_import.CHANNEL_LABEL_ALIASES`), so the sheet re-uploads through the one published importer (`BR-MET-032`) and no second parser exists. **(4) A QUANTITY NEVER TRAVELS WITHOUT ITS UNIT** (hard rule 3): `unit_of_measure` is the REGISTER's unit per `BR-MET-143` — kWh on the energy register, **kW on demand** — and where no unit is declared for a register the previous reading is WITHHELD and the blocker named, never printed bare. **(5) A METER NEVER READ PRINTS A DASH, NEVER A ZERO** (`BR-MET-106`), and an estimated head prints a dash too because `BR-MET-028` gives it no register value; both say why in `issue`. Every figure displays at two decimals with trailing zeros trimmed (`BR-MET-144` — `42864.000000` prints `42864`). **(6) A METER WHOSE REGISTER SET CANNOT BE RESOLVED STILL GETS A ROW**, with the reason stated and counted in `unresolved` — the DEF-075 posture: *"rating cannot answer"* must never read as *"this meter expects nothing"*, and a work list that drops the meter is the silent pass this CR was raised to remove. **(7) NOTHING IS INVENTED.** The read date is the cycle's own window START, derived from B17 §2; `read_method` is blank unless the caller states one, so `BR-MET-014`'s batch-tag inheritance is not silently retired; `demand_reset` is blank because `false` would be a CLAIM the register was not zeroed. | PO 2026-08-30 (CR-054 tasks 4 + 5) · BR-MET-049 · BR-MET-057 · BR-MET-059 · BR-MET-106 · BR-MET-028 · BR-MET-143 · BR-MET-144 · BR-MET-032 · BR-MET-014 · BR-AST-029 · hard rule 3 · DEF-324 · DEF-894 | **CONFIRMED** | ✅ **BUILT — CR-054 W1, 2026-08-30.** `06-backend/apps/metering/reading_sheet.py`, `GET /cycles/<id>/reading-sheet` (+ `/summary`); pinned by `06-backend/tests/test_cr054_w1_reading_sheet.py` (33 cases, every guard proved RED first). ⚠ The SHEET is machine-checked; the BUTTON that offers it is CR-054 task 6 and is not built — no gate in this repo reads rendered output. |
| BR-MET-171 | **THE GENERATED SHEET STAMPS EACH ROW WITH THE METER AND REGISTER IT WAS WRITTEN FOR, AND A ROW WHOSE METER NO LONGER MATCHES IS A WARNING BY NAME.** CR-054 task 7, verbatim: *"The row `key` column round-trips; a row whose meter no longer matches its key is a **warning by name** — 'row 412 was M-4021, now M-4099 — was the meter replaced?'"*. **(1) THE GRAMMAR IS `<cycle code>|<channel code>|<meter number>`**, meter LAST so a `split(maxsplit=2)` returns it whole however the utility spells it — a key that cannot be read back would report a false mismatch against a meter nobody touched. **(2) IT IS OPTIONAL AND STORED.** `read_import_rows.row_key` is NULL for a hand-built file, the blank published template and the manual grid, and NULL means *there is nothing to judge* — nothing is reported. It is STORED rather than checked at parse time because the validation engine re-reads the staged rows and never sees the file. **(3) WARNING, NEVER A REFUSAL AND NEVER SILENT.** A meter replacement is legitimate and common; refusing the row sends the clerk back to fix something that is not broken, and accepting it silently is the behaviour this rule replaces. The severity, the three resolutions (`accept_with_flag` · `fix_meter_id` · `reject`) and the disposition discipline are `BR-MET-122`'s, which is the nearest signed statement for the same class of fact — the file says one thing, the registry says another, and only the clerk can settle it. **(4) A KEY THIS GRAMMAR CANNOT READ IS `not_evaluated` WITH THE CELL SHOWN BACK**, never a manufactured warning: a failure to know is not a finding. **(5) THE REGISTER HALF IS REPORTED AND NOT JUDGED.** The key carries the register too, and CR-054 rules only the METER case, so the channel travels in the outcome detail and the severity is decided by the meter comparison alone — this engine does not answer a question nobody put to it. | CR-054 task 7 (PO 2026-08-30) · BR-MET-122 (the borrowed severity and posture) · BR-MET-015 (accept with a reason) · BR-MET-129 (a warned reading posts) · BR-MET-032 · BR-MET-020 (no threshold — the comparison is two strings) | **CONFIRMED** | ✅ **BUILT — CR-054 W1, 2026-08-30.** `validation.RULE_ROW_KEY` / `_row_key_outcome` (pure), context resolved by `services._build_context`, column added by `metering 0043`; pinned by `06-backend/tests/test_cr054_w1_reading_sheet.py`. |
| BR-MET-940 | **A READ BATCH POSTS WHAT IS CORRECT AND HOLDS WHAT IS NOT — PARTIAL POSTING, SUPERSEDING BR-MET-015's ERROR HALF AT THE POST GATE (PO, carried into `CR-054`; the same ruling `CR-047` shipped for the account importer on 2026-08-29).** PO verbatim: *"COMMITTING All or nothing... - all or nothing will not work - we accept what is correct and rejct what i not correct - then create csv file at loading stage itself so that suer can see all cases thata re rejcted"*, and, on the account importer four days earlier: *"if it is success then you read next record, if it is fail - you display on screen shich record failed and why (dont write to dB) and continue to uplaod next record"*. **THE WORKED FIGURE: a file of 1,180 readings carrying 2 unknown meters used to file 0 readings and refuse; it now files 1,178 and hands the 2 back.** **(1) WHAT IS SUPERSEDED IS THE BATCH CONSEQUENCE AND NOTHING ELSE.** BR-MET-015 reads *"errors must be resolved (reject/override/investigate) before post"*; its warning half went to BR-MET-129 on 2026-08-26 and its ERROR half still governs the ROW — an unresolved error still means that reading does not post, still needs a person, and a disposition given is still honoured. What no longer happens is an error on row 63 keeping 1,179 other customers' readings out of the system. **(2) THE HELD ROW CARRIES A REASON, IN THE CLERK'S WORDS, STORED ON THE ROW.** Six reasons: an unresolved error, a human's own disposition, a row too incomplete to file, BR-MET-127's billed reading, a refusal raised by the write, and one PostgreSQL itself turned down. The two sentences are stored (`rule_outcomes`, rule `post`) rather than computed later, because a refusal raised at the write cannot be re-derived once the transaction has ended. No rule id, no message key, no constraint name reaches the clerk (CR-016). **(3) HARD RULE 6 IS NOT WEAKENED AND THAT IS WHY THE BATCH STILL CLOSES.** A partly-posted batch reaches `posted` and stays immutable (BR-MET-017), so the two existing guards — `status == 'posted'` and BR-MET-018's file+tags key — refuse a second commit exactly as before. **The way back in for a held row is a corrected FILE, which is a new batch with its own hash**; leaving the batch open for a second attempt is what would have made a double-post reachable. This is CR-047's answer to the same question, taken deliberately rather than re-decided. **(4) EVERY WRITE IS ITS OWN SAVEPOINT, AND THAT IS A CORRECTNESS REQUIREMENT RATHER THAN A STYLE.** On PostgreSQL a failed statement aborts the whole transaction, so `except IntegrityError: continue` cannot work — the next row's first read raises and the batch is lost at COMMIT. A nested `transaction.atomic()` is the only shape that holds one row and keeps the rest (CLAUDE.md hard-won law 14). The outer transaction is unchanged, so a killed process still leaves nothing at all. **(5) A FAULT THAT IS NOT ABOUT THE ROW STILL TAKES THE BATCH DOWN.** Only `BizError` and `ValidationError` (the catalogue's two ways of saying *this row's data is wrong*) and a database refusal are held; a role refusal, an unreachable seam, a bug — anything else — propagates, because 1,180 politely-held rows over one permission problem is a data-quality report hiding an outage (CR-047's own reasoning, applied). | PO, carried into CR-054 (2026-08-30) · supersedes BR-MET-015 (error half, at the post gate only) · BR-MET-129 · BR-MET-017 · BR-MET-018 · CR-047 / BR-CUS-051 | **CONFIRMED** | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-30** — `services.commit_read_batch` + `apps/metering/read_rejects.py`. `tests/test_cr054_w24_backend.py`: the headline (2 posted, 1 held), hard rule 6 both ways, the savepoint through a business refusal and through a real unique violation, a fault that is NOT swallowed, and the PostgreSQL transcript law 14 requires. ⚠ **Four existing tests asserted the batch-wide refusal and are AMENDED IN PLACE with the supersession stamped in each docstring, never deleted** — `test_us_met_008_tc5_tc6`, `test_br_met_129__errors_still_gate_the_row` (renamed from `…_the_post`), `test_w7__insert_refuses_a_reading_already_on_file`, `test_us_ast_011_tc4b__the_error_holds_the_row_rather_than_posting_it`. |
| BR-MET-941 | **THE ROWS A BATCH HELD COME BACK AS A FILE THE CLERK CAN FIX AND UPLOAD, AND IT IS OFFERED BEFORE THE COMMIT AS WELL AS AFTER (`CR-054` task 16).** PO verbatim, in the same sentence as BR-MET-940: *"...then create csv file at loading stage itself so that suer can see all cases thata re rejcted"*. **(1) ONE PATH, TWO REPRESENTATIONS** — `GET /read-batches/{id}/results.csv?kind=failures|successes`, the account importer's own vocabulary, because two endpoints would be two places for the refusals and the streaming to drift apart. **(2) THE FILE IS THE OPERATOR'S OWN COLUMNS PLUS TWO** — `why_it_failed` and `how_to_fix_it` appended to the published template's own headers, in the template's own order, carrying the values the clerk typed (`raw_value`, and `read_at_raw` where the date would not parse). It re-uploads without being re-mapped, which is the only job it has. ⚠ **IT IS NOT THE OPERATOR'S FILE BYTE FOR BYTE, unlike CR-047's** — the reads importer stores only the nine mapped targets, so their own header spellings and any extra column are gone (`DEF-2672`, filed with the fix costed, not papered over). **(3) `failures` FROM `validated` ONWARD, `successes` ONLY AFTER THE POST.** The PO asked for the file *"at loading stage itself"* and `DEF-2487` ruled the same way one importer over: the moment a person most needs the list of what failed is before or just after a commit, not on a receipt screen a failed commit never reaches. A successes file before the post would be a header with no rows, which reads as an ANSWER (`DEF-320`). **(4) A WARNED READING IS A SUCCESS, NOT A REJECT** — BR-MET-129 decides that, not the exporter: a file of 1,180 rows with 400 vacant-unit warnings posts 1,180 readings and its rejects file is EMPTY, correctly. **(5) WHAT THE PRE-POST FILE LISTS IS WHAT THE POST ACTUALLY HOLDS**, asserted rather than assumed — a rejects file that disagrees with the commit is worse than none, because the clerk fixes rows that would have gone in. | PO, carried into CR-054 (2026-08-30) · BR-MET-940 · BR-MET-129 · CR-047 task 13 · DEF-2487 · CR-016 | **CONFIRMED** | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-30** — `apps/metering/read_rejects.py`, `GET /read-batches/{id}/results.csv`. Seven cases in `tests/test_cr054_w24_backend.py`, including the round trip: the rejects file is edited and uploaded back, and the corrected reading files. |

## CR-056 — the owner's account hangs off the ADDRESS (2026-09-01)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1010 | **A PREMISES GETS ITS OWNER AN ACCOUNT WHETHER OR NOT ANYTHING IS SHARED, AND THAT ACCOUNT HANGS OFF THE PREMISES.** The metering half of `BR-CUS-1010`. `ensure_owner_master_account` no longer raises `NotImplementedError("OQ-790")`: `OQ-790` closed on 2026-08-27 with the PO naming the mechanism — *"you ahev to call master account creation logic by passing the details and generate account number using Setting -> numbering and use this number to write premsies"* — and left ONE obstacle standing as WORK, recorded on its own row: *"`create_master_account` anchors on common-area units and a new premises has none, so the creation path must anchor on the premises."* **That obstacle is what `CR-056` W1 removed**, so the door opens with no new ruling and nothing guessed. **WHAT IT DOES:** takes the premises' declared owner (`BR-MET-150`), calls customers' own `create_master_account` through the seam — metering never touches another module's ORM (B1) — and writes the resulting account id back to `premises_owners.master_account_id`, which is the link `BR-MET-400` is about. **WHAT IT REFUSES, BY NAME AND WITHOUT INVENTING ANYTHING:** an owner already linked to an account (it hands that account back and writes nothing — the door is idempotent, not a second-account factory); a premises with no declared owner, because the 2026-08-31 ruling says an owner nobody has on file is **flagged, never invented**; and a premises that already has an active Master Account (`BR-CUS-1011`). **WHAT IT IS NOT:** it is not wired into premises creation by this lane. `CR-056` task 23 (New Connect) and task 24 (premises import) own the callers, and a door that works is not the same as a door somebody walks through — hard-won law 2. | `OQ-790` (CLOSED 2026-08-27, option (c)) · PO 2026-08-31 (`CR-056`) · `BR-MET-400` · `BR-MET-150` · `BR-CUS-1010` | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w1_account_on_the_address.py` |
| BR-MET-1011 | **AN EMPTY UNIT'S STANDING CHARGE IS LEVIED PER UNIT (PO ruling 2026-08-31, `CR-056`) — `$183.00` ON SIX FLATS AT `$30.50`, NOT `$30.50`.** Recorded here as well as on `BR-MET-105` because the ruling answers a question `BR-MET-105`'s own text says out loud is unruled, and a rule that is answered in one register and left open in another is how the same thing gets asked twice (law 13). **THE REASONING IS THE RULE, not the size of the number:** a standing charge pays for a CONNECTION; a meter is installed at that flat and the utility carries that cost whether or not anybody lives there. Charging once per address would carry five of six meters free while somebody lives in the sixth, and the same building would cost six times as much to serve empty as full. **The unit decides what it costs; occupancy decides who pays.** **NO NEW TABLE AND NO PREMISES-LEVEL PLAN** — plans attach to units and to nothing else, and a plan in two places is two prices for one meter with the wrong one winning silently. **ONE LINE PER EMPTY UNIT**, each naming its unit. | PO 2026-08-31 (`CR-056`) · `BR-MET-105` · `OQ-350` (the half it closes) · `BR-CUS-170` | **CONFIRMED** | W7 |

## CR-056 W3 task 18 — the owner reaches a customer THROUGH the account (2026-08-31)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1020 | **A PREMISES OWNER REACHES THE CUSTOMER THEY ARE THROUGH THEIR MASTER ACCOUNT, AND NEVER THROUGH THE COLUMN BESIDE IT.** `premises_owners` carries two paths to the same person: `customer_id`, straight at the party, and `master_account_id` → `accounts` → `customer_accounts` → the same party. **The first skips the only row that holds a balance, receives a bill and can be posted to**, so a lane resolving *"who owns this address"* through it gets a NAME where it needed an ACCOUNT — which is `DEF-650` again, wearing a foreign key. Under the PO ruling of 2026-08-31 (`CR-056` ruling 1) *"a Master Account is the owner's account at an address"*, and `BR-MET-400` already says *"Premises -> owner = Master account"*, so the account path is the ruled one and there is one answer instead of two that can disagree. **What is enforced:** the write door refuses `customer_id` BY NAME and its refusal names the path that works (a refusal that only says *no* leaves the clerk with the same job and no way to do it), and `owner_selectors.owner_detail_dict` answers `customer_id` and `customer_no` from the ACCOUNT — through the seam, so B1/B8 are untouched and this module still reads no `customers` table. ⚠ **THE COLUMN IS NOT DROPPED, AND THAT LIMIT IS DELIBERATE.** `BR-MET-150` is a PO ruling that asked for this link in its own words — *"linked to the customer they already are"* — and that INTENT is fully served through the account: the fact *this owner is that customer* is still recorded, one hop further along, on the row that also holds the money. Removing the physical column would additionally reverse the ruling's own sentence, which is a supersession only the PO can state (CLAUDE.md law 13), and it would destroy existing rows. It is **`OQ-1240`**, open and non-blocking. ⚠ **A STORED VALUE IN THE RETIRED COLUMN IS STATED, NEVER SILENTLY DROPPED:** `owner_detail_dict` serves `customer_link_column_ignored` so a row written before today says the value exists and no longer decides anything. | `CR-056` W3 task 18 · PO ruling 2026-08-31 (CR-056 ruling 1) · `BR-MET-400` · `BR-MET-150` · `DEF-650` · `apps/metering/premises_owners.py` · `apps/metering/owner_selectors.py` | **CONFIRMED (the mechanism; the column's removal is `OQ-1240`)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w2_customer_identity.py` |
| BR-MET-1024 | **NEW CONNECT MAY CREATE THE ADDRESS IT BUILDS ON, AND WHEN IT DOES, THAT ADDRESS'S OWNER GETS HIS ACCOUNT THROUGH THE ONE DOOR THAT ALREADY EXISTS.** Until today `create_connection` REFUSED without a `premises_id` (`metering.connection.premises_required`), so a new build had to be typed into the Premises screen first and then found again in the wizard — and the commit manifest reported *"premises: 1"* anyway, from the presence of units (`DEF-2961`). **WHAT IS BUILT:** the wizard's step 1–2 door accepts EITHER an existing `premises_id` (unchanged, every existing caller included) **or** a `premises` block, which it passes VERBATIM to `metering.services.create_premises` — the same validator, the same number series, the same audit row, the same mandatory owner. **NO SECOND CREATOR AND NO SECOND VALIDATOR:** a premises created here is byte-for-byte a premises created on S16, `owner_id` requirement included, which is what makes *every address gets its owner a Master Account* (`BR-MET-1010`) reachable from the wizard at all. **THE OWNER'S ACCOUNT IS `ensure_owner_master_account`**, the door `CR-056` W1 opened and named tasks 23/24 as the callers of; it is IDEMPOTENT, so an owner who already holds an account at that address gets it back and nothing is written. **AND IT IS OPT-IN, BY NAME.** The account is created only when the caller asks for it (`create_owner_account: true`), because an account is a money object and a wizard that mints one from a side effect of typing an address is the thing `BR-MET-112` — *"nothing is written until step 9"* — exists to prevent. ⚠ **`BR-MET-116`'s REFUSAL IS UNTOUCHED.** The BUILD account for a multi-unit development still anchors on common areas and still refuses a build with none; that is a different account for a different party and this rule does not relax it. ⚠ **AND THE MANIFEST STOPS LYING:** `premises` counts `1` when this connection created its address and `0` when it was handed one. | `CR-056` task 23 · PO ruling 2026-08-31 (`CR-056` ruling 1) · `BR-MET-1010` · `BR-MET-112` · `BR-MET-116` · `DEF-2961` · `apps/metering/connections.py` | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr056_w5_new_connect_premises.py` |


## CR-058 W3 — the premises import learns who the owner is (2026-09-01)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1030 | **THE PREMISES IMPORT CREATES THE OWNER'S MASTER ACCOUNT AND CUSTOMER NUMBER WHERE THE ROW CARRIES A NAME **AND** AT LEAST ONE IDENTIFIER — AND CREATES NOTHING AT ALL WHERE IT CARRIES A NAME ALONE.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-01:** *"If owner name is given and one identify given - DL, SSN, Phone or Tax ID - then you create master account and Customer ID - otherwise dont create anything"*. **THE THREE OUTCOMES, and the third is unchanged from today:** a row with an owner name and one identifier match-or-creates the Customer # and creates the premises' MASTER ACCOUNT (`BR-CUS-1010`: it hangs off the ADDRESS, `unit_id` NULL); a row with an owner name and no identifier LOADS THE ADDRESS, creates nothing and is FLAGGED; a row with no owner is exactly as it was. **WHAT THIS SUPERSEDES, STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED:** *"the import never invents a party"* STANDS and is NOT reversed — **an identifier is not an invention.** What changes is that a row carrying real identity evidence stops being treated as if it carried none. The already-ruled *"Load the address, create nobody, flag it"* (PO 2026-08-27) now governs the NAME-ONLY row specifically, which is what it was always about. **THE COLUMNS THIS NEEDS DID NOT EXIST.** The template carried an `Owner` column and no identity columns at all, so the rule requires six new ones — **owner kind** (person or company, because it decides which identifier counts, `BR-CUS-1022`), **SSN**, **driving licence**, **phone**, **Tax ID**, and the **owner account type** the master account is priced and reported by. Adding a column to a published template is something the utility SEES; it is product surface, not plumbing. **NO IDENTIFIER IS EVER CAPTURED UNDER AN UNSTATED KIND** and no identifier belonging to the other kind is captured at all — a company presenting a Social Security number is flagged, not filed (`BR-CUS-1022`). **NOTHING IS INVENTED TO SATISFY THE RULE:** a row naming an owner and an identifier but no account type creates NOTHING and is flagged, because the consumer category is what the bill is priced by and a guess there is a money decision taken by a shrug. **THE CREATION GOES THROUGH THE ONE DOOR CUSTOMERS OWNS** — `customers.create_master_account` through the seam, reached by `premises_owners.ensure_master_account_for_premises` — so BR-CUS-017's family, `BR-CUS-1011` and the customer resolution all apply to an imported owner exactly as to a hand-made one. There is no second creation path and there must never be (B1: customers owns accounts). **THE RECEIPT MUST SAY WHICH ROWS GOT AN OWNER ACCOUNT AND WHICH ARE WAITING FOR SOMEBODY TO IDENTIFY THE OWNER.** Those two figures are the whole mitigation: without them a half-identified migration is indistinguishable from a finished one, which is the same failure `premises_with_no_owner` was created to make visible. | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log, *"SEVEN CORRECTIONS…"* §1) · `CR-058` tasks 10–13 · **also closes `CR-056` task 24** (*"Premises import creates the Master Account — ruled once, never built"*) · NARROWS the PO ruling of 2026-08-27 (*"what if owner column is empty"*) at its own sentence · `BR-CUS-1010` · `BR-CUS-1011` · `BR-CUS-1022` · `BR-CUS-1023` · `BR-MET-1010` · `BR-MET-402` | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr058_w3_premises_owner_import.py` |
| BR-MET-1175 | **THE PREMISES IMPORT ASKS FOR THE OWNER'S CATEGORY, AND CREATES AND LINKS THE OWNER RECORD WHERE THE ROW STATES ONE.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-19:** *"Yes add owner category column in csv template"*. **WHAT WAS WRONG:** the user reported *"when we import premsies -> provide owner deetails -> in thos ec ases Master Account is not getting generated"*. The account WAS generated — what was missing is that **nobody owned it**. Reproduced on real PostgreSQL: a complete correct row created master account `AC-2026-00058` and left `premises.owner_id` NULL, wrote **no `premises_owners` row**, and put the operator's name in the legacy free-text box only. **THE BRANCH THAT WORKS IS THE ONE A FIRST MIGRATION CAN NEVER TAKE:** `ensure_master_account_for_premises` writes the link only when the premises ALREADY has a typed owner, and on a first upload nobody is on file, so EVERY row took the other branch. **WHY IT COULD NOT HAVE WORKED, AND IT IS NOT A BUG IN THE DECISION:** `premises_owners.category_id` is NOT NULL and `BR-MET-151` clause 3 forbids reading a category off a name, so the loader was refused a guess — **and was never given the chance to be told**, because the template offered no category column. **THE RULE:** the premises template offers **Owner category**, a LOOKUP over the tenant's own editable Settings list (seeded `owner_occupier`, `landlord`, `property_manager`, `hoa`, `builder` — read from the lookup, never hardcoded, because a tenant may rename, retire or add). A row that states a category has its party CREATED and linked through `match_premises_owner` — the same door a person uses on `PRM-04`, which takes the caller's category and leaves `owner_manager` alone as clause 4 provenance — and the premises then takes the existing already-on-file branch, so `premises_owners.master_account_id` is written. **There is no second creation path and there must never be.** ⚠ **A BLANK CELL IS NOT AN ERROR AND MUST NOT BECOME ONE:** no default and no guess — the address loads, the owner account is still created, the party waits for a person, and `PRM.OWNER_CATEGORY_MISSING` says so at rehearsal (WARNING + FLAG_ONLY, because an ERROR would refuse the first upload a utility ever sends — `DEF-1088`'s lesson). ⚠ **ONLY ON THE ELIGIBLE PATH:** a name and a category with NO identifier still creates nothing, exactly as ruled on 2026-09-01; this rule does not widen what earns an account. ⚠ **AND THE RECEIPT COUNTS THE OWNER RECORD APART FROM THE ACCOUNT** — `premises_owner_parties_created`, `premises_owner_no_category`, `premises_owner_party_refused`, and `premises_owner_no_owner_named`, the fifth verdict which had no counter at all (PO 2026-09-19, *"this is correct"*): four owner figures printed and a fifth could not, so four zeroes read as a clean run. | PO ruling 2026-09-19 (decision log) · NARROWS `BR-MET-402` clause 5 at its own sentence · `BR-MET-151` clause 3 (UNCHANGED — no category off a name) · `BR-MET-1030` · `BR-CUS-1010` · `DEF-2902`/`DEF-2903` (the declared link this finally writes on a new party) | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr058_w3_premises_owner_import.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_def_met_5802_declared_columns.py` · `10-scripts/premises_owner_import_walkthrough.py` (real PostgreSQL — asserts `premises.owner_id` and `premises_owners.master_account_id`, never a receipt count) |
| BR-MET-1031 | **THE UNITS IMPORT CREATES UNITS AND NOTHING ELSE — CONFIRMED, AND PINNED SO A LATER LANE CANNOT QUIETLY WIDEN IT.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-01:** *"this is correct no changes here"*. A units upload writes `units` rows: **no account, no customer, no `customer_accounts` link.** A unit with no plan loads anyway, flagged, and never bills (`BR-MET-100`). It is recorded as a rule rather than left as an absence for one reason — the premises import GAINED a conditional account on the same day (`BR-MET-1030`), and *"the one next to it did not"* has to be findable or the next lane reads the pattern and adds it. | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log §2) · `CR-058` task 19 · `BR-MET-096` · `BR-MET-100` | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence)** | `06-backend/tests/test_cr058_w5_doors_create_nothing.py` |


## BR-MET-1032 — a parked upload keeps its identifiers the way a customer record does (2026-09-01)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1032 | **AN IDENTITY CELL IS ENCRYPTED WITH ITS BLIND INDEX THE MOMENT IT IS STAGED, AND THE CHECKING SCREEN SHOWS THE LAST FOUR — WHILE THE FILE AN OPERATOR SENDS IS UNCHANGED AND CARRIES THE WHOLE NUMBER.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-01:** *"in spreadsheet we will porvide entire SSN number if required"* — **CONFIRMED, and nothing here narrows it.** A blind index cannot be built from four digits, so an import given only the last four could create a customer and never match one, which is the whole purpose of collecting it. **THE THREE PARTS, and only the first needed a person.** (1) **THE FILE** carries the whole number — ruled, unchanged, no new refusal, no column narrowed, no receipt figure moved. (2) **THE SCREEN** serves `••••6789` for a Social Security number, a driving licence and a Tax ID, exactly as `customers`' own read path serves `ssn_last4`/`driving_licence_last4`/`tax_id_last4`; **a phone is served in full, because `customers` serves it in full** — it is the utility's contact handle and a government identifier for neither kind (`BR-CUS-1022`). (3) **THE STAGING TABLE** stores every one of the four app-layer encrypted with its blind index beside it, in a reserved `__identity__` key inside `import_run_rows.raw` and `.mapped` — **the mechanism `customers 0036`/`0038` already built, given a second caller and not a second design.** **WHY STAGING IS NOT AN EXCEPTION:** a parked upload is not a transient — it sits in our database until somebody commits or discards it, which can be days, and a migration file is the densest collection of government identifiers this product will ever hold, every customer a utility has at once. *"It is only staging"* is exactly the reasoning that leaves the largest such collection as the one unencrypted copy; `tenancy.ein` is this repo's counter-example for that, not its precedent. **THE BLIND INDEX USES `customers`' OWN CONTEXT AND THE CIPHERTEXT DOES NOT** — the index must equal what the customer record's index would produce for the same number or the matcher cannot use it, and the ciphertext's AAD names the staging column so a staged blob cannot be dropped into a customer column without going through the door. **THE FULL VALUE STILL REACHES THE MATCHER**: the load reveals it from the vault and hands `customers` exactly what it handed before, so an imported person already on file still joins to that customer rather than creating a second. **BOTH IMPORT DOORS**, premises and accounts, because both stage through `import_run_rows` and one decision fixes both. **NOTHING ALREADY STAGED IS INVALIDATED**: this governs what is WRITTEN from now on, and every read path tolerates a row parked before it — an unprotected cell is read as itself. ⚠ **THE ARGUMENT AGAINST THE SCREEN HALF IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS NOT WEAK**: a row refused for a malformed identifier is harder to diagnose from four digits. Accepted, because the fix happens in the file and the refusal names the cell; **if that proves wrong in practice it is a defect to file, never a licence to widen the display.** | PO ruling 2026-09-01 (decision log, *"THE SPREADSHEET CARRIES THE WHOLE SOCIAL SECURITY NUMBER"* — the FILE half) · the two build judgments in that entry, decided there rather than put back (law 13) · `BR-CUS-092` · `BR-CUS-1022` · `BR-CUS-1023` · `BR-MET-1030` · `OQ-016` CLOSED (app-layer encryption) · `customers` migrations `0036`/`0038` | **CONFIRMED (the FILE half is the PO's own sentence; the SCREEN and STORE halves are build judgments recorded in the decision log)** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_1032_staged_identity.py` |
| BR-MET-1160 | **A READING WHOSE UNIT IS NOT THE UNIT THE TARIFF PRICES IN IS CAUGHT WHEN THE READING IS UPLOADED — NOT WHEN THE BILL RUNS.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-02:** *"refula at the time of billing - it will be too late to act , we have to ensure wehn we upload reading at that time it is check and corrected"*. Said on being told that the engine already refuses the pair at bill time (`BR-RAT-038`) and that the rate wizard already warns at save (`DEF-3108`, fixed the same day). **(1) WHY THIS POINT AND NOT THE OTHER TWO.** A refusal at bill time is a blocked bill run at 2am with nobody at the screen, on a reading accepted hours earlier — the customer is waiting for a bill and the operator finds a run that will not complete. At UPLOAD a clerk is standing at the import screen with the batch in front of them, and a mismatch is a correction they can make on the row that caused it. **The cost of catching it late is not the refusal — it is that the refusal arrives with nobody able to answer it.** **(2) THE TWO STRINGS COMPARED.** LEFT: the **register's** own unit (`BR-MET-143` — kWh on an energy register, kW on a demand register), which is the unit this quantity was measured in and CLAUDE.md hard rule 3's subject. RIGHT: the **rate schedule's `uom`**, resolved through the unit's plan at the version in force on the READ's date (`BR-RAT-007`, no fallback to today's prices) — which is the same string `rating.pricing.stages.normalize._gate_uom` refuses the bill against, so a read this rule passes is a read that run will price. ⚠ **It is NOT `tenant_services.billed_uom`**: that column is NULL on every tenant this product has ever provisioned and a rule reading it would be blind everywhere, which is `DEF-3108` exactly. **(3) THE VERDICT IS `BR-MET-143`'s AND IS NOT RE-DECIDED.** `metering.register_units.reconciliation` is the one implementation, the same one rating reaches through its own seam. A **dimension** mismatch is an **ERROR** — `therm` is declared energy and `ccf` volume, so no multiplier and no `conversion_factor` reconciles them (the conversion needs a heating value, a physical fact about the gas and not a column), and there is nothing a clerk can type on the import screen that makes this bill. A **scale** mismatch is a **WARNING** and that severity is ratified elsewhere rather than chosen here — `US-RAT-005 TC-7` is signed *"warn now, rating blocked later"* and reversing a signed case is a re-sign (HUMAN-GUIDE §6). An **unknown dimension** is a WARNING too: refusing on *"we do not know"* would be stricter than the ratified case with strictly less knowledge. **(4) THE MONEY, WORKED.** One therm of pipeline gas is about `0.9643 ccf`, so a `42`-unit gas read priced at the signed `$1.15/ccf` bills **`$48.30`** where the honest figure is about **`$46.58`** — roughly **4% over, `$1.72` a month on one meter**, on a bill that foots. **(5) NOTHING IS AUTO-FIXED AND NO THRESHOLD IS INVENTED** (`BR-MET-052`, `BR-MET-020`): the comparison is two strings and a declared dimension map. Every outcome carries its resolutions (`BR-MET-022`) and names **both** screens — `fix_schedule_uom` and `set_asset_uom` / `set_device_uom`, plus `reject` — because we cannot know which side holds the typo, and a refusal naming the wrong remedy is worse than a long one. **(6) A GAP IS NEVER READ AS AGREEMENT.** Where the pricing unit cannot be resolved — rating unwired, no plan on the unit, the plan naming no schedule for this service, no version in force on the read's date, the schedule stating no unit, the meter on no service point — the rule reports `not_evaluated` with that specific reason (the DEF-075 posture). **(7) A MAXIMUM REGISTER IS DELIBERATELY NOT GATED.** A demand peak carries kW and is priced by `demand_rate_per_kw`; gating it against the schedule's energy `uom` would refuse every demand bill there is, which is what `normalize._peak_demand` already says in the engine. The rule reports the skip rather than falling silent. **(8) THIS REPLACES NOTHING.** Three points, deliberately: refuse at SAVE where a tariff is typed (`DEF-3108`), refuse at UPLOAD where a reading arrives and a person can fix it (this rule), refuse at BILL as the backstop (`BR-RAT-038`) — because a reading can predate a tariff change. ⚠ **ENG PLACEMENT NOTE:** `BR-MET-136` names the seven locked integrity rules and defines the judgment set by subtraction, so this rule lands in the JUDGMENT half and a tenant can switch it off. It fails safe if they do — the engine still refuses the bill by name — but a check on a money path that a setting can silence deserves the PO's word rather than a lane's, and the gap is raised as **`OQ-1330`** rather than closed here (the posture `RULE_GENERATION_CHANNEL` takes for `OQ-417`). | PO 2026-09-02 (decision log, same day) · `BR-MET-143` (PO 2026-08-27) · `BR-MET-020/022/052/136` · `BR-RAT-038` · `BR-RAT-007` · `US-RAT-005 TC-7` (NOT moved) · `DEF-3108` · `DEF-3107` (nothing converts) · `OQ-004` closure · `OQ-1290` · CLAUDE.md hard rule 3 | **CONFIRMED (the WHERE is the PO's own sentence; the severities and the reported gaps are build judgments recorded in the decision log)** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_1160_upload_unit_mismatch.py` (10 cases; the guard proved RED first and the pre-change outcome list is quoted verbatim in the module docstring) |


## BR-MET-1161 — one home for an address, and everything else walks to it (2026-09-04)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1161 | ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-08 by `BR-MET-1162` — ITS COORDINATE CLAUSE ONLY. The address half below stands exactly as signed; a unit may now carry its own map point and parcel, and where it does not it reads its building's.** **AN ADDRESS IS STORED ON `premises` AND NOWHERE ELSE; A `unit` REFINES A PREMISES BY LABEL, NOT BY ADDRESS; AND EVERY OTHER ENTITY THAT NEEDS TO KNOW WHERE SOMETHING IS DERIVES IT BY WALKING TO THE PREMISES.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-04:** *"also note we are having addresses at one place now - Premises and Units -> then they flow to {Meters, Sub meter, assets} + { accounts} -> from there it flows to Routes and Cycles -? and bill derives it again dueing billing. Simialrly work orders are either related to Accounts/Customers or they are related to Assets or premises and Units - so they will also draw addresses from same place that we we can maintain the integrtiy, we can keep all service points = units, premises valid and intact, along with GIS data, and use it for all entities as well as maps - this is important to udnerstand - it will flow in Customer web portal /Self servcie portal also"*. **THE CHAIN, AND IT IS THE ONE THE PRODUCT ALREADY HAS:** `premises` (address of record + `lat`/`lng`) → `units` (label only, no address) → `service_points` → meters, sub-meters, assets; `units` → `accounts` → invoices → the printed bill; routes and cycles hang off the service point; a work order stands on one of its four anchors and reads the address through whichever it has. **THE COORDINATES TRAVEL THE SAME WAY**, so a map and an envelope cannot disagree — which is the *integrity* the PO named as the reason. **WHAT WAS ALREADY RIGHT, MEASURED:** `units`, `service_points`, `assets` and `work_orders` declare **no address column at all**, and `ck_work_orders_has_anchor` already refuses an order standing on none. **THE ONE PLACE IT WAS NOT, AND IT WAS A DOOR RATHER THAN A LEAK:** `customers.addresses.kind` accepted `service` as well as `mailing` (B2 §2), a second editable copy of the service address hanging off the account. **0 rows of it existed and no code path read one** — the only hits were the tuple that permitted it, the validator that checked against that tuple, and a docstring explaining why the mailing read refuses to fall back to it. The door is CLOSED (`ADDRESS_KINDS = ("mailing",)`) and a write asking for it is refused BY NAME with `customers.address.kind_service_retired`, never as a generic unknown lookup — a caller holding a real service address needs to be told where it lives. ⚠ **THIS SUPERSEDES A SIGNED B2 §2 STATEMENT at its second value**, recorded in the decision log of 2026-09-04 rather than edited into B2 in passing; it moves no ❄️ FROZEN B4/B5 figure, so it needs no finance re-sign. ⚠ **NOTHING WAS MIGRATED, so rows written before today can still exist** and `test_cr053_print_set.py` keeps the case that says the bill run never posts to one. **THREE EXCEPTIONS, EACH A DIFFERENT FACT RATHER THAN A SECOND COPY:** (1) `customers.addresses` `kind='mailing'` — **where the BILL goes**, which for every landlord, managing agent and anyone who has moved is deliberately not where the meter is; (2) `metering.interruptions.location_text` — **what a stranger typed** (`BR-CWP-027`), a report and not a record, resolving to a `premises_id` only when a clerk says so; (3) `tenancy.ServiceCentre.address` — **the utility's own walk-in counter**, which is not a property that takes service, has no `premises` row and never will, so it cannot borrow (a build judgment recorded in the decision log, not put back to the PO — law 13). **A FOURTH COPY IS A DEFECT, AND `G85` SAYS SO AT MERGE**: it parses every model with `ast` and fails any that declares an address-shaped column outside those homes, with `state` and `country` deliberately excluded as triggers because eight of eleven `state` hits are workflow columns. **WHAT THIS COST TO CLOSE, and it is why a defect row exists:** `DEF-CUS-3653` — the account header composed its `Austin, TX 78704` line from the retired kind, so closing the door would have made it return null on every account forever with every test green (hard-won law 2). It now reads `premise_locality`, served by `account_detail_dict` from `metering.selectors.premises_locality` through the customers seam — the walk itself, so the fix and the rule are one act. **AND IT FLOWS TO THE PORTAL, since the PO named it:** the customer portal shows the PREMISES address reached `account → unit → premises`, and `apps/portal` declares no address column and holds no copy. | PO ruling 2026-09-04 (decision log, *"ADDRESSES HAVE ONE HOME"*) · same ruling family as `CR-070 §W` (*"Connection = Account"*) · SUPERSEDES B2 §2 `addresses.kind` at its second value · `BR-CWP-027` · `BR-MET-1024` · `BR-CUS-1010` · `DEF-CUS-3653` · `DEF-2847` (why `TenantSettings.country` is not a customer's country) · `10-scripts/gates/g85_one_address_home.py` | **CONFIRMED (the chain and the reason are the PO's own sentence; the three exceptions and the gate's trigger set are build judgments recorded in the decision log)** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_1161_one_address_home.py` (3 cases; case 1 proved RED first) · `10-scripts/gates/g85_one_address_home.py --selftest` (2 sabotages) |
| BR-MET-1162 | **A UNIT MAY CARRY ITS OWN PARCEL NUMBER AND ITS OWN MAP POINT, AND WHERE IT DOES THOSE ARE THE TRUTH FOR THAT UNIT; WHERE IT DOES NOT, IT READS ITS BUILDING'S.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-08:** *"youa re correct address is on premsies/property - but GIS data for unit might change and hence we have to have parcel ID and GIs details on Unit also"*, and confirming the reading put back to them: *"The address half stands. The coordinate half narrows: a unit may carry its own point and parcel, and where it does, that is the truth for that unit. Where it does not, it uses its building's."* ⚠ **THIS AMENDS `BR-MET-1161` AND THE SUPERSESSION IS STATED, NOT ASSUMED.** The ADDRESS half of that rule is untouched — an address is still stored on `premises` and nowhere else, and a unit still refines a premises by LABEL. What narrows is its COORDINATE clause, which carried `premises.lat/lng` the same way *"so a map and an envelope cannot disagree"*; a unit is now allowed to differ, deliberately, because a cabin, a pad or a wing of a campus is somewhere its site office is not. **The fallback is part of the rule, not an implementation detail:** a unit with no point of its own reads its building's, and any screen showing a point says which of the two it is showing. | PO ruling 2026-09-08, recorded in `12-governance/02-decision-log.md` the same session | **CONFIRMED — THE COORDINATE HALF IS BUILT (`CR-089` wave A, 2026-09-08, `2.234.0`); THE PARCEL HALF IS NOT AND THAT IS STATED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE DISCOVERED.** `units.lat`/`units.lng` exist (`metering/0055`), nullable, NULL on all 1,109 units — opt-in by design. `selectors.unit_point` is the ONE definition of the fallback and serves `source: unit | premises | none`, so every screen showing a point says which. ⚠ **A UNIT'S OWN PARCEL COLUMN WAS NOT ADDED**: wave A's task list names only `lat` and `lng` (`A1`, `A7`), and `units.external_ref` cannot double as a parcel — it is `BR-MET-404`'s LEGACY match key under `uq_units_tenant_external_ref_ci`, which would refuse the two units in one building that share one parcel. That half of this ruling is UNBUILT and owed. | ⚠ **`G85` DID GO RED ON THE COMMIT THAT ADDED THESE COLUMNS, AND THAT IS THE GUARD WORKING** — proved red through `run_gates`' own entry point (`module.run()`, never `python3 <gate>.py` — `DEF-GOV-3733`): *"apps/metering/models.py::Unit declares ['lat', 'lng']"*. Its allow list moved in the same commit with this ruling quoted above the entry, and **FIELD-SCOPED**: `HOME_FIELDS` permits `lat`/`lng` on a unit and still refuses a `city` on one BY NAME, because putting the whole class on the list would have opened the door this ruling deliberately kept shut. A third self-sabotage proves that half bites. `06-backend/tests/test_cr089_a_where_a_property_is.py` — its `ADDRESS_SHAPED` set counts `lat`, `lng`, `latitude`, `longitude`. Its allow list moves in that same commit with this ruling quoted beside it, and **CLAUDE.md hard rule 8 is stamped in the same commit**, never before the code (hard-won law 1). |
| BR-MET-1163 | **`premises.external_ref` IS THE PARCEL NUMBER — the assessor's number for the plot — AND IS LABELLED AS SUCH ON EVERY SCREEN.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-08:** *"We will need parcel ID and we will ahve to display them"* and *"you can make reference # = Parcel ID"*, overruling a recommendation that a parcel be a NEW field beside the reference. **The recommendation was over-cautious and the measurement says so: the column is NULL on all 673 properties across every tenant**, so nothing is overwritten and no utility loses data. Its current label, *External / GIS ref*, tells a clerk nothing about what belongs in it. | PO ruling 2026-09-08 | **CONFIRMED — BUILT (`CR-089` wave A, 2026-09-08, `2.234.0`).** ⚠ **THE LABEL HALF IS SUPERSEDED BY `BR-MET-1173` (PO 2026-09-17), STAMPED 2026-10-07 (`DEF-MET-10008`, GIS Lane D).** `BR-MET-1173` made `external_ref` the import match key and moved the City's parcel to `gis_parcel_id`; the label stayed on `external_ref`, so *Parcel* named two fields. From 2.560.3 `external_ref` reads **Import reference** (Overview, Edit premise, New Connection) and **Parcel** names only `gis_parcel_id` (Add premises, and the Overview where a City GIS is in use). No data moved. The label reads **Parcel ID** on the create wizard, the list's edit form and the property's Overview, in all eleven catalogues. ⚠ **AND THE CREATE FORM HAD NO SUCH FIELD AT ALL** — `external_ref` was in `SingleForm` and in the payload and was DRAWN NOWHERE, so the City's parcel key reached the server with nobody able to see or correct it (hard-won law 2 wearing the other face). It is drawn now. Nothing is backfilled on real rows; the SAMPLE estate is given deterministic `R-114-nnnn-nnn` numbers so the field has something to show. | `06-backend/tests/test_cr089_a_where_a_property_is.py` |
| BR-MET-1164 | **A PREMISES CANNOT BE DEACTIVATED WHILE ANY UNIT ON IT IS ACTIVE, ANY ASSET IS STILL ATTACHED, OR ANY ACCOUNT ON IT IS OPEN; A UNIT CANNOT BE DEACTIVATED WHILE AN ACCOUNT ON IT IS OPEN. THE REFUSAL NAMES WHAT IS BLOCKING.** **PO verbatim, 2026-09-08:** *"if you ahve units - active, assets or active accounts, you cannot shut down building pr premises."* This settles a question left open earlier the same day — refuse, warn-and-allow, or cascade — and the answer is **REFUSE**. Each blocking count links to the list that clears it, so *why not* is also the route to *do this first*. | PO ruling 2026-09-08 | **CONFIRMED AND BUILT 2026-09-08 (`CR-089` wave D).** ⚠ **NEITHER PAGE HAD A STATUS CONTROL AT ALL** — both showed a status badge and offered no way to change it, and the unit page advised deactivating as though a button existed. Both now carry **Change status**, and the refusal names what is blocking with every count linked to the list that clears it. **THREE THINGS THIS RULE DID NOT SAY, DECIDED IN THE BUILD AND RECORDED IN THE DECISION LOG OF 2026-09-08 (law 13):** (1) with more than one count outstanding the refusal names the FIRST STEP rather than listing everything — accounts, then balances, then assets, then units, which is the order the work is actually done in, and B14 rule 33 caps it at six words; (2) **a unit's attached assets are COUNTED and do NOT block**, because this rule names ONE term for a unit and a meter blocking would refuse nearly every unit this product has — the count is drawn because the clerk is about to strand it; (3) **a unit may not be REACTIVATED onto an inactive property**, derived from this rule's own first half rather than invented, because allowing it would breach the invariant one press later. **BR-MET-047 is NOT superseded** — its money term (a unit that still owes) is enforced alongside these three and appears as a fourth count when it has something to say. ⚠ **AND THE LIST ROW'S OLD DOOR ENFORCES THE SAME WIDENED SET**: `POST /premises/{id}/deactivate` refused on BR-MET-047 alone, so a building with four live units could be shut down by pressing the other button. One computation (`selectors.premises_status_dependencies`), two callers. | `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_1164_status_change.py` (15 cases; proved RED first — with the guard removed two fail with *"DID NOT RAISE BizError"*, and with a unit's meters made blocking one fails on `KeyError: 'attached_assets'`) · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/StatusChangeDialog.dom.test.tsx` (6 cases, mounted under the real `/:tenant/…` route pattern) · walked in a browser on 2026-09-08: `10-scripts/cr089_wave_d_status_walkthrough.mjs` |
| BR-MET-1165 | **A NUMBER A REVIEW SHOWS IS TAKEN ON THE PRESS, NEVER RESERVED BY THE PREVIEW — AND WHAT THE PREVIEW SHOWS MUST BE WHAT THE ALLOCATOR WILL DO.** ⚠ **THIS RULE HAD NO HOME IN THE REPOSITORY UNTIL 2026-09-09.** It is stated in the *Premises & Units Blueprint* and is shared by `B7` (the premise number on the property review) and `E6` (the unit numbers on the add-units review), and both waves would have had to invent it separately. Two halves, and each is load-bearing on its own: **(a) NOTHING IS RESERVED BY LOOKING.** The allocation happens inside the create's own transaction — the PO's own words for it are *"just before updating teh record in the database"* — so **two clerks reviewing at the same moment are both shown `U-00421`**, and the second one to press gets `U-00424`. Reserving on preview would burn a number every time somebody opened a wizard and closed it, and would need a release path nobody has designed. The screen must therefore say the numbers are what the series will issue NEXT, and must never say they are held. **(b) THE PREVIEW MUST SIMULATE THE ALLOCATOR, NOT RESTATE ITS IDEA.** ⚠ **THE FIRST IMPLEMENTATION GOT THIS WRONG AND ONLY A BROWSER CAUGHT IT (`DEF-MET-4719`).** It always caught the series counter up past the highest number on file, reasoning correctly from `DEF-577` that the counter can stand behind the table. But `_allocate_unused_number` catches up **only on a collision**: it allocates, asks whether that exact value is already in the column, and moves the counter only when the answer is yes. On the `brazosbend` demo database the `unit` series read `next_number = 1` with `U-00420` on file and `U-00001` free — so **the review printed `U-00421` to `U-00423` and the three units created were `U-00001` to `U-00003`.** Every test was green and the endpoint agreed with itself; what disagreed was the product. A preview of an allocator is only true if it walks the allocator's own loop through the allocator's own arithmetic. | *Premises & Units Blueprint* (`B7`/`PUJ-08`, `E6`/`PUJ-26`); PO ruling on allocation timing, recorded on `CR-046 D1`; written into this register 2026-09-09 by the CR-089 wave E lane | CONFIRMED 2026-09-09 | `06-backend/apps/metering/services.py::preview_unit_numbers` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr089_e_unit_number_preview.py` (the collision path and the no-collision path, both proved against a counter moved under the test) · **and the one that actually decides it**: `10-scripts/cr089_wave_e_add_units_walkthrough.mjs`, which reads the numbers off the RENDERED review, creates three units for real, and compares them with the `unit_no` values the server hands back — walked 2026-09-09, `U-00004` to `U-00006` shown and `U-00004` to `U-00006` allocated |
| BR-MET-1166 | **A BASE CHARGE OF `0.00` IS NOT "FREE", AND THE ROW SAYS WHAT THE SERVICE IS PRICED ON INSTEAD.** ⚠ **THIS RULE HAD NO HOME IN THE REPOSITORY UNTIL 2026-09-09.** It is the first of the three the *Premises & Units Blueprint* holds and the register did not, written out in the decision log of 2026-09-08 so it would survive its document, and given a home here by the `CR-089` wave H lane. **THE OBSERVATION.** On the live plan screen for `SCW-U-001`, three of six services showed a base charge of **`$0.00`** — Trash, Solar and **Fire** — and nothing on the screen said whether that meant the service costs nothing or is priced some other way. **All three are priced some other way, and correctly:** trash per cart, solar by capacity, and fire by the size of the line multiplied by the count (`CR-063`). So a fire customer's `$0.00` base is *right* and reads as *broken*. **THE RULE, verbatim from the decision log:** *"a screen showing a base charge of zero must say what the service is priced on instead, on the same row. Not in a footnote, not in a tooltip. The figure and its explanation are one fact."* **WHAT MAY BE SAID AND WHERE IT COMES FROM, because this is the half a lane gets wrong.** The axis is `apps/tenancy/service_size_classes`' ratified law and is resolved on the SERVER, never keyed on the service code in a client: `capacity_uom_for_service` names the unit a service SUMS in, and `apps.rating.resolution.service_prices_by_size_class` answers whether the BAND picks the rate. Three shapes come out — **capacity** (solar, battery, storm water, broadband — the billable quantity is the sum of what is installed, and the sentence names the unit), **size bands** (water, electric, gas, trash, fire — the band picks a rate and the COUNT multiplies it), **no size axis** (sewer, ruled to have none, `BR-RAT-015`) — plus a fourth answer that is not a shape: **not ruled** (thermal steam's bands were never put to the PO, `OQ-560`), which the screen states as the open question it is. ⚠ **NO SERVICE IS NAMED IN THE IMPLEMENTATION AND NONE MAY BE**: a per-service table of prose in a client is wrong for a tenant who prices trash by weight, and silently empty for a twelfth service. ⚠ **AND IT CARRIES NO FIGURE.** The cell says what decides the charge; it never computes one. this is the same departure `formatMonthYear` and `formatMonthDay` already make, for the same reason — a display shape is a property of the place a date is drawn. `DateCell.formatNamedDate` is the one implementation and `namedMonth` is how a screen asks for it. ⚠ **A NATIVE DATE INPUT CANNOT BE MADE TO OBEY THIS AND NO API EXISTS TO ASK IT TO**, so a screen that draws one puts the named-month form beside it; | BR-MET-1170 | **A UNIT'S `City limits` IS ITS BUILDING'S, ALWAYS — THERE IS NO UNIT-LEVEL VALUE AND NONE MAY BE ADDED (PO ruling 2026-09-10).** PO verbatim: *"City limits - on Unit will be inherited from Premises only - please note"*. ⚠ **THIS CONFIRMS THE CODE RATHER THAN CHANGING IT, AND IT IS WRITTEN DOWN ANYWAY BECAUSE OF WHAT SITS NEXT TO IT.** `inside_city_limits` is on `premises` and nowhere else — migration `0013_br_rat_101_inside_city_limits` declares `model_name="premises"`, the `Unit` model has no such field, no unit screen renders one, and no unit frame in the blueprint draws one. Checked, not assumed. ⚠ **THE DOOR THIS CLOSES IS `BR-MET-1162`'s.** That ruling let a unit carry **its own `lat`/`lng`** — a cabin or a pad being somewhere its site office is not — and a lane reasoning by analogy could conclude that any address-shaped fact may now be refined per unit. It may not. `BR-MET-1162` is a **coordinate** exception and says so; `BR-MET-1161`'s address half stands exactly as signed, and city limits is an ADDRESS fact, not a map fact: a flat cannot be outside the city its building is inside. ⚠ **AND IT DECIDES MONEY, WHICH IS WHY THE ABSENCE IS THE FEATURE.** `BR-RAT-101`/`BR-RAT-102` multiply an address's bills by the outside-city surcharge. A unit-level override would let two flats in one building price differently on a fact that cannot differ between them, and — worse — would let a clerk answer it per unit, where `DEF-507`'s lesson already applies: `null` is *unrecorded* and `Outside` is an ANSWER that charges a customer. One place to ask, one place to be wrong, one place to correct. ⚠ **WHAT A UNIT SCREEN MAY DO:** SHOW the building's answer, read-only, saying where it comes from — the same shape `BR-MET-118` takes for services since the PO's ruling of the same day. **What it may not do is offer a control.** | PO 2026-09-10 · confirms `Premises.inside_city_limits` (metering `0013`) · narrows nothing, and explicitly does NOT extend `BR-MET-1162` · `BR-MET-1161` (an address lives on premises) · `BR-RAT-101`/`BR-RAT-102` (the surcharge) · `BR-MET-118` (the same shape for services) · `DEF-507` (null is not an answer) | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-10)** | no test needed for an absent column; the guard is `G85`, whose `HOME_FIELDS` allow list is FIELD-scoped and admits `lat`/`lng` on a unit while refusing an address field by name |
| BR-MET-1171 | **A READING'S ACCOUNT IS THE ONE STAMPED AT COLLECTION, AND THAT IS WHAT A SCREEN SHOWS AND LINKS TO — NEVER THE UNIT'S CURRENT OCCUPANT.** PO ruling 2026-09-14, verbatim: *"if you ahve account or meter in read details screen - please ensure you ahve link it back to account and mter to imporve interaction design"*. ⚠ **THE COLUMN EXISTED, WAS WRITTEN, AND WAS READ BY NOBODY.** `meter_reads.account_id` is a reviewed cross-module FK (B2 §2) and `services.collection_account_id` has stamped it since `DEF-658` (2026-08-24); `read_dict` served 41 keys and none of them was the account, so the reading detail printed *"The account is not served on a reading; open it through the unit"* over the top of a stored answer — *"a stored column and a printed absence for the same fact is the two-homes problem waiting to happen"* (*Meter Reads Blueprint* `B30`). **THE RULE HAS TWO HALVES AND THE SECOND IS THE ONE THAT BITES.** **(a)** The account is SERVED, by its NUMBER, and the number is the link (`B14` rule 31 — the uuid rides on `title` as evidence and is never the visible value). **(b)** It is resolved FROM THE STAMP ON THE ROW and never by walking to the unit. A reading belongs to whoever was on supply the day it was taken, so resolving today's occupant hands a year of the previous tenant's consumption to whoever moves in next — the exact hazard the stamp exists to prevent, reintroduced at the display layer. ⚠ **THE UNIT WALK IS ONE LINE AND PASSES EVERY SINGLE-OCCUPANT FIXTURE**, which is why the guard makes it IMPOSSIBLE rather than asserting a value: all three unit-scoped seams are monkeypatched to raise. ⚠ **AND THE TWO ABSENCES ARE DIFFERENT FACTS** — `not_stamped` (a vacancy read, a unit nobody has taken service at, any row committed before `DEF-658`) against `unresolved` (customers could not answer). One sentence over both would tell a clerk *"no account"* about a reading that has one, which is `DEF-075`'s shape. ⚠ **SERVED ON THE DETAIL, NOT IN `read_dict`**: every other caller of that serializer is a LIST, and one cross-module read per row is the N+1 the batched seam exists to remove (`NFR-3`, the DEF-324 discipline). | PO 2026-09-14 · *Meter Reads Blueprint* entry 03 `B30` / `MR-39` · `DEF-658` (the stamp) · B2 §2 (the reviewed FK) · `B14` rule 31 · CLAUDE.md hard rule 8 (nothing composes an address client-side) · `DEF-075` (an absence is not an answer) | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-14)** | `06-backend/apps/metering/selectors.py::read_account` · `06-backend/apps/metering/seams.py::account_references` → `apps.customers.selectors.account_references` (G20 resolves it) · `06-backend/tests/test_b30_read_serves_its_account.py` — 8 cases, and the one that decides it is `test_b30__it_reads_the_STAMP_and_never_the_unit_s_current_occupant`, proved RED against a unit-walking implementation · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ReadDetailPage.test.tsx` (the link, the uuid on `title`, and both absences) |
| BR-MET-1172 | **A STORED RULE-OUTCOME BAG IS NEVER RENDERED KEY BY KEY. A SCREEN STATES EACH RULE'S VERDICT IN ITS OWN CATALOGUE SENTENCE, BUILT FROM KEYS IT NAMES.** `DEF-MET-5204`, and it was a live `B14` rule 31 breach visible in a screenshot: the reading detail printed `Object.entries(outcome.detail)` as a `<dl>` with the RAW PAYLOAD KEY as each label, so **2 of 2 readings opened in a browser** rendered `basis` · *"no active account on the unit (BR-MET-036)"* to a clerk. **IT WAS ONE SEVERITY AWAY FROM BEING WORSE:** five stored `basis` strings carry an id (`BR-MET-036`, `OQ-009`, `CR-054`, `BR-MET-122`, `BR-MET-049`) and four sit on a severity that renders; `unit_mismatch`'s ERROR bag carries `plan_id`, a raw **uuid**; every unratified verdict carries `rule_ref`. **THE RULE:** a surface showing a rule outcome renders (i) the rule's NAME from `metering.reads.status.*` and (ii) ONE prose line composed from keys the code READS BY NAME — never by iterating the bag. A rule the surface has not been taught renders its name and nothing else. ⚠ **THE GUARANTEE IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN CAREFUL:** a key nobody has named cannot reach the DOM however the engine grows, which is what makes this a rule and not a copy pass. ⚠ **AND THE FIX HAS A DATA HALF AND A SCREEN HALF, EACH INSUFFICIENT ALONE.** The SCREEN half corrects every row ALREADY COMMITTED — the old strings are stored on those rows forever and no server edit can reach them. The DATA half strips the ids from all five `basis` strings anyway, because `rule_outcomes` is served verbatim in the payload and the next surface to render it must not inherit the breach; `B14` rule 31 says where the provenance goes — *"it is not deleted, it MOVES to the code comment beside the string"* — and that is where each id now is. ⚠ **THE REASON A RULE COULD NOT RUN IS NOT ON SCREEN AT ALL** (`B14` rule 33 — rationale is ZERO words, removed rather than shortened). It was also the widest leak: only 8 of the 29 reason codes the engine can store have a catalogue entry and the renderer fell back to the CODE, so a clerk read `maximum_register_not_priced_by_schedule_uom` verbatim. The rules that could not run are still NAMED; why is in the code and the register. | `DEF-MET-5204` (`D3`/`D4`/`D5`) · *Meter Reads Blueprint* entry 03 `B33`/`B34` · `B14` rule 31 (ratified PO 2026-08-31: *"no Databse ID on screen - that is a rule"*) · `B14` rule 32 clause 4 · `B14` rule 33 (rationale = zero words) · PO 2026-09-14 (*"reduce the content to 5 to 6 meaningful words"*) | **CONFIRMED (2026-09-14)** | `07-frontend/src/features/metering/readsShared.tsx::outcomeSentence` · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ReadDetailPage.tsx::OutcomeCard` · `06-backend/apps/metering/validation.py` (the five `basis` strings) · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/ReadDetailPage.test.tsx` — `DEF-MET-5204 / B14 rule 31: NOTHING from a stored bag reaches the clerk unless it was named`, which reproduces the ENGINE'S OWN payload (`basis` with its rule id, `plan_id` with its uuid, `resolutions`) and refuses every id family by regex; proved RED with the `<dl>` restored |

## BR-MET-1173 — the City's keys are their OWN columns, never the import match key (2026-09-17)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1173 | **A PREMISES CARRIES THE CITY'S PARCEL ID IN `gis_parcel_id` AND A UNIT CARRIES THE CITY'S ADDRESS-POINT ID IN `gis_point_id`. NEITHER IS EVER WRITTEN TO `external_ref`.** Both tables also carry `gis_status` (what the City calls the record, **stored as given and never filtered**), `gis_verified` and `gis_verified_at` (the last time the City confirmed this row). ⚠ **THE REASON IS A COLLISION THAT WOULD HAVE LANDED ON A REAL MIGRATING UTILITY.** `premises.external_ref` is `BR-MET-403`'s **import match key** — the id the utility's previous software printed on every work order — and `unit.external_ref` is `BR-MET-404`'s, its own comment calling it *"the LEGACY id"*. Both carry a case-insensitive unique-per-tenant partial index (`uq_premises_tenant_external_ref_ci`, `uq_units_tenant_external_ref_ci`). **A utility that migrated already has values in those columns**, so writing the City's parcel key into the same column would be refused by the unique index on the first run and would reach the clerk as *the parcel is already on another property* about a property with no parcel at all. *What our old system called this* and *what the City calls this* are two facts and take two columns. ⚠ **AND A GIS KEY IS NOT AN ADDRESS.** `G85` enforces hard rule 8 by matching column NAMES against an address-shaped list; none of these five names is on it, so the gate is silent about them **by construction rather than by permission** — see `BR-MET-1174`. A parcel id identifies a record in somebody else's register; it says nothing about where anything is, and nothing derives an address from it. | PO ruling 2026-09-17 (ten rulings, decision log of that date); the collision found by reading `apps/integrations/seams/metering.py` against `apps/metering/models.py` | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_1173_gis_keys.py` |

⚠ **WHAT A REPLACED KEY LEAVES BEHIND, AND WHY IT IS NOT OPTIONAL.** When a key
is REPLACED — the City split, merged, annexed or renumbered a parcel
(`BR-INT-073`, ruling 7) — **what it was and when it changed are kept**. A wrong
key means faithfully copying the wrong parcel's data forever, quietly, and the
history is the only way anybody finds out why six months later. It is kept on
the row itself (`gis_parcel_id_was`, `gis_key_changed_at`) rather than only in
`audit_log`, because `audit_log` is pruned on a retention schedule and this is a
fact about the row's identity rather than an action somebody took.

## BR-MET-1174 — G85 is silent about the GIS columns by construction, and that silence is counted (2026-09-17)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1174 | **`G85`'s `HOME_FIELDS` IS NOT WIDENED FOR THE GIS COLUMNS, AND `G85` PRINTS HOW MANY OF THEM IT SAW AND DELIBERATELY DID NOT TREAT AS AN ADDRESS.** `G85` matches by column NAME against `ADDRESS_SHAPED`. `gis_parcel_id`, `gis_point_id`, `gis_status`, `gis_verified` and `gis_verified_at` are not on that list, so the gate never collects them and never fails — **which is the right answer and the dangerous kind of right answer.** `DEF-CUS-5400` is this exact shape: the gate reported a clean zero over three `*_as_given` columns it could not see, and *a declared blind spot with no NUMBER attached is indistinguishable from an empty one* (hard-won law 7, shape 5). So the gate now COUNTS the `gis_*` columns it walked past and prints the count beside its verdict. ⚠ **`HOME_FIELDS` IS DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED.** Adding `gis_*` names to `Unit`'s entry would have been the false-pass shape the dict was built to prevent: the entry exists to permit `lat`/`lng` on a unit **and nothing else** (`BR-MET-1162`), and a lane that widens it for a parcel id has opened it for the next lane that wants a unit to print its own street. A parcel id needs no permission from an address gate, because it is not an address. | Built judgment, 2026-09-17, recorded rather than put to the PO (hard-won law 13 — this is ours to decide); `DEF-CUS-5400`'s lesson applied forward | **CONFIRMED** | `10-scripts/gates/g85_one_address_home.py` `run()` · its `selftest()` |
