## BR-MET-1176 — the Add Premises form asks who the owner is, and the identity is passed through rather than stored (2026-09-19)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1176 | **THE ADD PREMISES FORM COLLECTS THE OWNER'S KIND AND ONE IDENTIFIER, AND NO OWNER ACCOUNT EXISTS UNTIL THEY ARE SUPPLIED.** ⛔ **THIS CLOSES `OQ-MET-12472`, WHERE TWO SIGNED RULINGS MET AND ONLY ONE COULD HOLD.** *Every address gets its owner a Master Account* (PO 2026-08-31, `CR-056`) against *"Without identifier we cannot create Customer ID or master account ot regular account"* (PO 2026-09-01, `BR-CUS-1024`). Four options were put to the PO in plain English; **they chose the one that keeps the September rule**, against the option as worded — *"The Add Premises form must collect an identifier — it gains the same kind + identifier fields the import already has, and no account exists until they're supplied"* — **"this is correct"** (PO 2026-09-19). **SO THE SEPTEMBER RULE STANDS AND THE AUGUST RULE YIELDS ON THIS PATH.** `CR-056`'s sentence is not deleted: it is true of every address whose owner somebody has identified, and an unidentified owner is now a REPORTED state rather than a silence. **THE SIX FIELDS ARE THE IMPORT'S OWN SIX VIRTUAL COLUMNS, SPELLED IDENTICALLY** — `owner_kind`, `owner_ssn`, `owner_driving_licence`, `owner_phone`, `owner_tax_id` beside the `owner_account_type` `DEF-MET-5844` added — which is what lets **ONE function judge both doors**: `import_runs.owner_account_verdict` is CALLED by the form and not copied into it. A second copy would eventually tell one clerk that a name plus a phone earns an account and another that it does not, and on this path both answers reach the SAME person, one on the review step and one on the receipt. **THE WRONG KIND'S IDENTIFIER IS UNREACHABLE RATHER THAN REFUSED LATE** (`BR-CUS-1022`): no identifier box is drawn until a kind is picked, then only that kind's boxes exist, and the payload sends only that kind's value — so a company is never offered a phone, which would file a business under a telephone number where the company matcher never looks. The server still refuses it by name, for the seam and for every caller that is not the form. **WHAT DOES NOT CHANGE, AND EACH IS A SIGNED RULE THIS DOES NOT TOUCH:** the property SAVES when the owner cells are blank (PO 2026-08-27 — an address is a real address whether or not anybody can be billed for it) and the clerk is told before they press Create; no party is invented from the free-text box (`BR-MET-151` clause 3); the one door is `premises_owners.ensure_owner_master_account` (B1 — customers owns accounts); and the savepoint stays, because `create_premises` is one transaction and PostgreSQL poisons it (CLAUDE.md law 14). ⚠ **THE IDENTITY IS PASSED THROUGH AND IS NOT STORED ON THE OWNER, AND THAT WAS THIS LANE'S CALL RATHER THAN THE PO'S.** `premises_owners` gains no kind and no identifier column: identity has one home and it is `customers`, which is the module with the field encryption and the four blind indexes a government number needs, and `BR-MET-1020` already rules the metering→customer shortcut out by name. **The cost it would have bought is smaller than it looks and was measured:** `ensure_owner_master_account` returns the account it already made BEFORE it reads the payload, so the identity is asked **once per OWNER, not once per ADDRESS** — an owner's second building needs nothing typed and the response says `owner_account_already_linked`. Whether `premises_owners` should nevertheless carry it, so `PRM-04` can settle a party away from a property form, is `OQ-MET-12482` with its cost written out. ⚠ **AND ONE BLOCKAGE HAD NOTHING TO DO WITH IDENTITY (`DEF-MET-5847`):** the door refused every owner picked from the list with `customers.contact.name_required`, because `create_master_account` falls back to the premises' free-text `owner_manager` and `CR-089` `p008` split that box from the picker. The owner row's own `contact_name`, `email` and `phone` are forwarded now, as `{full_name, email, phone, is_primary}` — `{"name": …}` is refused as an unknown field. | PO ruling 2026-09-19 (decision log) · CLOSES `OQ-MET-12472` · keeps `BR-CUS-1024` · narrows `BR-MET-1010`'s *"whether or not"* at its own sentence · `BR-CUS-1022` · `BR-MET-151` clause 3 · `BR-MET-1030` (the import's identical five verdicts) · `OQ-790` (the account number, verified not rebuilt) | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence)** · **SUPERSEDED FOR ADD A PREMISE 2026-10-09 by BR-MET-1195** (`CR-177-BRC10`: the screen links an existing master account and opens none; New Connection and the import are unchanged) | `06-backend/tests/test_def_met_5844_add_premises_owner_account.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/AddPremisesWizard.test.tsx` · `10-scripts/def_met_5844_owner_account_walkthrough.mjs` (a browser, real PostgreSQL — asserts the account, the declared link and the number's SERIES, never a warning string alone) |

## BR-MET-1177 — one address block on the Add Premises step, and the lookup is an aid inside it (2026-09-19)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1177 | **THE *WHERE IS IT?* SECTION DRAWS ONE HEADING, ONE ADDRESS BOX AND ONE POSTCODE BOX, AND THE CITY LOOKUP IS A CONVENIENCE LAID OVER THEM RATHER THAN A SECOND SET.** **PO ruling 2026-09-19, shown the confirmed reproduction: "yes one is optional".** **WHAT A CLERK MET:** two headings — *Where is it?* then *Where is this property?* — two address boxes, and two postcode boxes, one of them *Zip (optional)* directly above the form's own *ZIP / postal code*, with nothing saying which counted. The lookup panel rendered its own heading and its own three fields inside a section that already had a heading and four fields. **THE BLUEPRINT DECIDES THE SHAPE AND THIS IS NOT A JUDGMENT CALL:** `05-prototype/Premises & Units Blueprint 2.pdf` p9 (S16·PRM-07, frame P2) draws `Street address *` with the help line *"Start typing and we will look it up."*, then `Town / city` · `State` · `ZIP`, then the *Found on the city map* panel — one of each, no Unit box, no second heading — and `PUJ-03`'s own acceptance sentence puts the lookup's refusal *"in a grey line under the box"*, singular. CLAUDE.md's blueprint ruling of 2026-09-14 gives a module with a blueprint its layout, copy, flows and states. **SO THE PANEL IS HEADLESS AND FIELDLESS**: it takes the form's own `Street address` and `ZIP` as props and renders only the City's ANSWER — the refusal line, the offline banner, the candidates and the count. The help line under `Street address` was already *"Start typing. We look it up."*; it is now literally true and used to describe a box somewhere else. **THE LOOKUP MUST NEVER BECOME A PREREQUISITE:** `Street address` is the only required answer on this step, and a utility whose lookup is switched off, or whose property is not in it, has exactly the path it has always had — which is what the panel's `absent` state already guarantees and what the walk proves with nothing intercepted. ⚠ **IT WAS INVISIBLE ON EVERY DEMO TENANT AND THAT IS WHY IT SURVIVED REVIEW.** `brazosbend` has no City connection, so the panel collapses to `absent` and draws nothing; **the defect exists only where the lookup WORKS**, which is the one state no walkthrough had ever been run in. ⚠ **AND THE TEST GUARDING IT ASSERTED THE DEFECT** — *"draws Unit and Zip, which the frame does and the first build did not"*, a true statement about frame 66.1 read in isolation: the panel was checked against its own frame and never against the section it renders into. The assertions are inverted rather than deleted, so a lane that puts a box back meets a red test naming the ruling. | PO ruling 2026-09-19 (`DEF-MET-5845`) · `05-prototype/Premises & Units Blueprint 2.pdf` p9 + `PUJ-03` · CLAUDE.md blueprint ruling 2026-09-14 (a blueprint owns layout, copy, flows and states) · B14 rule 36 · supersedes frame 66.1's three fields where they duplicate the form's own | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence)** · **AMENDED 2026-10-09 by BR-MET-1191** (`CR-177-BRC10`: no lookup over the address box; *Check address* asks the City once, and the Map section appears only after a match) | `07-frontend/src/features/metering/FindTheAddress.dom.test.tsx` · `10-scripts/def_met_5844_owner_account_walkthrough.mjs` (walks the step with the City lookup ON **and** OFF, and screenshots both) |

## BR-MET-1178 — a build prices ITSELF; the application it came from is provenance, never a dependency (2026-09-21)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1178 | **A NEW CONNECT BUILD RAISES ITS OWN CHARGES, AND `service_request_id` IS PROVENANCE ONLY — CARRIED, SHOWN AS *WHERE THIS CAME FROM*, NEVER REQUIRED AND NEVER A DEPENDENCY.** **PO ruling 2026-09-21, verbatim:** *"we are keeping application process and new connect process seperate - applciation is submitted - reviewed and approved or rejected - once that is doen application life is completed. then approve applciation may create a new conenct request - as draft request - this request is new work and recognized seperately from applcaition and hence we should not make it dependent on applciation #"*. ⚠ **WHAT THIS SUPERSEDES IS A SENTENCE THAT WAS LIVE IN CODE AND ON A SCREEN, NOT A NUMBERED RULE** — and it is written out here because a supersession nobody can find is not one. `apps/metering/connections._post_connection_quote` opened with *"This build has no service request, so there is no quote to raise. **A quote arrives with an approved application; a build a developer starts without one is priced by nobody and charges nothing.**"*, and step 6 of the wizard refused with *"Charges are raised from an application."* **Both are SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21.** **MEASURED WHILE THEY STOOD: not one of the nine connections in the demo tenant carried a quote total, and the wizard sent `service_request_id: null` on every commit — so NO build made through this screen could EVER raise a charge**, and the payer, the payment terms and the posting date that step 6 collects were read by nothing. **HOW A BUILD PRICES ITSELF, AND IT IS THE SHAPE THE DRAWING ALREADY DREW.** Accounts blueprint p094, in red: *"Every quantity is counted from the services grid, not typed. Six units, twenty service points, one premise — change a tick on step 4 and these move with it."* So a line is a **CHARGE CODE the clerk picks out of the tenant's own catalogue** plus a **BASIS** (`per_unit` · `per_meter` · `per_premise`), and the QUANTITY is counted from step 4. ⚠ **WHAT CROSSES THE WIRE IS A COUNT, NEVER A FIGURE** — unchanged, and it is `CLAUDE.md` hard rule 2 with `BR-BIL-007`/`BR-BIL-020`: the client sends `(charge_code, quantity)`, billing reads the price off the tenant's charge row and multiplies, and nothing is rounded before finalization. ⚠ **A BUILD WITH A REQUEST STILL TAKES THE REQUEST'S QUOTE, AND THAT IS NOT A DEPENDENCY.** The ruling removes a REQUIREMENT, not a source: where an approved application priced the work, those lines are what the customer was shown, `BR-CUS-090`'s waivers apply to them and `_refuse_quote_price_drift` still refuses a catalogue that has moved. The finance-proved posting of 2026-09-16 (`BL-2609-000082`, `DR 1200 / CR 4600 + 4630`) is untouched. ⚠ **THE PRICE-DRIFT REFUSAL HAS NOTHING TO COMPARE ON A BUILD'S OWN LINE AND IS NOT RUN** — it exists because a request's fee line stores the `unit_amount` a customer was QUOTED; a build's own line stores no price at all, so the catalogue IS the price and there are not two figures to disagree. `OQ-BIL-12376` stays open and is about the request path. ⚠ **NOTHING IS CHARGED TWICE, AND THE GUARANTEE IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN A STAMP.** The request path stamps `service_request_fees` through `seams.mark_request_quote_invoiced`; a build's own lines have no such row, and they do not need one — `commit_connection` refuses any connection that is not `DRAFT` with `metering.connection_already_committed`, so a second commit cannot happen at all. The bill it raised is found again through `connections.quote_invoice_id`. ⚠ **NO TOTAL IS SHOWN BEFORE THE PRESS, AND THE ABSENCE IS STATED RATHER THAN FILLED.** `billing.fee_bill.price_quote_bill` prices counted lines and takes an `account_id`; a draft build has no account until step 8 writes one (`BR-MET-112`), so the screen shows the CODE, the BASIS and the QTY and no money. That is `DEF-BIL-5486` and it is a missing door, not a missing screen. The drawn `$3,030.00` is an illustration (`B14` rule 37). ⚠ **NO DEPOSIT.** A deposit is a liability at `2300`; `BR-RAT-020`/`BR-RAT-023` put its amount on the plan's deposit rules; `OQ-BIL-12381` is open. Connection fees and work-order fees only. | PO ruling 2026-09-21 (decision log, same day, verbatim) · Accounts blueprint p094 read AS AN IMAGE (`B14` rule 42) · supersedes the two SENTENCES quoted above, both dated · `BR-MET-112` · `BR-MET-113` · `BR-BIL-007` / `BR-BIL-020` / `BR-BIL-024` · `BR-CUS-090` · `CLAUDE.md` hard rule 2 · `DEF-BIL-5486` (the priced preview) · `OQ-BIL-12376`, `OQ-BIL-12381` (both OPEN, neither resolved here) | **CONFIRMED (the PO's own sentence)** | `06-backend/tests/test_po20260921_a_build_prices_itself.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/NewConnectWizard.test.tsx` |
| BR-MET-1179 | **A COMMON AREA IS OPTIONAL ON EVERY PREMISE TYPE, AND IS NEVER PRE-SELECTED. NO TYPE FORBIDS ONE.** **PO ruling 2026-09-21, verbatim:** *"build this rule - no common area for single family or related classes"* — and, the same day, correcting how that was first written down: *"i said Comemrcial Building also Common area is optional not mandetory , I did not say No common area"*. **WHAT THE RULE IS: a DEFAULT, not a prohibition.** New Connect's step 3 opened with *Include a common-area unit* **already ticked**, so a clerk building a house — who has no unit numbers to type — left the boxes empty, carried on, and committed **one common area and no dwelling at all**, with nothing on screen saying that was wrong. **Built as:** `emptyDraft()` sets `includeCommonArea: false`, the tick is offered on every premise type, and the wizard refuses a build with no DWELLING (*"Add at least one unit."*) rather than counting a shared unit as one. ⛔ **NOTHING IS REFUSED FOR HAVING A COMMON AREA, AND A LANE MUST NOT BUILD ONE.** A four-code refusal — `single_family`, `mobile_home_park`, `commercial_building`, `government_building` — stood in `metering/services.py` for part of one afternoon and was removed by the PO's correction above. **It came from a question rather than from an answer:** the PO was asked *"which types should have no common area at all?"* and offered ticks; four ticks were recorded as a prohibition they never uttered. ⚠ **THE LESSON IS WORTH MORE THAN THE RULE: when a ruling will become a REFUSAL in code, put the refusal back to the PO in those words** — *"the product will refuse a commercial building that has a common area"* — rather than offering a list to tick. A refusal is a strong thing to build from a checkbox. ⚠ **AND DO NOT REASON ABOUT COMMON AREAS FROM TYPE NAMES.** Measured on the seeded demo the day this was minted: **79 properties — 67 single-family, 8 commercial, 3 apartment complexes, 1 government — and EXACTLY ONE carries a common area.** It is `500 Main St`, a `commercial_building` with 31 units, and **all three apartment complexes have none.** The type everyone assumes needs one has none in our own data. That intuition is what produced the wrong question. ⚠ **`premises_types` IS A PER-TENANT LOOKUP LIST (`BR-MET-034`)**, so the six above are `brazosbend`'s and the going-live customer will have their own. Nothing in this rule reads a type at all, which is what makes it safe for a list nobody has seen. | **PO 2026-09-21** (ruled, then corrected the same day) · decision log 2026-09-21 *"THREE RULINGS ON THE NEW CONNECT WIZARD"* §3 and *"CORRECTION — A COMMON AREA IS OPTIONAL, NOT FORBIDDEN"* · BR-MET-101 · BR-MET-034 · BR-MET-112 | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-21** | `07-frontend/src/features/customers/NewConnectWizard.test.tsx` · `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_040_common_area.py` |

## BR-MET-1180 — an approved application raises a DRAFT build; the property is still written at the last button (2026-09-21)

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1180 | **AN APPROVED APPLICATION RAISES A DRAFT BUILD ON EITHER SHAPE OF ADDRESS, AND THE PROPERTY IS STILL WRITTEN AT THE LAST BUTTON.** **PO ruling 2026-09-21, verbatim:** *"then approve applciation may create a new conenct request - **as draft request** - this request is new work and recognized seperately from applcaition and hence we should not make it dependent on applciation #"*. **TWO SHAPES, ONE ANSWER.** Where the application's address **resolved** to a premises, the draft is raised AGAINST that property and the wizard has no address to create. Where it **did not**, the draft is raised with **`premises_id` NULL** and the address is created at the wizard's last button, exactly as it is for a build a clerk starts directly. ⛔ **THE SECOND HALF IS `BR-MET-112` AND IS NOT NEGOTIABLE:** creating the property at approval would leave it standing the moment a clerk abandons the wizard — with no units, no meters and no plan — and `commit_new_connect`'s own docstring records that **no screen in this product can find that wreckage**. **SCHEMA:** `metering/0059` drops `connections.premises_id`'s `NOT NULL` and adds `ck_connections_premises_once_submitted` — *a build that has been SUBMITTED has an address*. ⚠ **KEYED ON `submitted_at`, NEVER ON `state`**: the state column is a cache `refresh_connection_state()` rewrites and a cancel outranks, so a constraint on it could be defeated by cancelling. Every row that existed when this landed satisfies it and nothing was backfilled. **WHAT THE APPROVAL WRITES:** one `connections` row — the `CON-` number, `state=draft`, `service_request_id`, `target_activation`, and the premises only where one already resolved — plus one `connection.created` audit row, and nothing else. **WHAT THE COMMIT WRITES:** the property (where there is none), the units, the meters, the plans, the owner's account, the work orders and the charges, in ONE transaction through `commit_connection`. ⚠ **THE COMMIT FINISHES THAT DRAFT AND DOES NOT CREATE A SECOND BUILD BESIDE IT** — `commit_new_connect` takes `connection_id`; a commit that ignored it would leave an abandoned draft on the New connects tab for ever while every other check passed. ⛔ **`request_id` STAYS PROVENANCE (`BR-MET-1178`)**: `/connections/new` with no build sends `connection_id: null` and behaves exactly as before. ⚠ **AND THE VERDICT'S `premise_exists` REFUSAL IS WITHDRAWN — THE DRAWING SETTLED IT.** `service_request._verdict_for` returned `not_possible` with *"There is already a premise record at this address"*, greying the option on every matched address; **Accounts blueprint p089, read AS AN IMAGE at scale 2.4 (`B14` rule 42), draws `A new connection` LIVE on `SR-26-0001`, whose address matched `PR-0731 · exact`**, over the callout *"Nothing is preselected, deliberately. The resolver matched this address to PR-0731 exactly, and that is EVIDENCE — not the decision."* An existing property with no service is an ordinary new connection. It is `possible` and **not** `fits_the_evidence`: no screen draws that badge for a matched address, and awarding one would author the rule `OQ-356` exists to ask for. ⚠ **THE APPLICATION'S ELEVEN ANSWERS ARE OFFERED AND NEVER DECIDED WITH** — `GET /connections/{id}` serves them as `prefill`; the owner is still REQUIRED at step 1, and the unit PATTERN is deliberately not prefilled from `units_on_property`, because turning a count into labels invents names that reach `uq_units_tenant_premises_label` and every bill after it. | **PO ruling 2026-09-21** (decision log, same day, verbatim) · Accounts blueprint **p088–p089** read AS IMAGES (`B14` rule 42) · `BR-MET-112` · `BR-MET-1024` · `BR-MET-1178` · `BR-CUS-070` · `BR-CUS-080` · `CLAUDE.md` hard rule 8 · `DEF-CUS-6109` · `DEF-MET-6117` (the false reason, corrected in the same commit) | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-21** | `06-backend/tests/test_def_cus_6109_an_application_becomes_a_build.py` · `06-backend/tests/test_cr094_approval_raises_the_job.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/customers/ApplicationBecomesABuild.dom.test.tsx` · `10-scripts/cr126_s9_request_to_record_walk.mjs` |
| BR-MET-1181 | **A PREMISES NAMES ITS MASTER ACCOUNT DIRECTLY, AND THE OWNER RECORD IS OUT OF THE CHAIN A SCREEN WALKS (PO ruling 2026-09-22; `DEF-MET-6139` high).** **PO verbatim:** *"why you are point is at owner id - why not directly at master accoutn Id - it will solev the problem … master Account do not have any Owner ID - please confirm ; we are not using it - owner IDs are not required hence no screens or fucntionality for it"*, and on the three consequences put back to them: *"yes - im okay with this"*. ⚠ **IT IS NOT A NEW RULING. IT IS `OQ-790`'s OWN MECHANISM, NAMED BY THE SAME PERSON ON 2026-08-27 AND NOT BUILT FOR TWENTY-SIX DAYS:** *"you ahev to call master account creation logic by passing the details and generate account number using Setting -> numbering and use this number to write premsies"*. **What stopped it was code, not a missing decision:** `_ensure_owner_master_account` read `row.owner_id` FIRST and returned `no_typed_owner` before it ever looked at the identity payload — so a clerk typed a name, a kind and a tax id and all three were discarded because they had not also picked a row from `premises_owners`, **a table no screen in this product can write to** (`createPremisesOwner` is defined in `endpoints.ts` and `metering/api.py` and is called by nothing). The gate could not be satisfied from the product at all. **MEASURED BEFORE A LINE WAS CHANGED**, on real PostgreSQL, a clone of the `brazosbend` beta, 2026-09-22: a property saved with `Harriet Vane`, kind `person`, an SSN and account type `residential` left master accounts at **1 → 1**, no account on the address, and one warning. The same payload for a company with a Tax ID answered identically. **0 of 79 properties** on that database had an owner account the money path could find. **(1) THE COLUMN.** `premises.master_account_id`, UUID NULL, FK → `customers.Account`, PROTECT. Nullable and **nothing is backfilled**: a row written before the ruling declares nothing here, which is a real state and not a gap. **(2) THE PRECEDENCE, RULED RATHER THAN LEFT TO A FUNCTION'S READING ORDER**, because three columns can answer *who is the master account for this address* and three answers that disagree is who gets billed wrongly: **1.** `premises.master_account_id` — the declaration, wins whenever set; **2.** `premises_owners.master_account_id` — the PRE-RULING declaration, for rows that predate this, still written beside the new column wherever a typed owner exists; **3.** `accounts.premises_id` — *which premises this account covers*, **never** *who owns this premises*, and **never promoted to answer this one** (both master AND regular accounts carry it, so promoting it would make every tenant at an address a candidate owner of it). **(3) `BR-MET-400`'s *DECLARED, NEVER INFERRED* SURVIVES AND IS NARROWED, NOT WEAKENED.** Both columns the precedence reads are DECLARATIONS; this is a SHORTER CHAIN between the same two ends, never a guess. The hallway derivation stays retired for that rule's own reason — *"a fallback that disagrees with the declared owner would silently bill a different party"*. **(4) WHERE IT IS IMPLEMENTED, AND WHY IT IS CHEAP.** The whole money path joins through two seam functions in `apps/metering/premises_owners.py` — `owner_master_account_for_premises` and `premises_ids_owned_by_account` — with **four call sites in the entire backend** (`customers/selectors.py:884`, `:917`, `:1056`, `customers/move_out_settlement.py:1470`, counted at the tip). Changing what those two read carries the empty-unit base charge, the move-out shortfall, the sub-account list and the account header **with no consumer touched**. **(5) THE FORM OPENS OR ATTACHES THE ACCOUNT, AND NOTHING NEW IS COLLECTED.** A name, a kind, one identifier of that kind and an account type were already on the Add Premises form and already validated (`BR-MET-1176`); what is removed is the gate in front of them. An account is created through the one existing door — `ensure_master_account_for_premises` → `customers.create_master_account` through the seam — so `BR-CUS-017`'s family, `BR-CUS-1011`'s one-owner-account-per-address and the customer resolution all travel with it. `master_account_id` on the payload ATTACHES an account that already exists (`BR-MET-400`: *"it can be existing master account also"*), on **`POST /premises` and `PATCH /premises/{id}` both** — the PATCH door is the reporter's own *"we are unabel to find and map master account to premises later"*. A named account that is not `kind='master'` is **refused by name**, which is precedence 3 refused at the door. **(6) EVERY OUTCOME REACHES THE SCREEN.** The five verdicts each carry their own `message_key` and exactly one wording of it (`refused` carries the refusing door's own key, never a second wording). Before this, `create_premises` computed a distinct answer every time and **a whole-front-end grep found zero occurrences of any of them outside the import receipt** — the wizard fired one toast and threw the array away. **(7) WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE.** No owner-record screens are built — `createPremisesOwner` stays uncalled and that is now a **DELIBERATE ABSENCE**, recorded so no lane "fixes" it by wiring a screen the PO has said they do not want. `premises_owners` is **NOT deleted**: 19 backend files read it, `BR-MET-151`'s free-text import matching and the move-out shortfall stand on it, and retiring it is separate work. `owner_manager` is never dropped. `BR-MET-112` is not weakened — nothing is written until the last button; what changed is what that one press writes. `BR-MET-151` clause 3 is not crossed — **no party record is created by this path at all**, so no category is read off any name; the name is used as a NAME, which is the string `create_master_account` has always fallen back to out of `owner_manager`. ⚠ **WHAT THIS COSTS A CLERK, STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED:** the identity ask used to be ONCE PER OWNER (the owner record carried the link, so a second address for one party reused it) and is now once per ADDRESS for a party with no owner record. Whether a second address should find the first one's account by name is `OQ-MET-12573`, OPEN — and nothing guesses at it, because matching a party by a typed string is what `BR-MET-151` clause 3 forbids. | PO ruling 2026-09-22 (decision log, *A PREMISES NAMES ITS MASTER ACCOUNT DIRECTLY*) · `OQ-790` (CLOSED 2026-08-27, option (c)) · `DEF-MET-6139` · `CR-130-BRC07` §9 · BR-MET-400 (narrowed in its chain) · BR-MET-1010 · BR-MET-1176 · BR-CUS-1010 · BR-CUS-1011 · BR-MET-112 · BR-MET-151 | **CONFIRMED** | `06-backend/tests/test_br_met_1181_premises_master_account.py` · `10-scripts/br_met_1181_premises_master_account_readback.py` (real PostgreSQL) |

## BR-MET-1182 — New Connect is five steps, the premise step owns scope and the record, and the books entry is written in the same act (2026-09-28)

**PO rulings of 2026-09-28, on `CR-150-BRC09`.** Premises, units, occupancy and what the last button
writes are metering's subject, so the half of that day's rulings that governs them lives here. The
customer half is `BR-CUS-1102`–`1104`; the whole set, in the PO's own words, is in
`12-governance/02-decision-log.md` under *THE NEW CONNECT REDESIGN — THE PRODUCT OWNER'S EIGHTEEN
ANSWERS*.

**what is removed is rationale text (B14 rule 33), which is not information the clerk acts on.** **Parked drafts reopen on their mapped step — 1→1, 2→2, 3/4/5→3, 6→4, 7/8→5 — and old `?step=` links redirect**, so no clerk loses a keystroke; | BR-MET-1183 | **SCOPE IS ASKED ON THE PREMISE STEP, AS A DROPDOWN UNDER THE ADDRESS SEARCH, AND AN ALREADY-SERVED **UNIT** BLOCKS THE BUILD WHILE AN ALREADY-SERVED **BUILDING** DOES NOT.** PO verbatim: *"these options are correct but this decisions can not made until premise is searched and should be dropdown"*. The three choices — a new premise · new units at an existing premise · a new service at an existing unit — are unchanged; the dropdown is **pre-filled from what the address search found and stays changeable.** ⚠ **THE REFUSAL FIRES ONLY WHEN THE EXACT UNIT ALREADY HAS THAT SERVICE.** PO verbatim, in two parts: *"if premises is found - then it cannot be new connect request; it will be move in request in such case"*, then *"Just display the error and show the message; next step will be blocked"*, then, asked which case triggers it, **option (a)**. **A served building with unserved units is STILL A NEW CONNECT and all three scope choices keep working on it.** The clerk is shown the error, **Continue is blocked, nothing routes them anywhere automatically and nothing is carried over.** ⚠ **READ THE TWO HALVES TOGETHER**: the first sentence alone would refuse every unserved unit in a served building, which is the ordinary case at an apartment complex. | PO rulings 2026-09-28 (decision log, answers 2 and 3) · `CR-150-BRC09` · `DEF-CUS-7602` · `BR-MET-112` | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-28** | ⛔ **OWED.** Both sides: an exact-unit-and-service match refuses and blocks Continue; a served building with an unserved unit advances on all three scope choices. |

| BR-MET-1185 | **THE UNITS STEP IS ONE COMMON SELECTION AND THEN ONE TABLE, GROUPED BY UNIT, WITH A REQUIRED *APPLY TO ALL* AND ROWS THAT STAY EDITABLE — AND OCCUPANCY LIVES ON THE UNIT ROWS.** PO verbatim: *"grouped by units"*; on a row after the bulk action, *"this is correct"* (it stays editable); on occupancy, *"why dot you keep this with units itself"*; and on the two buttons, *"you ahve to show screens what wil happen when user press Add row and add work order - screens are required"*. **Columns, ruled and short: Unit · Kind · Service · Asset · Size · Qty · Work order · Completes · Occupancy.** **The unit is named once at the head of its group.** **`Apply to all` is REQUIRED before Continue and every row stays editable afterwards** — both halves matter: required, because a clerk could otherwise reach the next step with most rows unfilled; editable, because three units may take a 1" meter and the fifteenth a 2". **Occupancy carries its four real choices** — *Vacant, service off* · *Vacant, base charges only* · *Landlord-billed between tenants* · *Open an account now* — set once in the common selection and changeable per row, **and the Review step SHOWS it without ASKING it**. **`Add row` and `Add work order` add IN PLACE at the bottom of the group, never in a dialog**; a work order belonging to the build rather than a service point becomes its own row with Service, Asset and Size reading *—* and an *Applies to* of whole build, one unit or one service point; **only PUBLISHED templates are offered.** ⚠ **`Qty` IS DRAWN AND NOT BUILT** — `OQ-CUS-12770`, OPEN: which quantities were 1, 2 and 15 is money on a real invoice and nothing may guess it. ⚠ **THE TABLE IS THE SHARED COMPONENT, NOT A BESPOKE GRID** (B14 rule 43): units, service points, work orders, charges and messages all grow through one component. | PO rulings 2026-09-28 (decision log, answers 13 and 14) · `CR-150-BRC09` · `DEF-CUS-7605`, `DEF-CUS-7606`, `DEF-CUS-7607`, `DEF-CUS-7608`, `DEF-CUS-7613` · `OQ-CUS-12770` (OPEN) · B14 rule 43 · `05-prototype/new-connect-redesign-2026-09-28/step3-units.png`, `step3a-add-row.png`, `step3b-add-work-order.png` | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-28** | ⛔ **OWED.** The negative case is the one to write: advance without pressing *Apply to all* and assert the named refusal. | BR-MET-1187 | ⚠ **THE ONE-TIME CHARGES REACH THE BOOKS IN THE SAME ACT AS THE CONNECTION, AND THE PAYMENT WITH THEM — IF THE BOOKS ENTRY CANNOT BE WRITTEN, THE CONNECTION IS NOT CREATED.** PO verbatim: *"we create entries in books"* and *"do it at creation once submitted"*, over the sentence shown to them and accepted: *"It is one act: if the books entry cannot be written, the connection is not created either."* **Pressing Create writes the connection, an INVOICE on the owner's account for the one-time charges due after the payment terms, and the PAYMENT if one was taken.** ⚠ **WHAT THIS SUPERSEDES, QUOTED FROM THE CODE BECAUSE IT IS A MONEY PATH:** in `06-backend/apps/metering/connections.py`, when `_post_connection_quote` cannot post, `commit_connection` takes `quote_total` **from the client**, saves it, sets **`one_time_charge_invoice_no` to `None`**, appends an `absence` — **and the commit carries on.** So a build can be created today with its money remembered as a figure on the build and **no invoice anywhere**: nothing reaches Billing, nothing reaches Finance, nobody is ever asked for it. On a build the size of the drawn example that is **`$1,035.00` that exists on one screen and in no ledger.** Five refusal codes reach that branch — `build_names_no_charge`, `quote_has_no_chargeable_line`, the payer's own code, `quote_payment_terms_not_captured`, `billing_quote_door_unpublished`. ⚠ **A BUILD THAT NAMES NO CHARGE HAS NOTHING TO WRITE AND IS UNAFFECTED** — the rule bites where there IS an entry to write and it fails; stating that boundary is part of the ruling, because *"the connection is not created"* read without it would refuse every build that raises no fee. ⚠ **`BR-MET-112` IS UNCHANGED AND IS WHY THIS IS BOTH-OR-NEITHER:** nothing is written until the last button, and a build that rolled back must not leave a receivable behind it. ⚠ **`BR-MET-1178` IS UNCHANGED:** the client sends `(charge_code, quantity)`, billing reads the price and multiplies, and no money is multiplied up in the client (hard rule 2). ⚠ **`BR-BIL-024` IS UNCHANGED: payment terms carry NO DEFAULT** — a guessed due date starts a late-fee clock on a real invoice. ✅ **BUILT 2026-09-28 by lane MONEY** — `CR-150-BRC09` tasks `B1` and `B2`; the suite runs on sqlite, which does not) · `06-backend/apps/metering/connections.py::_post_connection_quote` | **CONFIRMED 2026-09-28 — supersedes the commit-anyway behaviour quoted above** | ✅ **BUILT 2026-09-28 — lane MONEY, `CR-150-BRC09` tasks `B1` and `B2`.** `commit_connection` step 6 now REFUSES when a chargeable quote cannot be posted (`_refuse_a_books_entry_that_cannot_be_written`, `message_key` `metering.connection_books_entry_required`), and the two absences that mean *there is nothing to write* — `build_names_no_charge` and `quote_has_no_chargeable_line` — are named in `QUOTE_ABSENCES_WITH_NOTHING_TO_WRITE` with the ruling's own boundary argued beside them. **The client's `quote_total` is now written NOWHERE** (`DEF-MET-7641`, hard rule 2); | BR-MET-1189 | **TWO READINGS OF ONE REGISTER ON ONE DATE ARE BOTH KEPT, AND THE ONE THAT COUNTS IS THE HIGHEST OF THOSE THAT ARE APPROVED AND VALIDATED (PO 2026-10-04, `DEF-WRK-9642`).** PO verbatim: *"take the reading records in register and use date and highest consumption reading if it is apprved and validated"*. **(1) STORED, NEVER REFUSED, NEVER OVERWRITTEN.** A second reading for the same meter, register and date — an office reading and a field job's, an event wizard's and an upload's — is written beside the first. The `duplicate` outcome stays on the row as a FACT (severity OK, `kept: second_reading_same_day`) so a reader can see the sibling, and it sets no status and blocks nothing. `uq_meter_reads_head` is dropped (`metering/0074`); the same key stays indexed. **The SAME value again** (same meter, register, date and value) is not a second reading but the one already on file: the event door (wizards, Work Accept) answers it `already_recorded` and stores nothing; a file upload keeps BR-MET-016's *already on file* row error. **(2) THE ONE THAT COUNTS** for billing, consumption and usage, estimation history, the prior a later reading is measured from, the export pair and the sub-meter reconciliation is the HIGHEST register value among the readings on that register and date that are **approved** and **validated**. ONE implementation: `apps/metering/counted_reading.py`, reached through `selectors._read_heads()` and `services._head_qs()`. **(3) WHAT THE TWO WORDS MEAN — read off this repo's reading lifecycle, not invented.** **Approved** = in the register as a committed lineage head nobody has objected to: `lifecycle` `committed` or `billed` (`BR-MET-053`; a reading gets there only by a person — the uploader's certification at commit, `US-MET-010`, or the office pressing Accept on a field job in *Awaiting approval*, `BR-MET-110`), `superseded_by_id` empty, and no open dispute (`BR-MET-024`). **Validated** = its one primary status (`BR-MET-021`) is not one of `missing`, `negative_usage`, `mismatch`, `unknown_meter`, `duplicate`, `tou_sum_sanity` (billing's own `UNBILLABLE_READ_STATUSES`); a WARNED reading is validated (`BR-MET-129`, `BR-MET-022`). **(4) THE WORKED FIGURE, as put to the PO.** Meter E-5000: office **3,400 kWh** on 4 October, technician R. Patel's move-in WO-0043 **3,412 kWh** the same day; last reading 3,300 kWh on 4 September. Both kept; both approved and validated, so **3,412 counts** and the bill measures **112 kWh**, never 100. If the 3,412 were disputed or failed validation, the 3,400 would count (100 kWh). The next reading, 3,500 kWh on 5 November, is measured from 3,412 — **88 kWh**. **(5) WHEN NONE OF THE DAY'S READINGS IS APPROVED AND VALIDATED** nothing is removed and every consumer decides exactly as before this rule. **(6) THE METER'S READING LIST SHOWS BOTH** and marks them: *Counts for billing* on the one that counts, *Kept as history* on the other; a reading alone on its date shows neither. **(7) A CORRECTION IS STILL A CORRECTION:** `override` and the `insert & update` write mode still supersede (they now close the counted reading); `BR-MET-016`'s intra-file and same-cycle-same-value clauses are untouched. | PO 2026-10-04 (decision log, same day) · supersedes `BR-MET-016` / `BR-MET-052` in part · `BR-MET-021/022/024/053/110/129` · `US-MET-010` | **CONFIRMED** (the rule is the PO's sentence; the meaning of *approved* and *validated* in clause 3 is our reading of the code, recorded in the decision log 2026-10-04) · 🔒 **HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** (billing-input path) | `06-backend/tests/test_def_wrk_9642_same_day_readings.py` (11 cases, red first; green on SQLite and PostgreSQL 16) |

## BR-MET-1190–1195 — Add a premise, redesigned (2026-10-09)

**PO rulings of 2026-10-09** (decision log *"PO GO — BUILD THE ADD-A-PREMISE REDESIGN"* and *"PO RULINGS — ADD-A-PREMISE REDESIGN, CONTINUED"*; change request `CR-177-BRC10`; drawing `05-prototype/add-premise-2026-10-09/`). Six new rules and two amended ones; the plan the PO approved numbered them 1–8, kept here in brackets.

| ID | Rule | Source | Status | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-MET-1190 | **(1) A PREMISE'S ADDRESS IS UNIQUE WITHIN THE UTILITY; AN EXACT MATCH WARNS AND THE CLERK MUST CHOOSE *OPEN IT* OR *ADD IT ANYWAY*.** PO verbatim: *"when address is added, we need to check for uniquness, exact same address if existing in system then you should promp user that eact same address is available"*. **Exact** = the same street, city, state and ZIP, ignoring capitals, spaces and punctuation (`101 silver creek rd.` = `101 Silver Creek Rd`; `7 Oak Rd` ≠ `7 Oak Road`). Checked when the clerk completes the address and again on Continue and on save; the prompt names the other premise by its NUMBER and address (never an id, B14 rule 31) with *Open it* and *Add it anyway* — **no reason is asked** — and Continue waits for one of them. The save refuses an exact duplicate (*"This address already exists."*) unless the request carries `add_anyway: true`. Applies to a person's Add a premise form (and the City's door for it); the import and New Connection have their own duplicate handling. | PO 2026-10-09 | **CONFIRMED** (PO GO 2026-10-09) | `06-backend/tests/test_cr177_add_premise_redesign.py` · `07-frontend/src/features/metering/addPremise/AddPremisePage.dom.test.tsx` |
| BR-MET-1191 | **(2) THE ADDRESS IS CHECKED AGAINST THE CITY MAP ONLY WHEN THE CLERK PRESSES *CHECK ADDRESS*; WITHOUT A CITY MAP CONNECTION IT SAVES AS TYPED, MARKED CANNOT BE CHECKED.** PO verbatim: *"below this address - we need a button to check address validdity - uisng gis api or city map … if integration is done for that tenant - then address matches then it will shwo the detials otherwise it will show that this integration not available and adderss cannjot be validated"*. **No search while typing** (`DEF-MET-10034`). One request, three answers: a match (the City's address, parcel and map point), no match, or *City map not connected*; a City map that exists and cannot answer says the City's own reason. | PO 2026-10-09 | **CONFIRMED** (PO GO 2026-10-09) | as above |
| BR-MET-1192 | **(3) *USE THESE DETAILS* REPLACES THE TYPED ADDRESS WITH THE CITY'S AND RECORDS THE PARCEL, THE POINT AND THE CHECKED DATE.** The address stays on the premise alone (CLAUDE.md hard rule 8; the point is `premises.lat/lng`). The City is asked again at the save, and what it says then is what is written (`C16`); *Keep my address* saves the typed address, unchecked. | PO 2026-10-09 | **CONFIRMED** (PO GO 2026-10-09) | as above |
| BR-MET-1193 | **(4) ONLY THE UTILITY'S ACTIVE SERVICES ARE OFFERED, IN ONE DROPDOWN WITH *ALL SERVICES* AT THE TOP, AND NONE IS TICKED BY DEFAULT.** PO verbatim: *"Services - also multi seelct drop down - active services for that utility"*; *none ticked* was plan rule 4, approved as written with the GO. ⚠ **SUPERSEDES, FOR THIS SCREEN, `CR-036` (h)'s default** that opened the control with every enabled service on the address. `BR-MET-003` (services ⊆ enabled) is unchanged. | PO 2026-10-09 | **CONFIRMED** (PO GO 2026-10-09) | as above |
| BR-MET-1194 | **(5) REQUIRED CUSTOM FIELDS MUST BE FILLED BEFORE A PREMISE IS SAVED.** Continue refuses, opens the closed *Custom fields* section and marks the field; the save refuses it too (`customFields.required` on `custom_data.<key>`, the validator `BR-TEN-052` already applies on the screen a field is typed in). | PO 2026-10-09 · `DEF-MET-10034` | **CONFIRMED** (PO GO 2026-10-09) | as above |
| BR-MET-1195 | **(8) A PREMISE'S OWNER IS AN EXISTING MASTER ACCOUNT FOUND BY SEARCH AND LINKED; THE ADD-PREMISE SCREEN CREATES NO ACCOUNT.** PO verbatim: *"in owner - allow to search amster account from master account list. if it is found then shwo the details, if it is not foudn then show this account not available; anyway woner is options - and we shoudl allow to move forward"*, then *"instead of chnge - it should be link or use this. you should nothave these field - Account type … Person or company … Managed by - it will come as part of account seach"*. Search by name or account number; a hit shows account type, person or company, managed by and status read-only with *Link this account*, then *✓ Linked* and *Unlink*; a miss reads *This account is not available.* and the clerk carries on — the owner stays optional. The six payload keys that opened an owner account from this screen are refused by name from it. ⚠ **SUPERSEDES `BR-MET-1176` FOR ADD A PREMISE**: a new owner account is made on Accounts (or by New Connection / the import, unchanged). | PO 2026-10-09 | **CONFIRMED** (PO GO 2026-10-09) | as above |

**The two AMENDED rules, as the plan numbered them (6) and (7):**

- **BR-MET-031** (its row above is stamped the same commit) — **(6) AMENDED 2026-10-09 — THE ADD-PREMISE FLOW STARTS AT PROPERTY; BULK ADDS GO THROUGH IMPORT ONLY.** PO verbatim: *"our mistake please remove how many step … there is seperate improt fucntion for premsies bulk import so step 1 how many not required"*. Add a premise is **Property · Units · Review**; the CSV paths live on the five-stage Import runner (`DEF-520`) and the pattern generator on Add units (`CR-089` wave E). The rule's two bulk paths are unchanged — only the door moved.
- **BR-MET-1177** (its row above is stamped the same commit) — **(7) AMENDED 2026-10-09 — MAP FIELDS SHOW ONLY FOR A UTILITY WITH A CITY MAP CONNECTION, AND ONLY AFTER A MATCH.** The lookup laid over the address box is gone (`BR-MET-1191`); the *Map* section (parcel, point, *Checked against GIS*) appears on Add a premise only once *Check address* has matched, which a utility without a City map can never reach.
- **BR-MET-1202** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-22 (PO) — WHERE A READING OR PHOTO WAS TAKEN IS ITS OWN FACT, NOT AN ADDRESS.** PO verbatim: *"yes we have capture gps location again on phone because it might be taken later , and location gps and gps set in system may differ"*. A field reading, a route photo and a field-added-meter proposal keep the phone's `lat`/`lng` at capture as evidence of where the technician stood. It never replaces or corrects the premises' or unit's point (`BR-MET-1161`, `BR-MET-1162`), the two may differ, and it carries NO address field. Fourth narrowing of hard rule 8's coordinate clause; `G85` permits `lat`/`lng` only on these classes. Decision log 2026-10-22 *"TWO PO RULINGS"* (1).

**MQ-033 — utility-defined meter-read FORMATS (`CR-192-BRC10`; decision log 2026-10-24, the three rows headed "MQ-033").** PO, verbatim: *"B. Reading templates, like work templates - we understand your recommendation , but utility users want customizations they wantt to collect various data during meter reads hence we need to create meter read format templates so that the customization can be handled"* · *"3. Settings → Reads; The template is chosen automatically: route → cycle → service. A route can override its cycle, and a cycle its service. we can set it once or we can create another format for same service and replace it later"* · *"this is all user defined we dont have to provide anything readymade , this will be similar to custom fields"* · *"this is correct"* (to the summary below). Built server-side by lane MQ033-SERVER (task 10 of 13), `apps/metering/read_formats.py`.

- **BR-MET-1210** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — A UTILITY BUILDS ITS OWN READ FORMATS IN SETTINGS → READS, AND NOTHING IS READY-MADE.** A format belongs to one service. No sample format, question or default is seeded; a route that resolves to no format reads exactly as before. Our recommendation (one rule set per service) was declined.
- **BR-MET-1211** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — A QUESTION IS DEFINED LIKE A CUSTOM FIELD, PLUS PHOTO.** The same data types, list options and required flag as `tenancy.CustomField` (read from tenancy's constant, never copied), plus `photo`. A question's key is its identity in every answer.
- **BR-MET-1212** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — THE FORMAT IS CHOSEN ROUTE → CYCLE → SERVICE → NONE, AND REPLACING IT MOVES A POINTER.** A route's format overrides its cycle's, a cycle's its service default; a level that names a format of another service is passed over. Only a PUBLISHED format can be pointed at. A format still pointed at cannot be retired (our judgment: *"replace it later"* is moving the pointer first).
- **BR-MET-1213** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — FORMATS ARE VERSIONED; A PUBLISHED VERSION NEVER CHANGES.** Editing a published format opens a new draft; publishing it gives the next number and moves every LATER reading; a reading already taken keeps the version it was asked under (`BR-WRK-008`'s reason).
- **BR-MET-1214** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — ASKED WHEN, AND FOLLOW-UPS.** Each question is asked at *every reading* · *out of range* · *can't read* · *once per stop*; a follow-up is shown only when an EARLIER question's answer matches (this is how an answer *requires a photo*). Our readings: *every reading* means a reading with a figure; *out of range* is the engine's HIGH/LOW/ZERO verdict (or the reader's accepted-anyway); *once per stop* is satisfied by any reading at the same stop, route and day.
- **BR-MET-1215** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — ANSWERS TRAVEL INSIDE THE READING'S OWN ITEM AND ARE STAMPED WITH THE VERSION.** Batches of 50 unchanged; the server checks each answer against the same version.
- **BR-MET-1216** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — AN ANSWER NEVER CAUSES A READING TO BE DROPPED: STORED AND FLAGGED.** A wrong type, an option not on the list, an unknown question, a question not asked or not shown, a missing required answer, an unknown version — each is stored as received with its reason and the reading is marked for review; the reading is judged exactly as it would have been without answers (`CR-168-BRC10` §F's hard rule).
- **BR-MET-1217** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — AN ANSWER CAN RAISE A WORK ORDER OF A UTILITY-CHOSEN TYPE, LINKED BACK TO THE READING.** One job per reading and question, raised through Work's route-finding raise path (`BR-WRK-067`), as a finding of kind *read answer*; a raise Work refuses leaves the finding open for a person. Our reading: the *type* is a published Work job template.
- **BR-MET-1218** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-24 (PO) — ANSWERS ARE SHOWN ON THE READING, FILTERABLE IN READS LISTS, AND EXPORTED.** Shown with each question's words as the stamped version worded them. Filter `answer[<key>]=<value>` built in task 10; list columns and export are task 13.
- **BR-MET-1219** — **CONFIRMED 2026-10-31 (PO, FB-022) — A FIELD READING LOWER THAN THE METER'S LAST IS ACCEPTED, NOT REFUSED, AND WAITS FOR A CLERK BEFORE IT BILLS.** PO verbatim: *"if the current reading is less than previous reading it shows warning to technician , but do not refuse the reading accept"*, and R1 A · R2 A the same day. **(1)** The reads door posts it with BR-MET-052's `negative_usage` flag and NO consumption (never invented); any OTHER engine error still refuses the reading. Supersedes, for the field door only, the 2026-10-21 "back to the technician" refusal of a lower reading (`BR-WRK-063` as superseded); an office file keeps BR-MET-052. **(2)** Until a clerk corrects it, billing skips it — the latest approved reading in the period closes the bill (5,010 / 5,140 / 4,790-flagged → **5,140**), and none at all is the missing-reading case (4,812 → 4,790 → not billed). **(3)** A later reading is never measured from it (4,812 → 4,790 → 4,900 = **88**, not 110). **(4)** Several readings of one meter in one cycle on different dates are all stored and the latest approved bills (5,010 / 5,140 / 5,155 → **5,155**); BR-MET-1189 still settles a same-date pair. **(5)** A reading the pipeline refuses takes a photo added to it and a late position for it with it, each refused by name — never a 500 (`DEF-MET-10796`). 🔒 **HUMAN-REVIEW-PENDING** (billing input). Tests: `06-backend/tests/test_fb022_refused_reading_with_photo.py` (+ `_pg`).
